- **Hour 2026-06-07 01:03 KST:** read paper 026 — *Fully Automated Tooth Segmentation and Labeling for Both Full- and Partial-Arch Intraoral Scans Using Deep Learning* (Cao, van Nistelrooij, Vinayahalingam, Radboudumc, Int Dent J 2025). Key insight: **strongest H5 evidence in the reading list** — the +0.197 macro-F1 jump on partial-arch from artificial-partial-arch data augmentation (Model 1 → Model 2) is bigger than any H5 transfer result we've seen; the four cheap tricks (artificial-partials + Stratified-Transformer alignment + FDI-pair-offset MAP postprocessor + real partials) beat every prior SoTA on 3DTeethSeg (Score 0.9870, vs paper 025's 0.94 Dice on imperfect arches). The FDI-aware postprocessor is **a classical dynamic-programming MAP over a multivariate-Gaussian prior on FDI-to-FDI offsets** — a beautiful hybrid AI pattern (deep learning for per-tooth prediction, classical combinatorial inference for global consistency) and a new form of H3 conditioning for the segmentation sub-task. Concrete action: (a) **adopt artificial-partial-arch augmentation as v0 sub-task 1's primary training trick** (1-day preprocessing, OBB-based crop, 90% probability, skewed distribution favoring fewer teeth), expected +0.10-0.20 F1; (b) **adopt FDI-pair-offset postprocessor as v0 final stage** (50 lines of NumPy, $0 compute, +0.005-0.01 macro-IoU free); (c) **defer DL-based alignment module to v1** (Stratified Transformer is overkill for v0, use OBB+curvature-cue heuristic from paper 025); (d) **use 3DTeethSeg 1200/600 split as v0 eval protocol**, target Score ≥ 0.95 v0 / ≥ 0.98 v1; (e) **add a "prepared tooth" augmentation** (random surface trim 10% probability) to handle the highest-error clinical condition Cao identified. Note in `papers/026-cao25-automated-tooth-seg.md`. **Open question for HK: v0 base model — Cao's ToothInstanceNet (newest, best ablation, no code), paper 024 Kunwar (code + Blender postprocessing released), or paper 025 ArchSeg (no code, requires reimplementation)?** Recommendation: pilot all three on 3DTeethSeg 1200/600, 1-week $100 Lambda. **Next paper: TSegNet (Rekik 2022, 3DTeethSeg challenge baseline) for the original 2022 comparison point, or the ToothInstanceNet/DentalNet/Stratified-Transformer trio for the building blocks of Cao's pipeline.**

## Scholar weekly digest

- **Week of 2026-06-05:** read paper 001 — *3DTeethSeg'22: 3D Teeth Scan Segmentation and Labeling Challenge* (Ben-Hamadou et al., MICCAI 2022 challenge). Key insight: largest public IOS dataset (1,800 scans / 900 patients / 23,999 FDI-labeled teeth) is now downloadable, and every winning method is 2-stage (centroid detection → per-tooth segmentation) — strong empirical support for **H1**. IGIP team's dental-arch-curve post-processing (Bezier fit through centroids used to correct FDI labels) is a direct analogue of the inductive bias we need for **H3** (conditioning on global arch context for outer-surface generation). Concrete action: download the dataset this week, use it as the public-data backbone for sub-task 1. Note in `papers/001-3dteethseg22.md`.
- **Week of 2026-06-06:** read paper 002 — *DeepSDF: Learning Continuous Signed Distance Functions for Shape Representation* (Park, Florence, Straub, Newcombe, Lovegrove, CVPR 2019). Key insight: **strong support for H4** (implicit SDF > explicit mesh). An 8-layer MLP with a per-shape latent code learned via the **auto-decoder** trick (no encoder — codes and decoder weights jointly optimized with a Gaussian prior on the codes) hits SoTA on ShapeNet reconstruction and partial-depth completion at 7.4 MB with watertight surfaces and analytic normals. The auto-decoder formulation is directly extensible to our setting: condition `f_θ(z, x)` on a context feature from adjacent + opposing teeth (H3) and infer the missing tooth's code at inference time via MAP. The L1-clamp loss, layer-4 skip connection, and code-LR-100×-decoder-LR ratio are the four tricks worth adopting verbatim. Concrete action: clone the official PyTorch repo, run the pipeline end-to-end on a toy crown dataset, and queue **DiGS** (CVPR 2022) as paper 003 — it directly fixes DeepSDF's main weaknesses (slow inference, thin-structure failure). Note in `papers/002-deepsdf.md`.
- **Week of 2026-06-06 (continued):** read paper 003 — *DiGS: Divergence Guided Shape Implicit Neural Representation for Unoriented Point Clouds* (Ben-Shabat, Hewa Koneputugodage, Gould, CVPR 2022). Key insight: **strongest support yet for H4**, *and* the cleanest path to H3 we've seen. DiGS is a SIREN (sinusoidal-activation MLP, not ReLU) trained with a **soft Laplacian / divergence penalty** on the learned SDF — no normal vectors, no sign labels, no normal-estimation pre-processing needed. The geometric insight is that the gradient field of a true SDF is **incompressible** (low divergence nearly everywhere), and penalizing `|∇·∇Φ|` acts as the canonical **Dirichlet-energy smoothness prior** (Sec. 5.3 derives this formally). The architectural price is a non-negotiable `C²` activation, hence SIREN. Pairs with a **geometric initialization** that pre-shapes the field to a sphere, plus a multi-frequency variant (MFGI) that bakes in high-frequency capacity without losing the geometric structure. SoTA on SRB and ShapeNet among **unoriented** methods (Table 2: dC 0.19 vs. SIREN-wo-n's 0.42; Table 3: IoU 0.939 on 260 shapes, beating every normal-supervised method). **The crown jewel for us: the auto-decoder extension to DFAUST (Sec. 6.2) is exactly the conditional generation pipeline we want for H3** — learn one code per training tooth, MAP-infer at test time given the context teeth's SDF samples. **Critical empirical finding: DiGS w/o normals *beats* DiGS w/ normals on ShapeNet mean IoU** (0.939 vs. 0.920) because normals push the network to fit internal structure and create ghost geometry — for printable crowns, this means we should deliberately train without normals even if we had them. **Concrete action: promote DiGS to the default backbone for sub-tasks 3–4, drop DeepSDF from the plan, and queue a diffusion-on-implicit-fields paper (DiffusionSDF / LION / CIGS) as paper 004** to add the H2 (diffusion) layer on top of this DiGS + auto-decoder base. Also open question for HK: keep the inner surface (sub-task 3) as a deterministic offset rather than DiGS-generated, since clinical fit needs < 50μm margin gap and learned inner surfaces are unlikely to beat a geometric pipeline. Note in `papers/003-digs.md`.
- **Week of 2026-06-06 (paper 004):** read paper 004 — *Diffusion-SDF: Conditional Generative Modeling of Signed Distance Functions* (Chou, Bahat, Heide, ICCV 2023). Key insight: **first paper to put a diffusion model over a 1D latent that parameterizes a neural SDF**, giving us clean, multi-modal 3D shape completion conditioned on partial point clouds, 2D images, or noisy real scans. The two-stage architecture is the same pattern as LION (paper 005): **(1) train an "SDF-VAE"** (GenSDF backbone + 5-layer VAE over 3 plane features, 1D latent of dim 128) to compress thousands of SDFs into a 1D latent space, then **(2) train a 6-block DALL·E-2-style attention-based diffusion model** over those 1D latents with **cross-attention conditioning** on encoder features (PointNet for points, ResNet-18 for images). The trick that unlocks geometric detail beyond what the latent space alone can express is the **end-to-end fine-tune**: re-run each diffusion sample through the SDF decoder and add the L1 SDF loss on top of the diffusion MSE. SoTA on Acronym unconditional generation and on partial point-cloud completion across Chair/Couch/106-class (Table 1–2). **Strong support for H1** (2-stage VAE-then-DDM is correct), **strong support for H2** (diffusion on implicit fields produces clean thin structures), **mild support for H3** (cross-attention conditioning is the right mechanism, but the paper's conditioning is on single partial point clouds, not on adjacent+opposing teeth). **H4 reaffirmed**: the SDF latent is the right data substrate for the diffusion prior when clinical fit is the metric. **Critical contrast with paper 002/003 (DeepSDF/DiGS):** the auto-decoder (one code per shape) catastrophically fails on multi-class (CD 17.83 vs. Diffusion-SDF's 0.607, supplement Table 2) — 128 dims can't carry both "category" and "instance" info. The shared SDF-VAE backbone sidesteps this by baking category/pose into the weights and letting the 1D latent carry only per-instance variation. **Concrete action:** Diffusion-SDF is now the H2 × H4 candidate, paired with DiGS (paper 003) as the H4 reconstruction backbone. The LION (paper 005) point-cloud path is the H2 × H4' alternative — pilot both next. **Compute note:** Diffusion-SDF trains on A100 40GB; the SDF-VAE alone is expensive. Note in `papers/004-diffusion-sdf.md`.
- **Week of 2026-06-06 (paper 005):** read paper 005 — *LION: Latent Point Diffusion Models for 3D Shape Generation* (Zeng, Vahdat, Williams, Gojcic, Litany, Fidler, Kreis — NVIDIA Toronto / U. of Toronto, NeurIPS 2022). Key insight: **LION is the direct alternative to Diffusion-SDF (paper 004): same "VAE + latent DDM" two-stage pattern, but the latent is *point-structured* (`h0 ∈ ℝ^((3+1)×2048)`) instead of a 1D vector that parameterizes a neural SDF.** Architecture: (1) hierarchical point-cloud VAE with **PVCNN** encoder/decoder, global shape latent `z0 ∈ ℝ^128` + point-structured latent `h0`, trained with **aggressive KL annealing** (1e-7 → 0.5 linearly over 8000 epochs, β-schedule from Vahdat et al. 2021); (2) two latent DDMs — small ResNet-FC for `z0`, **conditional PVCNN DDM `ϵψ(h_t, z0, t)` for `h0` with `z0`-conditioning via AdaGN** (the same trick as Stable Diffusion's text conditioning); (3) **diffuse-denoise trick** (encode → forward-diffuse `τ` steps in prior → reverse-denoise → decode) as the inference mechanism for partial/noisy/voxelized inputs. SoTA on ShapeNet airplane/chair/car (1-NNA-CD 67.41/53.70/53.41, vs. PVD's 73.82/56.26/54.55) and 13-class ShapeNet-vol (1-NNA-CD 51.85 vs. PVD's 58.65) with no class conditioning. **The 13-class result is the cleanest empirical support for H3 we've seen: a hierarchical latent where `z0` captures global shape and `h0` captures per-tooth details is sufficient to disambiguate across diverse categories without explicit class labels.** **Strong support for H1** (2-stage architecture), **strong support for H2** (latent diffusion > direct diffusion, by 6+ 1-NNA points on the 13-class setting), **strongest architectural support for H3** (the `ϵψ(h_t, z0, t)` conditional DDM with AdaGN conditioning is *exactly* the template for conditioning on adjacent+opposing teeth, and the diffuse-denoise trick is *exactly* the inference algorithm for "partial scan → complete missing tooth"). **Mild contradiction with H4** (LION is explicit point cloud, not implicit SDF — but the H2-vs-VAE half of H2 is supported; the question is just "SDF latent vs. point cloud latent"). **Strong support for H5** (trained entirely on synthetic ShapeNet). **The big new architectural idea: diffuse-denoise with a `τ` knob gives the dentist a meaningful UX** — "τ=10: respect the prep margin strictly, low hallucination" vs. "τ=200: ignore the prep margin, generate a beautiful occlusal surface" — and the LION paper's voxel-guided synthesis (Fig. 12-13) is a direct proof-of-concept. **Concrete action: this is the H3 implementation template** (a) VAE on complete arches from 3DTeethSeg22, (b) encoder fine-tuned to map partial arches (one tooth missing) to meaningful `z0`, (c) tooth DDM conditioned on `z0` via AdaGN, (d) diffuse-denoise at inference with dentist-controlled τ. **Compute note:** 110 V100-hours for the VAE alone, 440 for the DDMs = 550 V100-hours per single-class model. ~$1,500 on Lambda for the pilot. **Decision gate for next week: pilot both LION and Diffusion-SDF (papers 004/005) on a single tooth class with ~100 training meshes** — pick the one that produces better printable meshes (chamfer to GT + visual smoothness of occlusal surface). This is the single most important architecture decision for the next quarter. Also queued: paper 006 = either "Score-Based Diffusion on Neural Fields" (skip-the-VAE path) or "Neural Dual Contouring" (clinical-grade surface reconstruction). Note in `papers/005-lion.md`.
- **Week of 2026-06-06 (paper 006):** read paper 006 — *Neural Dual Contouring* (Chen, Tagliasacchi, Funkhouser, Zhang — SFU / Google / Princeton, SIGGRAPH 2022). Key insight: **NDC is the surface-reconstruction half of the pipeline we've been building** — given the implicit field (Diffusion-SDF) or point cloud (LION) coming out of our generative model, NDC turns it into a printable mesh with **sharp cusps and smooth outer surface** in a single feed-forward pass (0.5s/shape on V100). The architecture is a 3D U-Net that predicts per-vertex offsets and per-edge existence flags for every voxel cell, supervised against the output of running Dual Contouring offline on a high-resolution grid (the "soft teacher" trick). Three variants: NDC for SDF / binary occupancy, UNDC for unsigned-distance / point clouds, NDCx (heavier backbone) for higher fidelity. The UNDC variant is critical for us because patient IOS scans come in as raw point clouds, not SDFs. **Strong support for H1** (2-stage: generator + learned mesh extractor), **refines H4** (the implicit field is the substrate, but the mesh extractor is what determines final quality — NDC and FlexiCubes are both arguments for "use a *learned* extractor, not MC"). **The big architectural idea: per-cell edge-existence flags** are predicted by the 3D U-Net, so the connectivity is *adaptive* (more edges in high-detail regions, fewer in flat regions), unlike MC which is fixed by the grid resolution. **Concrete action:** (a) adopt NDC as the default mesh-extractor for the v0 prototype, (b) run NDC on 3DTeethSeg22 as a sanity check (sharp cusps? clean margins? < 50μm intaglio smoothness?), (c) pilot NDC vs MC on the same Diffusion-SDF outputs as a 1-week experiment, (d) bookmark **SDC (CVPR 2024)** for the end-to-end fine-tuning story. **Note: paper 006 was committed in a separate batch by an earlier scholar run**; this entry documents it for completeness. Note in `papers/006-neural-dual-contouring.md`.
- **Week of 2026-06-06 (paper 007):** read paper 007 — *FlexiCubes: Flexible Isosurface Extraction for Gradient-Based Mesh Optimization* (Shen, Munkberg, Hasselgren, Yin, Wang, Chen, Gojcic, Fidler, Sharp, Gao — NVIDIA Toronto / U. of Toronto / Vector Institute, SIGGRAPH 2023). Key insight: **FlexiCubes is the *v1 mesh extractor* for our pipeline** — the direct successor to NDC (paper 006) and the **first isosurface extractor to satisfy both *Grad* (differentiable optimization) and *Flexible* (sharp features) simultaneously**, a property the paper's Table 1 taxonomy marks with a checkbox that no prior method has. Built on **Dual Marching Cubes** (Nielson 2004, an obscure but manifoldness-guaranteeing variant of Dual Contouring) with **four sets of learnable, differentiable parameters per cell**: interpolation weights `α ∈ R⁸` for edge crossings, dual-vertex weights `β ∈ R¹²` for positioning inside the primal face, quad-split weights `γ ∈ R` for triangulation, and grid-vertex deformations `δ ∈ R³` from DMTet. All parameters are passed through `tanh(·)+1` to keep them positive and bounded, and the dual vertex is computed as a *convex combination* of primal-face crossings — so gradients are well-behaved and the output vertex is guaranteed to stay in the cell. **Killer result for us: FlexiCubes is the *only* mesh extractor that survives an equilateral-triangle regularizer without geometric collapse** (Table 4: CD 5.46 with reg vs 4.87 baseline, while DMTet goes 5.17 → 6.69 and MC goes 6.33 → 8.56). This means we can add a **clinical-quality regularizer** (margin preservation, occlusal convexity, developability) without losing occlusal anatomy. **Strong support for H1** (2-stage: generator + learned extractor is the only paradigm that works), **mild contradiction with H2** (mesh-based GET3D with FlexiCubes beats DMTet by 4–5 FID, narrowing the case for point-cloud diffusion — restate H2 as "diffusion on implicit fields > mesh-based VAE"), **refines H4** (the implicit field is the substrate, the *mesh extractor* determines final quality — use DiGS as the field, FlexiCubes as the extractor), **mild support for H5** (synthetic CAD pretraining + light fine-tune on patient data, as the paper's nvdiffrec-from-synthetic-then-finetune pattern demonstrates). **Quantitative wins:** 34.87% inaccurate-normals at 64³ vs DMTet's 48.66% (Table 2); FID 17.51 vs 22.41 on ShapeNet chairs in GET3D (Table 6); CD 0.45 vs 4.51 in nvdiffrec on the NeRF-synthetic Chair scene (Table 5). **Caveats:** ~5× more memory than MC at 64³ (116 MB) — budget 64³ for v0, 96³ for v1; rare self-intersections require a trimesh / PyMeshFix post-processing pass before 3D printing; the extractor is not globally continuous (mesh jumps when isosurface slips over a grid vertex) but stochastic optimization (Adam) doesn't care. **Concrete action: adopt FlexiCubes as the default mesh-extractor for v1, replacing NDC** — the 4–5 point FID improvement and 10× CD improvement are decisive, and the drop-in pattern is well-validated in the GET3D + nvdiffrec experiments. Add a **margin-preservation regularizer** to the diffusion model's training loss (FlexiCubes is the *only* mesh extractor that can take a regularizer without geometric collapse). The **final v0 architecture** is now: Diffusion-SDF (paper 004) for the generator + DiGS (paper 003) as the implicit-field backbone + FlexiCubes (this paper) at 64³ as the mesh extractor + trimesh/PyMeshFix for self-intersection repair. Total compute budget ~$2,000 on Lambda for the v0 pilot. Note in `papers/007-flexicubes.md`.
- **Week of 2026-06-06 (paper 008):** read paper 008 — *PoinTr: Diverse Point Cloud Completion with Geometry-Aware Transformers* (Yu, Rao, Wang, Liu, Lu, Zhou — Tsinghua, ICCV 2021, arXiv:2108.08839). Key insight: **PoinTr is the v0 completion backbone for sub-task 4 (outer surface)** — the cleanest *single-pass* point cloud completion architecture in the literature, and the one whose **dynamic Query Generator is the most direct implementation of H3** (conditioning on adjacent + opposing teeth) that we've seen so far. The architecture: (1) FPS-downsample the partial point cloud to 128 center points, (2) extract DGCNN local features (channels 3→8→32→64→64→128) and add position embeddings to form "point proxies", (3) run a 6-layer transformer encoder + 8-layer decoder, (4) the Query Generator produces M=96/224 *dynamic* queries from the encoder's max-pooled global feature, (5) the decoder's predicted proxies are then upsampled via a FoldingNet head to the full point cloud. The **Geometry-Aware Transformer Block** is a one-line DGCNN-style kNN attention augmentation that injects spatial inductive bias into self-attention — and the ablation (Table 4) shows it's most useful in the *first* block only. Results: **CD-Avg 1.09 (×10³) on ShapeNet-55 vs GRNet's 1.97 (-45%)**, **F1@1% 0.464 vs 0.238 (+95%)** — the largest single improvement over a strong baseline in the table. On the ShapeNet-34 unseen-category generalization test, PoinTr degrades 67% (1.07→1.84) vs GRNet's 72% — meaningful for our patient-variability story. On KITTI (real LiDAR cars), the synthetic-trained model fine-tunes and transfers — **the cleanest H5 precedent in the reading list.** **Hypothesis connections: H3 STRONG support** (the query generator is literally the H3 conditioning mechanism — encoder sees the partial arch, decoder queries are dynamically generated from encoder output, decoder produces the missing tooth's points), **H5 STRONG support** (synthetic-only training, fine-tune to real scans), **H1 mild support** (encoder-decoder cleanly separates understanding from generation, so the front-end detector can be swapped without retraining), **H2 mild contradiction** (PoinTr is single-pass, no diffusion — we lose the multi-modal sampling that LION/Diffusion-SDF give us), **H4 mild contradiction** (PoinTr is the *most explicit* representation in our reading list — point cloud, no SDF — but it has a clean role as the completion backbone upstream of DiGS (paper 003) and FlexiCubes (paper 007)). **Concrete action: (a) adopt PoinTr as the v0 prototype for sub-task 4 outer surface**, (b) build a **synthetic 10,000-arch dataset** (Bézier arch prior + CAD-library per-tooth shapes + random masking of 1–3 teeth) and pretrain PoinTr on it as the H5 pilot, (c) fine-tune on 3DTeethSeg22 with one tooth masked per arch, (d) add a learned 16-dim FDI-number class embedding to the Query Generator's input (small extension, gives us class-aware completion), (e) downstream of PoinTr, lift the predicted point cloud to an SDF via DiGS and extract a mesh via FlexiCubes for the printability check. **Compute note: PoinTr is by far the cheapest to train in our reading list — ~$50 on Lambda for the PCN/ShapeNet-55 fine-tune** vs LION's $1,500 or Diffusion-SDF's $2,000, so this is the right v0 to ship. **Open question for HK: single deterministic completion (PoinTr) vs distribution of completions (LION/Diffusion-SDF)?** I'd lean PoinTr for the v0 prototype (fast iteration), LION for the v1 product (dentist wants options). **Next paper to read: SnowflakeNet or Seedformer (ICCV 2022) — direct successors to PoinTr with better local detail (cusps, fissures, sharp features critical for the occlusal surface).** Note in `papers/008-pointr.md`.
- **Week of 2026-06-06 (paper 009):** read paper 009 — *SnowflakeNet: Point Cloud Completion by Snowflake Point Deconvolution with Skip-Transformer* (Xiang, Wen, Liu, Cao, Wan, Zheng, Han — Tsinghua / Kuaishou Y-tech / Wayne State, ICCV 2021 **Oral**, arXiv:2108.04444; extended to TPAMI 2023). Key insight: **SnowflakeNet is the strongest v0 candidate for sub-task 4 yet** — it directly addresses the "cusps + fissures + sharp features" requirement that PoinTr (paper 008) can't, and does it with the simplest possible pipeline: a **3-layer Snowflake Point Deconvolution (SPD)** stack that grows a 512-point seed into a 16,384-point complete shape through rooted-tree splitting (r₁=1 refine, r₂=4, r₃=8), with a **skip-transformer** between layers that uses the previous layer's displacement features as kNN-restricted attention context for the current layer's splitting. The kernel interpretation is the conceptual hook: each parent's 128-dim feature is a vector of "shape characteristic activations" (learned kernels K_m) and each child point is the activation-weighted sum of kernel rows — a 1D deconvolution applied independently per point. Results: **PCN CD 7.21 (×10³) vs NSFA 8.06 (-10.5%)**, **Completion3D CD 7.60 (×10⁴) vs PMP-Net 9.23 (-17.3%)** — and on PCN it **beats PoinTr's 8.38**, which is the closest direct comparison in our reading list. Skip-transformer ablation: Full 8.48 vs Self-att 8.89 vs No-att 9.30 vs No-connect 9.39 — the cross-layer attention is the unique contribution, the in-layer self-attention variant (closer to PoinTr) loses 0.41 CD. **Hypothesis connections: H1 mild support** (internal coarse+fine 2-stage via seed-then-grow), **H2 mild contradiction** (deterministic single-pass, not diffusion — but the SPD product-of-conditionals is a clean alternative generative decomposition), **H3 STRONG support** (skip-transformer *is* the H3 conditioning mechanism, just over parent-child neighbors — extend to FDI-aligned neighbor teeth for our use case), **H4 REFINES** (implicit-SDF wins for the *field*; SPD-style local generation wins for the *surface quality* — our DiGS+FlexiCubes stack could inherit SPD's skip-transformer as a "neighborhood-conditioned SIREN update"), **H5 strong support** (synthetic-only pretraining, KITTI transfer in TPAMI extension). **Concrete action: (a) promote SnowflakeNet to v0 pilot alongside PoinTr** — both are <$50 compute on Lambda and complete in a week, (b) bump kNN-k from 8 to 16–24 for tooth-scale data (default k is too local for the occlusal surface), (c) add a 4-term multi-resolution CD loss to the v0 pipeline (the trick that makes SPD train stably), (d) inject a second attention head into the skip-transformer that conditions on adjacent + opposing teeth (the H3 extension), (e) visualize what the SPD kernels learn on tooth data — if they correspond to cusps / fossae / ridges, that's a strong clinical-interpretability win. **Compute note: ~$60 for a v0 pilot (6h synthetic + 2h fine-tune + 1h mesh extraction) — the cheapest full stack in the reading list.** **Open question for HK: deterministic SnowflakeNet vs multi-modal LION/Diffusion-SDF for the v1 product?** Recommendation: pilot both, in parallel, on the same 100-tooth subset. **Next paper to read: SeedFormer (ICCV 2022, arXiv:2207.10399) — the direct successor to SnowflakeNet with patch-based self-attention and Voxel-Set-Attention; or SVDFormer (ICCV 2023) for a 2023-era point of comparison.** Note in `papers/009-snowflakenet.md`.
- **Week of 2026-06-06 (paper 010):** read paper 010 — *SeedFormer: Patch Seeds based Point Cloud Completion with Upsample Transformer* (Zhou, Cao, Chu, Zhu, Lu, Tai, Wang — Nanjing U. + Tencent Youtu Lab, ECCV 2022, arXiv:2207.10315). Key insight: **SeedFormer is the most parameter-efficient (3.20M params, 23× smaller than GRNet) and the strongest H3-supporting completion architecture we've seen, with the cleanest architectural idea in the reading list: Patch Seeds — preserve local regional features (256 seeds × 128 channels) instead of max-pooling to one global vector, and propagate those regional features to every upsample layer via inverse-distance interpolation + a regional positional encoding `θ(s_i − s_j)` in the transformer.** Combined with the **Upsample Transformer** (each new point = self-attention-weighted average of kNN-neighbor features, with r_l separate kernels per layer — a direct cousin of SnowflakeNet's per-point 1D deconv), SeedFormer sets a new SoTA on **PCN (CD-Avg 6.74 vs SnowflakeNet 7.21, −6.5%)**, on **ShapeNet-55 (CD-Avg 0.92 vs PoinTr 1.09, −15.6%)**, and on **ShapeNet-34 unseen categories (CD-Avg 1.34 vs PoinTr 2.05, −34.6% on generalization — the strongest evidence for H5 in the reading list)**. The KITTI real-scanned transfer (Fidelity 0.151 vs GRNet 0.816, 5.4× improvement) is the cleanest synthetic→real H5 precedent. **Two architectural ideas we should adopt verbatim:** (1) **the seed-generator softmax removal** (Sec. 3.3) — softmax's [0,1] normalization biases seed points toward the *seen* region, but we need them to *spread* to the missing region; w/o softmax wins by 0.09 CD and is qualitatively decisive for unseen-region generation; (2) **the regional positional encoding** `δ = ρ(p_i − p_j) + θ(s_i − s_j)` (Eq. 6) — this is the *only* architecture in our reading list that explicitly represents *which anatomical region* a new point belongs to, and it's the cleanest implementation of H3. **Strongest H3 support yet** (Patch Seeds = per-region conditioning, regional positional encoding = per-region attention bias, two-seed-group decomposition = interpretable "preserved" vs. "inferred" trust heatmap for the dentist). **Strongest H5 support yet** (ShapeNet-34 unseen-category CD 1.34 vs PoinTr 2.05; 3.20M params generalize rather than memorize). **H1 mild support** (encoder→seed generator→upsample layers is a 2-stage architecture, and the global-feature ablation loses 0.23 CD — fine-grained evidence that "preserve local" beats "collapse to global"). **H2 no relevant evidence** (deterministic encoder-decoder, no diffusion — the right *baseline* against which to compare LION/Diffusion-SDF). **H4 no relevant evidence** (point cloud, not SDF — but the Patch Seeds output is a *structured* input to DiGS, ideal for our sub-task 4 + paper 003 + paper 007 stack). **Concrete action: (a) adopt SeedFormer as the v0 completion backbone for sub-task 4, alongside PoinTr and SnowflakeNet — all three are <$100 compute pilots on Lambda**, (b) use the **point-wise attention variant** (3.12M params, 7.87G FLOPs, 6.85 PCN CD — best compute-accuracy trade-off in our reading list) for the v0 prototype, (c) **remove softmax in the seed generator** (critical for spreading seeds to unseen regions), (d) **replace inverse-distance interpolation (Eq. 2) with an FDI-aware two-kernel variant** for inner (neighbor-driven) vs. outer (opposing-driven) surface distinction, (e) **bump kNN-k from 8 to 16–24 for tooth-scale data** (default k too local for occlusal surface), (f) downstream of SeedFormer, lift to DiGS SDF and extract a FlexiCubes mesh for the printability check, (g) ship the **two-seed-group trust heatmap** UX — color each completed point by its nearest seed, giving the dentist a free per-region confidence map. **Compute note:** point-wise attention variant trains in ~6h on a single A100; full Upsample Transformer variant in ~12h; total v0 pilot budget ~$30–$100 on Lambda — the cheapest full v0 in our reading list. **Open question for HK:** the *inverse-distance* interpolation in Eq. 2 is the wrong default for the occlusal surface (which should be dominated by opposing teeth, not by geometrically-close adjacent teeth). Test a learned FDI-aware kernel vs the default. **Next paper to read: AnchorFormer (CVPR 2023, arXiv:2303.04724) — the direct successor to SeedFormer with learned (rather than fixed) anchor positions, generalizing the Patch Seeds idea to per-instance seeds; or PMP-Net++ (TPAMI 2023) for the multi-step point-moving-paths extension; or SVDFormer (ICCV 2023) for a 2023-era point of comparison with self-view augmentation.** Note in `papers/010-seedformer.md`.
- **Week of 2026-06-06 (paper 011):** read paper 011 — *AnchorFormer: Point Cloud Completion from Discriminative Nodes* (Chen, Long, Qiu, Yao, Zhou, Luo, Mei — USTC / U. Rochester / HiDream.ai, **CVPR 2023, pp. 13581–13590**, code at github.com/chenzhik/AnchorFormer; **no arXiv preprint**). Key insight: **the direct successor to SeedFormer (paper 010), and the new SoTA on every public completion benchmark** — and the source of the **+0.133 F1 jump on ShapeNet-34 unseen categories** (0.535 vs SeedFormer 0.402, 33% relative — the largest generalization gain in our reading list and the strongest H5 evidence we've seen). The three-step innovation stack: (1) **anchor generation via dual-attention transformer blocks** (8 blocks × L=16 anchors = 128 anchors), with a clever `gi − Xᵢ` *feature-difference* signal (Sec. 3.1 Eq. 2) that biases anchors toward underrepresented patterns — the right inductive bias for "where is the missing region"; (2) **anchor scattering** via a single global MLP `ΔA = MLP(MaxPool(F))` (Sec. 3.2 Eq. 4) that *learns* to push anchors into unobserved regions, vs. SeedFormer's *interpolated* seeds that stay close to observed geometry; (3) **point morphing** (Sec. 3.3, the surgical innovation) — at each of 256 sparse points (128 anchors + 128 input), modulate a canonical 2D grid (K=64 points) with a **global α** and a **per-sparse-point local βⱼ** via `h_out = α·(h_in − µ)/σ + βⱼ` (Eq. 6, a FILM/AdaGN operator), producing 16,384 dense points. Also a novel **MST-based compactness loss** (Eq. 9–10, λ=1.2, γ=0.05) that penalizes long edges in an MST of the predicted points. **Results: PCN CDL1 6.59 (vs SeedFormer 6.74, −2.2%)**, **ShapeNet-55 F1 0.558 (vs 0.472, +0.086)**, **ShapeNet-34 unseen F1 0.535 (vs 0.402, +0.133)**, **KITTI MMD 0.458 (vs 0.526, −13%)**. Ablation (Table 5): anchors contribute −0.52 CD (largest), morphing −0.09, compactness −0.09 — the *anchor mechanism* is the heavy lifter. **Hypothesis connections: H1 mild support** (internal 2-stage: encoder predicts+scatters anchors, decoder morphs grid), **H2 no evidence** (deterministic, no diffusion — clean baseline for LION/Diffusion-SDF comparison), **H3 STRONGEST support yet** (three independent H3 mechanisms: learned per-instance anchors, learned offset scattering into unobserved regions, and global-α/local-βⱼ modulation — the cleanest H3 implementation in the reading list), **H4 no evidence** (point cloud, not SDF — but anchors are the perfect sparse input to DiGS), **H5 STRONGEST support yet** (the +0.133 F1 on unseen categories is unprecedented in our reading list; KITTI synthetic→real transfer with no fine-tune). **Three concrete actions for our project: (a) promote AnchorFormer to v0 completion backbone, replacing SeedFormer as default** — add to the 008-010 three-way pilot, same <$100 compute on Lambda, much better generalization for patient variability; **(b) adopt modulation-based point morphing (Eq. 6) as our surface refinement layer downstream of DiGS** — input DiGS samples at sparse points, learn (α, βⱼ) per sparse point, modulate a 2D grid to produce 3D offsets, sum with sparse-point coordinates; the α/βⱼ split is the cleanest implementation of "arch-level vs cusp-level" H3 conditioning; **(c) add the MST-based compactness loss (Eq. 9) as a regularizer for our FlexiCubes (paper 007) mesh output** — keeps cusps/fossae from blurring into a single convex blob, λ=1.2, γ=0.05 starting values. **Two parallel pilots to run:** (A) AnchorFormer + DiGS + FlexiCubes (deterministic v0 stack), (B) AnchorFormer-as-encoder + LION-style latent DDM with the modulation operator as conditioning (H2 × H3 intersection). **Open question for HK:** the +0.133 F1 unseen-category jump is so large that we need to know *why* before committing. Two hypotheses to test in the v0 pilot: (H-A) anchor scattering is the unique contribution (random-offset baseline should drop to SeedFormer level), (H-B) dynamic anchors are the unique contribution (fixed-seed baseline should drop to SeedFormer level). Whichever dominates should be the v1 architectural property. **Note: paper was CVPR-only with no arXiv preprint** — must cite via CVPR Open Access. **Note in `papers/011-anchorformer.md`**. **Next paper to read: PMP-Net++ (TPAMI 2023) for the multi-step point-moving-paths extension, or SVDFormer (ICCV 2023) for a self-view-augmented 2023-era point of comparison, or SVRF/Diffusion-Point-Voxel (CIGS, CVPR 2024) to start closing the H2 evidence gap.**
- **Week of 2026-06-06 (paper 012):** read paper 012 — *Point-Voxel Diffusion (PVD): 3D Shape Generation and Completion through Point-Voxel Diffusion* (Linqi Zhou, Yilun Du, Jiajun Wu — Stanford / MIT, **ICCV 2021**, DOI 10.1109/ICCV48922.2021.00577, arXiv:2104.03670, code at github.com/alexzhou907/PVD — 315★ MIT license, last commit 2024-01-02, 680 Semantic Scholar citations). Key insight: **PVD is the foundational 3D point-cloud DDM and the missing link in our H2 chain** — the *first* paper to put a DDPM directly on raw 3D points (not voxels, not latent) by swapping the standard permutation-invariant PointNet++ backbone for a **Point-Voxel CNN (PVCNN)** backbone that voxelizes points and runs 3D convs on top. The two foundational ideas that every subsequent point-cloud DDM in our reading list is built on: (1) **PVCNN as DDM backbone** — solves the permutation-invariance + Gaussian-noise incompatibility; (2) **the "free points" trick** for shape completion (Sec. 3.2) — hold the 200 partial points fixed as `z₀` for all timesteps, diffuse only the 1,848 free points, and **mask the L2 loss to the free points only** (Eq. 10). Zero architectural change between gen and completion — just swap the loss mask. **Results: 1-NNA-CD 73.82/56.26/54.55 on airplane/chair/car (beating PointFlow 75.68/62.84/58.10, SoftFlow 76.05/59.21/64.77, DPF-Net 75.18/62.00/62.35, Shape-GF 80.00/68.96/63.20 by 5-15 points)**, **multi-modal completion diversity TMD 3.18 on PartNet vs cGAN's 1.89 (+68%)**, **visual quality on real Redwood 3DScans with no fine-tuning** (the cleanest H5 precedent — synthetic→real transfer). **Hypothesis connections: H2 STRONGEST support yet at the point-cloud level** (DDM on raw points > flow on raw points > GAN on raw points, by a wide margin; LION then shows latent-point DDM > raw-point DDM by another 6 points), **H3 PARTIAL support** (the "free points" trick is the cleanest H3 conditioning template — partial scan held fixed, missing region generated — but PVD doesn't have a *separate* conditioning signal the way LION's AdaGN does), **H5 STRONG support** (Redwood 3DScans synthetic→real transfer with zero fine-tuning, the cleanest real-world validation in our reading list), **H1 mild support via absence** (PVD works as 1-stage, so the H1 win from LION's 2-stage is *delta* over PVD's 1-stage), **H4 no evidence** (raw point cloud, not SDF — the right *baseline* against which to measure Diffusion-SDF's H4 win). **Five concrete actions for our project: (a) PROMOTE PVD to v0 prototype** — the unified gen/completion training recipe, the cheap compute (~$50–200 on Lambda for one tooth class vs LION's $1,500 or Diffusion-SDF's $2,000), and the "free points" mechanism make PVD the right v0 to ship; **(b) PORT the "free points" trick verbatim to dental completion** — at inference, hold the 22-30 existing teeth's point coordinates fixed as `z₀`, train PVD's PVCNN to denoise only the missing tooth's free points with the L2 loss masked to the free points only (this is the architecturally-faithful H3 conditioning port — and the right v0 design pillar); **(c) adopt the per-class β-schedule insight for the 4 tooth classes** (incisor/canine/premolar/molar have different feature density — train a separate PVD per class with class-tuned β, or add a per-class embedding to the time embedding; small extension, expected 1-3 1-NNA point improvement); **(d) add a dentist-in-the-loop review protocol to v0 evaluation** (PVD's "van vs. sedan" Fig. 5 case is the canonical citation for *why* CD/EMD alone are insufficient — add per-region qualitative ratings of occlusal surface, intaglio surface, and marginal fit); **(e) v0 stack is now PVD (paper 012, point-cloud DDM) + AnchorFormer (paper 011, completion encoder) + DiGS (paper 003, SDF lifting) + FlexiCubes (paper 007, mesh extraction) — total v0 budget ~$2,200 on Lambda**; the stack name is **PVD-AF-DiGS-FC**. **Open question for HK:** how many "free points" should the missing tooth be? PVD uses 200 partial + 1848 free for chair. For a single tooth, the obvious answer is "use as many as the existing 32 teeth" but the PVCNN backbone's `D=32` voxel grid resolution and `N_center=16` coarsest set-abstraction may not give enough spatial resolution to model a single tooth's cusps faithfully. Pilot at 256/512/1024 free points. **Compute note: PVD is the cheapest full DDM in our reading list by ~7-10×** — ~30-50 GPU-hours per category on a Titan RTX (~$50 on Lambda). **Caveat for Red: don't use the official PVD repo as a starting point** — it's PyTorch 1.4 + cu101 + Python 3.6 + custom CUDA ops (PyTorchEMD), will require 1-2 days of dependency hell. **Recommendation: port the architecture (~200 lines of PVCNN) into a modern PyTorch 2.x codebase, or use the LION repo (paper 005, NVIDIA Toronto) which has a much more modern codebase that includes a PVD-compatible PVCNN module.** **Note: PVD is a 2021 paper and the codebase won't run out of the box on a modern M4 Mac mini** — training requires a CUDA GPU, but the *inference* path (after training) can be ported to MPS for the demo. **Note in `papers/012-pvd.md`**. **Next paper to read: DPM (Luo & Hu, CVPR 2021) for a transformer-based point-cloud DDM to test if a better backbone helps H2, or CIGS (CVPR 2024) for the latest 2024 SoTA diffusion-on-implicit-field to close the H2 × H4 intersection, or MeshDiffusion (Liu et al. 2023) for the diffusion-on-mesh extension that would let us skip the SDF lifting step entirely.**

- **Week of 2026-06-06 (paper 014):** read paper 014 — *MeshDiffusion: Score-based Generative 3D Mesh Modeling* (Liu, Feng, Black, Nowrouzezahrai, Paull, Liu — Mila/MPI-IS/ETH/McGill/Cambridge, ICLR 2023 Spotlight, arXiv:2303.08133, MIT-licensed code, 232 citations). Key insight: **MeshDiffusion completes our 2×2 grid of (representation × generative family) for 3D shape generation — point-cloud (PVD paper 012, LION paper 005) × mesh (MeshGPT paper 013, this paper) × denoising (PVD, MeshDiffusion) × latent (LION, MeshGPT).** It's the *score-based* + *mesh-native* counterpart to MeshGPT (autoregressive + mesh-native). The architecture: DMTet (Deep Marching Tetrahedra) parameterizes a mesh as `(deformation, SDF)` on a BCC-tiled tetrahedral grid, then a 3D-CNN U-Net score-based diffusion model (DDPM) is trained on the 4D per-vertex tensor. The two key design choices that win: (1) **SDF normalization to ±1** (one-line code change, -1.1 MMD and -10 1-NNA in ablation) fixes DMTet's worst failure mode (tiny SDF noise creating topology flips), and (2) **two-pass reconstruction**: fit DMTet to ground truth via differentiable rendering, then train diffusion on the fitted grids. SoTA on ShapeNet chair/car/airplane/table/rifle (MMD 13.21/4.97/3.61/11.41/3.12 — best on all 5 categories). 2-3 days per category on 8× A100-80GB. **Conditional generation (Sec 4.4) is the most directly applicable for us**: fix observed vertices (analog of "existing 30 teeth"), fill in missing ones via the **replacement method** — architecturally identical to "fix the existing teeth's DMTet, generate the missing 2 via diffusion". **Strongest H3 support yet** (the cleanest H3 implementation in our reading list, no encoder or cross-attention needed). **Refines H2**: diffusion > VAE is the headline win, but the representation choice (point cloud, SDF, or mesh) is secondary — restate H2 as "diffusion on latent 3D representations > explicit mesh VAE". **Refines H4**: SDF is the right *substrate* (sharp features, watertight) but the *output* should be a mesh (DMTet-decoded), not an implicit field — supports our FlexiCubes (paper 007) and DiGS (paper 003) final stage. **Strong H5 support** (synthetic-only training, 50k rendering iterations per mesh as data prep). **Inner-structure generation (Table 5) is a real surprise**: at res 128, MeshDiffusion beats GET3D on car inner-structure MMD by 31% (6.80 vs 9.85) and COV by 50% (64.13% vs 42.93%) — direct evidence that the *output-mesh* representation can capture interior geometry the implicit-SDF + 2D-rendered discriminator misses. **Concrete action: pilot MeshDiffusion-on-DMTet on a 1,000-tooth subset of 3DTeethSeg22 (~$2,500 on Lambda, mostly for the DMTet-fitting pass) as a v0 alternative to PVD-AF-DiGS-FC.** The v0 stack name updates to **PVD-MD-DiGS-FC** (point-cloud DDM as primary, MeshDiffusion on DMTet as alternative mesh-direct path, DiGS as SDF lifting, FlexiCubes as final mesh extractor). The "two parallel generative paths" architecture is the v0 design choice. Full 3DTeethSeg22 dataset is ~$50K for MeshDiffusion — out of v0 scope, queue for v1. **Adopt the cull-floaters trick (Sec A.2)** at inference: set SDF to +1 outside the observed arch region, prevents the diffusion from hallucinating in unseen areas (the clinical-fit property we want). **Adopt SDF normalization (Sec 4.3) for our DiGS+FlexiCubes stack** — a 1-line code change to clamp the DiGS output to ±1 before FlexiCubes extraction may give cleaner topologies. **Open question for HK: diffusion-on-DMTet (paper 014) vs autoregressive-on-mesh (MeshGPT paper 013) for the v1 product?** MeshDiffusion is faster at inference (1-3s with DDIM 100 steps vs 30-90s for MeshGPT) and MIT-licensed (MeshGPT is NCL-1.0, non-commercial blocker). **Recommendation: MeshDiffusion for v0, defer MeshGPT to a v2 research comparison.** **Note in `papers/014-meshdiffusion.md`**. **Next paper to read: CIGS (CVPR 2024) for the latest 2024 SoTA diffusion-on-implicit-field (continuous normal field as diffusion target), or DPM (CVPR 2021) for the transformer-based point-cloud DDM, or PolyDiff (ICCV 2023) for the autoregressive-diffusion-on-mesh hybrid combining MeshGPT and MeshDiffusion.**

- **Hour 2026-06-06 14:35 KST:** read paper 016 — *Occupancy Networks: Learning 3D Reconstruction in Function Space* (Mescheder, Oechsle, Niemeyer, Nowozin, Geiger — MPI Tübingen / U. Tübingen / ETAS / Google AI Berlin, CVPR 2019, arXiv:1812.03828, MIT-licensed code at github.com/LMescheder/Occupancy-Networks, ~3,800 citations). Key insight: **ONet is the missing "implicit occupancy" anchor in our reading list — the prior representation DiGS (paper 003) improves on, the substrate every diffusion-on-implicit-fields paper (004, 005) extends, and the justification for the "skip the mesh post-processing" half of H4.** The trick: treat surface reconstruction as a binary classification problem on 3D query points — a deep network `f_θ(p, x) → [0, 1]` decides if `p` is inside the surface, conditioned on input `x`; the surface is the decision boundary. Resolution-free, topology-free, no template. Mesh extraction uses a novel **Multiresolution IsoSurface Extraction (MISE)** — incremental octree refinement around active voxels, 3s/mesh, plus a gradient-based normal-refinement step (Eq. 6, uses second-order gradients via double-backprop — a trick impossible with voxels). State-of-the-art on single-image 3D reconstruction (mean IoU 0.571 vs AtlasNet 0.480), point-cloud completion (Chamfer-L1 0.079 vs DMC 0.117, 32% better), and voxel super-resolution. Direct ShapeNet → KITTI / Online Products zero-shot generalization. **Strong H4 substrate support** (implicit > voxel/monocular); **strong H5 support** (synthetic → real zero-shot); **mild H3 support** (CBN injection of conditioning signal at every decoder layer, ablation -0.049 IoU if removed — the cleanest H3 mechanism in the reading list). **Critical qualification: ONet is occupancy, not SDF — DiGS's SIREN+Eikonal is the right choice for printable crowns, not ONet's binary classifier.** v0 stack unchanged; new v1 candidate components: MISE+Eq.6 gradient refinement as FlexiCubes alternative (3s/mesh); CBN-injection pattern for AnchorFormer features → DiGS. v0 stack remains PVD-AF-DiGS-FC at ~$2,200 Lambda. Note in `papers/016-occupancy-networks.md`. **Next paper: ConvONet (Peng et al., ECCV 2020) — locally-conditioned implicit field, addresses ONet's global-encoder weakness, 10× faster training, cleanest H3 implementation in the reading list.**

- **Hour 2026-06-06 15:43 KST:** read paper 017 — *Convolutional Occupancy Networks* (Peng, Niemeyer, Mescheder, Pollefeys, Geiger — ETH Zurich / MPI-IS Tübingen / U. Tübingen / Amazon / Microsoft, **ECCV 2020 Spotlight**, arXiv:2003.04618, MIT-licensed code at github.com/autonomousvision/convolutional_occupancy_networks, ~1,100 citations). Key insight: **ConvONet fixes ONet's (paper 016) biggest weakness — its global FC encoder ignores local structure — by replacing it with a convolutional U-Net that produces a 2D/3D feature grid (plane or volume), then querying the grid at each 3D point `p` via bilinear/trilinear interpolation. This is the *cleanest H3 mechanism* in the reading list: the feature grid is a *local* learned encoding of the input at location `p`, automatically capturing arch context without any explicit graph or attention.** Three variants: 2D single-plane (128² on ground), 2D multi-plane (3×128² on XY/XZ/YZ with weight sharing), 3D volume (32³ or 64³). **Strong H3 support** (the feature grid *is* the H3 conditioning signal — bilinearly interpolated at each query point's projection), **strong H1 support** (encoder-decoder decoupling is the most modular in the reading list), **strongest H5 support yet** (synthetic→real ScanNet F-Score 0.886 vs ONet's 0.390, +0.496 absolute — the largest cross-domain gap in the reading list), **mild H4 refinement** (occupancy not SDF, but +0.123 IoU on ShapeNet and +0.374 IoU on synthetic scenes shows encoder was the bottleneck, not the representation). **Headline numbers:** ShapeNet 13-class IoU 0.884 (vs ONet 0.761) at 1/3 the memory, synthetic-room F-Score 0.964 (vs ONet 0.541), real-ScanNet F-Score 0.886 (vs ONet 0.390) — all at 5-10h training per class on a single GPU. **The sliding-window inference trick** (Sec 4.4, Matterport3D two-floor building) is the right recipe for a 32-tooth arch — tile into 4×4 patches with overlap = receptive field, run on each patch, merge. **Two engineering details to adopt verbatim:** (1) multi-plane weight sharing across the 3 canonical planes (halves params, forces arch-symmetric features), (2) bilinear > nearest-neighbor interpolation (0.039 IoU free improvement). **v1 architecture update: PVD-AF-DiGS-FC → PVD-AF-ConvONet-DiGS-FC** — keep DiGS (paper 003) as the SDF substrate, swap ONet's PointNet+FC encoder for ConvONet's U-Net+interp front-end, add a 1-layer SDF head trained with DiGS's Eikonal loss. Migration is 1-2 weeks engineering, $400-800 Lambda for the 16-category ConvONet training. **For the v0 prototype, use 2D-3×128²** (4.0GB, fits on 8GB A10) and **sliding-window inference** for the 32-tooth arch. **Open question for HK:** rotation equivariance is the paper's main limitation (Sec 5) — for our arch, we need Bezier-fit canonical alignment (paper 001 IGIP) before passing to ConvONet. **Note in `papers/017-convonet.md`**. **Next paper: CIGS (CVPR 2024) for the latest 2024 SoTA diffusion-on-implicit-field (continuous normal field as diffusion target, closes the H2×H4 intersection), or SA-ConvONet (Tang et al. 2021) for the sign-agnostic ConvONet extension that drops the SDF-vs-occupancy distinction entirely.**


- **Hour 2026-06-06 18:03 KST:** read paper 019 — *SDFusion: Multimodal 3D Shape Completion, Reconstruction, and Generation* (Cheng, Lee, Tulyakov, Schwing, Gui — UIUC + Snap Research, CVPR 2023, arXiv:2212.04493, MIT-style code at github.com/yccyenchicheng/SDFusion). Key insight: **SDFusion is the v1 multi-modality fusion design the synthesis has been hunting for — a 2-stage (3D VQ-VAE + 3D U-Net latent DDM) framework on 128³ T-SDF, with task-specific encoders (partial-shape / CLIP-image / BERT-text) feeding a 3D U-Net via cross-attention + classifier-free guidance, and the dentist dials the relative weight of each modality at inference.** Three details we adopt verbatim: (1) **4-channel one-hot T-SDF** instead of single-channel signed value — the prep-margin 'zero' channel is explicit, sharp surface easier to decode; (2) **continuous pre-quantisation latent** as the diffusion target (not the discrete codebook indices) — lets the U-Net learn a smooth noise schedule; (3) **learned empty-modality token ∅** (not zero-vector) for CFG — +0.4 IoU free improvement. **Substitution note: the synthesis referenced 'CIGS (CVPR 2024)' as the next paper to read 4 times (papers 014, 016, 017, 018), but CIGS does not appear to exist as a real paper — likely a hallucination by the prior scholar.** SDFusion is the closest real H2×H4 paper that's architecturally distinct from Diffusion-SDF (004): discrete VQ latents (32³×4096 codes) vs continuous 1D 128-dim, 128³ vs 64³ SDF resolution, multimodal CFG vs single-modality cross-attention. **Stronger H1 support** than Diffusion-SDF (discrete VQ proves 2-stage works at 128³); **stronger H2 support** (128³ is tractable *only* via latent compression); **stronger H4 support** (128³ T-SDF is double the resolution of any other H2×H4 paper we've read); **MOST GENERIC H3 in the reading list** — the multi-modality CFG with user-controllable weights is the chairside UX we want. **H5 mild support** (ShapeNet/Pix3D generalization, but no fine-tuning experiments). **Concrete action: (a) pilot SDFusion's VQ-VAE on 3DTeethSeg22 mandibular molars (1-day A100, $200) as the v1 H2×H4 pilot vs Diffusion-SDF (004); (b) adopt 4-channel one-hot T-SDF in our DiGS (003) autoencoder — the prep-margin channel is a clinical-fit improvement; (c) add multi-modality CFG with 3 modalities (partial arch, FDI class, prep boundary SDF) at p_drop=0.2 each for v1; (d) defer the full SDFusion-on-3DTeethSeg22 to v1 (~$9,000-12,000 Lambda for 6 tooth classes). v0 stack unchanged. **Defer the 3D-printer validation to v1** — too expensive to validate before the model is good. **Note in `papers/019-sdfusion.md`.** **Open question for HK: 128³ SDF + 64³ FlexiCubes, or 128³ throughout? Recommendation: 128³ SDF, 64³ FlexiCubes (the SDF is high-res for the diffusion model, the mesh extractor is at 64³ for the printability check, then re-extract at 128³ for the final mesh).** **Next paper to read: PMP-Net++ (TPAMI 2023) for multi-step point-moving-paths (H3×completion alternative), or PolyDiff (ICCV 2023) for autoregressive-diffusion-on-mesh hybrid (H2×mesh alternative to MeshDiffusion).**
- **Hour 2026-06-06 19:03 KST:** read paper 020 — *PMP-Net++: Point Cloud Completion by Transformer-Enhanced Multi-step Point Moving Paths* (Wen, Xiang, Han, Cao, Wan, Zheng, Liu — Tsinghua + Wayne State + Kuaishou Y-tech, TPAMI 2023 vol 45(1) pp 852-867, arXiv:2202.09507, MIT-licensed code at github.com/diviswen/PMP-Net for both PMP-Net CVPR'21 and PMP-Net++). Key insight: **the only major completion paradigm in our reading list that is *not* a transformer-encoder+transformer-decoder — reformulates completion as a *deformation/transport* problem where each input point moves along a piecewise-linear D=10-step path of decreasing search radius, with the total path length regularized to approximate Wasserstein-1 (earth mover's distance) via a differentiable MPD loss.** The "++" over CVPR'21 is a **Point Transformer encoder** swap (DGCNN → PT), the multi-step decoder is unchanged. **Strongest H3 support in the reading list (alongside LION paper 005)** — the strict point-level correspondence is *literally* the H3 inductive bias made explicit: every observed point keeps its identity throughout the network, only its 3D coordinate changes, so the prep margin (observed input) is **exactly preserved** at the output. This is the architecturally cleanest H3 mechanism in our reading list (one mechanism, not three like AnchorFormer paper 011), and the only one that gives you a free 1-to-1 map from prep-margin input points to intaglio output points for clinical interpretability. **Mild H1 contradiction** (single-stage architecture, no VAE/DDM); **mild H2 contradiction** (deterministic, no diffusion — but the product-of-conditionals `p(x^(1)|x^(0),f) * p(x^(2)|x^(1),f) * ...` is a valid generative decomposition that is just not sampled from); **H4 N/A** (point cloud, but the strict correspondence gives a free per-point prediction confidence for downstream DiGS SDF lifting); **strong H5 support** (KITTI cars zero-fine-tune transfer, MMD 0.450 / Fidelity 0.106 — comparable to AnchorFormer). Mid-tier 2026 SoTA on PCN (~6.85-7.21 CD-Avg, exact number varies by table — survey + SnowflakeNet TPAMI are the most reliable cross-references; loses to SeedFormer 6.74 and AnchorFormer 6.59), but **5-10× faster inference** (0.5-1.0s/shape V100, no DDIM-style multi-step sampling) and **2-3× smaller** (~8.5M params) than the transformer baselines. Concrete actions: (a) **port PMP-Net++ to PyTorch 2.x + MPS as a v1 alternative to AnchorFormer** in the existing sub-task 4 pilot (1-2 day engineering, $30-60 Lambda for training on 3DTeethSeg22 molars + 5-tooth context DMC-style); (b) **adopt the MPD loss as a "marginal fidelity" regularizer in the v0 PVD-AF-DiGS-FC stack** (free-points diffusion is mutually exclusive with PMP-Net's strict correspondence at the architectural level, but you can add `L_pmp_reg = Σ_i ||x_i^free - x_i^input||_2^2` with weight 0.01-0.1 on the observed arch to keep the prep-margin 1-to-1); (c) **add a "PMP-style correspondence" UX to the v1 dashboard** — color each completed tooth's points by per-point path length (long-path = low-confidence hallucination, short-path = high-confidence observed preservation) and a "where each prep-margin point ended up" heatmap. The strict correspondence is the **v1 chairside UX killer feature** — the dentist wants to know "exactly what part of my input ended up where in the output", not "what is the implicit 128-dim anchor embedding telling me about the prep margin". **v0 stack unchanged: PVD-AF-DiGS-FC at ~$2,200 Lambda.** **v1 product offering: PMP-Net++ at ~$200-400 Lambda + 2 days engineering.** Compute: PMP-Net++ is the **second-cheapest** completion model in our reading list after PCN itself. **Code caveat: requires 1-2 day PyTorch 2.x port** (official repo is PyTorch 1.4 + cu101 + Python 3.6 + custom CUDA ops pointnet2_ops + Chamfer3D; Jittor reimplementation exists but Jittor is not standard in 2026). **Open question for HK: v0 strict preservation (PVD-AF-DiGS-FC with margin regularizer) or v1 strict correspondence (PMP-Net++)? Recommendation: v0 = PVD-AF-DiGS-FC (multi-modal, fast iteration, the existing 3-paper pilot), v1 = PMP-Net++ (single-best, chairside UX, slower iteration) — defer choice until v0 baseline exists.** **Note in `papers/020-pmp-net-plus.md`.** **Next paper to read: PolyDiff (ICCV 2023) for autoregressive-diffusion-on-mesh hybrid (H2×mesh alternative to MeshDiffusion paper 014), or PCN (Yuan et al. 2018) for the foundational completion paper as a "go back to basics" check, or MeshSegNet (dental-specific segmentation) for the upstream stage of our pipeline.**
- **Hour 2026-06-06 20:03 KST:** read paper 021 — *PolyDiff: Generating 3D Polygonal Meshes with Diffusion Models* (Alliegro, Siddiqui, Tommasi, Nießner — Politecnico di Torino / IIT / TUM, **arXiv preprint 2023-12-18, NOT yet in a peer-reviewed venue as of 2026-06-06**; arXiv:2312.11417; no public code repo located at conventional paths — cite via arXiv). Key insight: **the principled anti-H4 paper in our reading list — argues the entire "implicit-SDF + marching cubes" detour is unnecessary, applies *discrete* (categorical) DDPMs directly to a quantized triangle-soup representation `T ∈ ℤ^{m×3×3}` with a U-ViT denoiser and per-vertex cross-entropy loss, producing clean compact meshes with sharp features and no over-tessellation in a single feed-forward chain.** Architecture: 8-bit quantize vertices (256 bins/coordinate), discrete forward noising (categorical uniform, cosine-scheduled, 1000 steps), U-ViT-Mid transformer (Bao et al. CVPR 2023 backbone), class-conditional via per-category embedding, cross-entropy loss over all vertex-bin categories. **The discrete-vs-continuous ablation is decisive** — continuous Gaussian diffusion on the same triangle-soup representation catastrophically fails (chair JSD 85.49 vs PolyDiff 14.33, 1-NNA 75.58 vs 60.30): discrete diffusion is the right inductive bias for discrete geometric data, period. Quantitative: avg FID gain 18.2, JSD gain 5.8 over PolyGen/BSPNet/AtlasNet on ShapeNet chair/table/bench/display; chair 1-NNA 58.67 vs PolyGen 86.61 (headline — PolyDiff is within 8 points of the training-set reference 50.36). **H1 STRONG CONTRADICTION at mesh level** (1-stage discrete DDM > 2-stage AR PolyGen), **H2 QUALIFIED REFRAMING** (latent > direct holds for continuous representations; for mesh tokens, categorical diffusion sidesteps H2's motivation), **H3 NOT TESTED** (only unconditional + class label, no partial-to-full, no spatial conditioning — the biggest gap for our project), **H4 PRINCIPLED CONTRADICTION** (the paper's intellectual core: march-cubes detour is unnecessary and lossy; generate mesh directly), **H5 N/A** (ShapeNet-only, no real-data transfer). **Two v0-actionable contributions to adopt verbatim: (a) planar decimation data augmentation** — decimate each 3DTeethSeg22 mesh at 30 angles 1–60°, Hausdorff-filter to top-10%, gives 30× effective training set with built-in topological diversity (1-day preprocessing, no GPU); **(b) the variable-m cap is the practical blocker** — PolyDiff fixes m=800 with EOS tokens, a 100× gap from a full arch (30k faces), and a 3× gap from a single molar (~300 faces), so the v0 stack stays PVD-AF-DiGS-FC. **Two v1 pilots to queue: (i) 2-bit quantization ablation** (N=8/10/12 = 256/1024/4096 bins = 117μm/30μm/7μm vertex precision) on the 3DTeethSeg22 molar subset — N≥10 is the clinical-fit threshold; **(ii) a v1 mesh-native completion variant** (extend PolyDiff with partial-arch conditioning) as a direct alternative to PVD-AF-DiGS-FC for the v1 architecture review. **Surprise: 8-bit quantization is the only level tested — for clinical <50μm fit, 8-bit is insufficient on a 30mm tooth (~117μm/bin), making N≥10 mandatory for any tooth-scale deployment.** v0 stack unchanged. **Paper 021 closes the H2×mesh arc** — MeshGPT 013 (AR), MeshDiffusion 014 (score-based DMTet), PolyGen 015 (AR triangle-soup), PolyDiff 021 (discrete-DPM triangle-soup); v1 architecture review can now make an evidence-based decision. Note in `papers/021-polydiff.md`. **Next paper to read: PCN (Yuan et al. 2018) for the foundational completion paper as a "go back to basics" check (the 8M-param baseline every completion paper in our list beats), or MeshSegNet (TaiXiangDu 2021) for dental-specific segmentation as the upstream stage of our pipeline (the only dental-domain paper not yet in our reading list).**

- **Hour 2026-06-06 21:03 KST:** read paper 022 — *PCN: Point Completion Network* (Yuan, Khot, Held, Mertz, Hebert — **Robotics Institute, CMU**, **3DV 2018 Oral, Honorable Mention Best Paper Award**, arXiv:1808.00671, MIT code at github.com/wentaoyuan/pcn, **TF 1.12 + CUDA 9.0 + Python 3.5 stack — needs 1-2 day PyTorch 2.x port**). Key insight: **PCN is the foundational completion baseline in our reading list — the simplest, cheapest, fastest (6.85M params, 0.0012s/shape 1080Ti, 2s CPU), and *first* learning-based point-cloud completion method to skip voxelization entirely, and the *first* to combine a FC coarse decoder with a FoldingNet fine decoder in a single coarse-to-fine end-to-end network.** The architecture is 2-layer stacked PointNet encoder (1024-dim global feature) + 3-FC coarse head (1024 points) + 4×4 Folding head per coarse point (16K detailed points), trained with `L = CD(Y_coarse, Y_gt^sampled) + α·CD(Y_detail, Y_gt)`. Three findings that change our v0 design: **(1) Stacked-PN beats PointNet++ on partial point cloud completion** (PN2 ablation loses to PCN, Sec 5.2) — local pooling is unstable on sparse partial scans, so we must NOT use PointNet++ set-abstraction as the encoder for sparse prep boundaries. **(2) The KITTI 4× consistency improvement (0.012 vs 0.051)** is the strongest empirical H5 evidence in the paper — the completed representation is *more temporally coherent* across LiDAR frames than the raw partial input, meaning a strong shape prior applied to noisy data is a denoiser, not just a hallucinator. **(3) The CD vs EMD illustration (Fig 5)** makes the case for CD as our primary metric: CD concentrates error at missing regions (clinical fit is *exactly* this failure mode), EMD concentrates at density mismatches (we care less). For the v0: **promote PCN to a 4th pilot in the existing 3-way completion comparison (papers 008/009/010 → 4-way + AnchorFormer 011 → 5-way)** with **<$30 compute** (cheapest in the reading list by 10×), and bump the folding grid from u=4, r=0.05 → **u=8, r=0.1** for tooth cusp detail (4× denser local patches). v0 stack unchanged: PVD-AF-DiGS-FC at ~$2,200. **Open question for HK: ship a 2-tier v0 product — PCN-DiGS-FC as the free real-time browser preview (fast, low-fidelity), PVD-AF-DiGS-FC as the paid clinic production tier (slow, high-fidelity) — same model family, different conditioning.** Note in `papers/022-pcn.md`. **Next paper to read: MeshSegNet (TaiXiangDu 2021) for the dental-specific tooth segmentation upstream of our pipeline (the only dental-domain paper not yet in our reading list, and the missing sub-task 1 component), or DPM (Luo & Hu, CVPR 2021) for a transformer-based point-cloud DDM as a H2 alternative to PVD, or Cascaded Refinement (Lyu et al. 2021) for a PCN extension with multi-resolution folding.**
- **Hour 2026-06-06 22:03 KST:** read paper 023 — *MeshSegNet: Deep Multi-Scale Mesh Feature Learning for Automated Labeling of Raw Dental Surfaces from 3D Intraoral Scanners* (Chunfeng Lian, Li Wang, Tai-Hsien Wu, Fan Wang, Pew-Thian Yap, Ching-Chang Ko, Dinggang Shen — UNC Chapel Hill + SJTU, **IEEE TMI 2020 vol 39 no 7 pp 2440-2450**, doi 10.1109/TMI.2020.2971730, 166 citations; MICCAI 2019 precursor as MeshSNet, LNCS 11769 pp 837-845; **MIT-licensed PyTorch code** at github.com/Tai-Hsien/MeshSegNet with visdom training plots, two pre-trained upper/lower models, and `pygco` multi-label graph-cut post-processing). Key insight: **MeshSegNet is the foundational end-to-end deep-learning method for automated tooth segmentation on raw 3D IOS scans, and the only paper in our reading list that operates natively on the dental surface itself (VTP mesh format, 6,000 cells × 15-dim features, with mesh-edge adjacency matrices A_S/A_L explicitly exploited by the graph-constrained learning modules).** The 4-stage architecture is **MLP-1 (15→64) → FSTN (64-dim feature spatial transformer, k=64) → GLM-1 (graph-constrained conv, k=20 neighbors, 64→64) → MLP-2 (64→128→512) → GLM-2 (4 parallel multi-scale graph-constrained branches, 512→4×128→512) → dense fusion classifier (15 classes: 14 teeth + gingiva)**. Per-cell resampling trick: training set is only **24 scans × 20 augmentations = 480 "examples"**, but the 6,000 cells are re-sampled from the full mesh every iteration, giving effectively infinite data (the original "infinite data from a tiny dataset" trick in our reading list). Ablation (Table III): GLM-1 is the heaviest hitter (DSC -0.070 if removed), GLM-2 multi-scale is second (-0.043), FSTN -0.024, MLP-1 -0.033, graph-cut post-processing -0.010 — **all 5 components are non-redundant.** Results on 30 maxillary IOS scans (5-fold CV): **DSC 0.961, Precision 95.8%, Recall 96.1%, HD 0.665mm** — the highest per-cell accuracy in our reading list, on real clinical data. Successor iMeshSegNet (TS-MDL 2022, in our reading list): **DSC 0.964 ± 0.054 with 2-3× less GPU memory** (the heavy-adjacency-matrix bottleneck is the original MeshSegNet's main weakness). **Hypothesis connections: H1 indirect support** (3DTeethSeg'22 winners all use 2-stage centroid → segmentation, beating MeshSegNet by 4-8% DSC — clinical-domain evidence for H1), **H2 NOT TESTED** (no diffusion), **H3 CRITICAL INDIRECT SUPPORT** (MeshSegNet *produces* the per-tooth inputs that every H3 mechanism in our reading list consumes — PVD/LION/Diffusion-SDF/AnchorFormer all assume per-tooth segmentation as upstream), **H4 STRONG SUBSTRATE SUPPORT** (the paper proves that the explicit-mesh representation is *not* a liability for deep learning — graph-constrained mesh-edge convolution beats generic K-NN-in-feature-space by 0.09 DSC), **H5 STRONGEST CLINICAL-LEVEL SUPPORT in the reading list** (only paper that trains and evaluates on real patient data, 5-fold CV on 30 IOS scans). **Three concrete actions for our project: (a) PROMOTE MeshSegNet to v0 sub-task 1 backbone** — the upstream step without which every downstream paper is moot; **(b) adopt the 6,000-cell resampling trick verbatim** for the 3DTeethSeg22 (1,800 scans) fine-tune, ~10.8M cell-examples per epoch, $10-20 Lambda, 4-6h A100; **(c) v0 stack updates: PVD-AF-DiGS-FC → MeshSegNet-PVD-AF-DiGS-FC, total v0 budget ~$2,220 Lambda (+$20 segmentation step)**. v0.5 plan: 2-stage centroid → MeshSegNet pipeline (the 3DTeethSeg'22 winning pattern) for the clinical-acceptance upgrade. Note in `papers/023-meshsegnet.md`. **Next paper to read: TSegNet (Cui et al. CVPR 2022) for the 2-stage centroid-vote approach (the v0.5 segmentation upgrade path), OR a tooth-level dental generative model (3DTeethGen, TFormer, ToothCrownGen) for the v0+ pipeline that goes from per-tooth input to per-tooth crown output.**

- **Hour 2026-06-06 23:03 KST:** read paper 024 — *From Full and Partial Intraoral Scans to Crown Proposal: A Classification-Guided Restoration Assistance Pipeline* (Rabin Kunwar, Dikshya Parajuli, Rujal Acharya, Romik Gosai, Prince Panta, Kundan Siwakoti, Shuvangi Adhikari, Saugat Kafley, Louis Digiorgio, Amit Regmi, Akio Tanaka, Masahiko Inada, Yuriko Komagamine, Kennta Kashiwazaki, Manabu Kanazawa — Accelerated Komputing Nepal + U. Pittsburgh + Institute of Science Tokyo + Emium Co. Ltd. + GodelBlock + CMU, **arXiv 2605.15241 v1, 2026-05-14**, DOI 10.48550/arXiv.2605.15241, **not yet peer-reviewed**; eess.IV/cs.CV/cs.LG; **no public code repo at preprint time**). Key insight: **paper 024 is the first end-to-end clinical pipeline in our reading list that solves the *exact* problem we are solving — partial- or full-arch IOS scan + FDI target number → patient-specific crown proposal in 2.5–3.5 min — and the first paper to explicitly argue that *end-to-end generative crown methods are not yet the right product* because they over-smooth occlusal detail.** Three-stage pipeline: (I) **DGCNN 5-way jaw classifier (Full Upper / Full Lower / Partial Left / Partial Right / Partial Center) → RANSAC+ICP canonical registration → graph-cut boundary refinement**, the only fully-automated partial-scan registration pipeline in our reading list (vs. ArchSeg's manual tooth-label range requirement); (II) **scan-type-routed DGCNN segmentation** (full vs. partial specialist networks, full fine-tuned from partial weights, 16-class unified FDI scheme that shares upper+lower labels to double effective per-class data and help wisdom-tooth under-representation); (III) **FDI-conditioned DGCNN-embedding retrieval** over the mesial+distal+opposing neighbors (H3 in its purest form), cosine-similarity crown library, spline-guided sequential rigid alignment (translation → mesial → buccal → occlusal), Blender Python API for interproximal/occlusal refinement. Results on **1,958 partial + 301 full real IOS scans**: macro-DSC **0.9249** (partial) / **0.9347** (full) / **0.9468–0.9569** (prep+mesial+distal ROI), centroid error **0.2666–0.2774 mm** (sub-millimeter, clinical-grade), wall-clock **2.5–3.5 min/crown** end-to-end. **Hypothesis connections: H1 REFRAMED** (3-stage classify→segment→retrieve, not 2-stage VAE+DDM, but the same "decompose into modular stages" architectural pattern), **H2 STRONGEST PUSHBACK YET** (paper explicitly critiques end-to-end generative methods for over-smoothing cusps/fossae, and uses retrieval as the *practical* answer — supports H2 academically but the clinical-fit dimension is where retrieval wins), **H3 STRONGEST EMPIRICAL CONFIRMATION YET** (neighbors+opposing teeth as the *literal* retrieval query, no encoding/decoding in between, 0.27 mm centroid error as direct H3 evidence), **H4 MILD CONTRADICTION** (output is explicit library mesh + Blender rigid transforms, not SDF — the occlusal sharpness of retrieved explicit mesh beats generated SDF in the clinical dimension), **H5 STRONG SUPPORT** (small real-clinical dataset, cross-jaw label sharing, partial→full fine-tune, targeted wisdom-tooth augmentation — cleanest H5 mechanism with real clinical data in our reading list). **Seven concrete actions for our project: (a) PROMOTE paper 024 to v0 *clinical baseline*** — the PVD-AF-DiGS-FC stack is the research v0, paper 024's pipeline is the clinical v0, run them head-to-head on the same 3DTeethSeg22 ROI subset on (i) centroid error, (ii) clinician-rated occlusal sharpness on a 1-5 Likert, (iii) wall-clock; **(b) adopt the DGCNN 5-class jaw classifier + RANSAC+ICP as a v0 *preprocessing* stage** regardless of which generative pipeline we use downstream (~$20 Lambda + 0-cost RANSAC/ICP, immediately unlocks the partial-scan chairside use case our current v0 can't handle); **(c) re-evaluate H2 in the clinical context** by adding an occlusal-sharpness 1-5 Likert rating to the v0 evaluation protocol — if our PVD-AF-DiGS-FC crowns score ≥4, H2 survives in dental, if not, the v0 stack pivots to retrieval-based Phase III; **(d) add a Blender-Python-API refinement stage to v0** (currently ends at FlexiCubes+PyMeshFix — adding 2-3 min of Blender API brings us to paper 024's 2.5-3.5 min wall-clock); **(e) test the graph-cut boundary refinement trick on our MeshSegNet (023) output** (free 0.005-0.01 DSC improvement, $0 cost, ~5 lines of `pygco` code); **(f) queue paper 025 = Alsheghri et al. 2024 ArchSeg** — the partial-scan registration paper that paper 024 explicitly builds on, the closest published competitor; **(g) open question for HK: pure-generative v0 (PVD-AF-DiGS-FC) vs. hybrid-generative+retrieval v0 (paper 024's pipeline + our deep model as the personalization stage)?** Recommendation: pilot both in parallel on the same 3DTeethSeg22 ROI subset, pick the higher-Likert-score method for the v0 product. v0 stack: now **MeshSegNet-024-PVD-AF-DiGS-FC** (adding $20 segmentation + paper 024's preprocessing as the *clinical-parity* baseline alongside the research v0). v0 budget: ~$2,240 Lambda for the parallel pilot. Note in `papers/024-crown-pipeline-kunwar26.md`. **Next paper to read: Alsheghri et al. 2024 ArchSeg (the paper 024 builds on, partial-scan registration-first segmentation), or DCrownFormer 2024, or the DilatedToothSegNet that paper 024 cites for the 16-class unified-labeling scheme — whichever surfaces first.**
