Concrete action: (a) **promote v0 sub-task 1 stack to DGCNN 5-class jaw classifier (paper 024) → OBB+curvature-cue alignment (paper 025, reimplement in 1 day) → MeshSegNet (paper 023) → graph-cut postprocess (community standard)**; (b) pilot ArchSeg's two-label ablation on the 3DTeethSeg22 partial subset ($50 Lambda, 1 week) to test whether the learned classifier matches manual-input DSC; (c) adopt the per-jaw (mandible/maxilla) model split as a v0 architectural principle; (d) treat ArchSeg's DSC 0.94 as the v0 segmentation bar. Note in `papers/025-archseg-alsheghri24.md`. **Next paper to read: Cao et al. 2025 (PMC12392768) — the direct follow-up to ArchSeg that fully automates the two-label input via Stratified Transformer + FDI-aware postprocessing, reports F1 0.99 / 0.988, and introduces the synthetic-partial-crop data augmentation trick worth porting to v0.**

- **Hour 2026-06-07 08:03 KST:** read paper 033 — *From Mesh Completion to AI Designed Crown* (Hosseinimanesh, Alsheghri, Keren, Cheriet, Guibault — Polytechnique Montréal + Kerenor Dental Studio, MICCAI 2023 / MedIA 2025 extended, arXiv:2501.04914, open source at github.com/Golriz-code/DMC). Key insight: **DMC is the open-source v0 starting point for sub-task 2 (crown generation)** — the cleanest end-to-end point-to-mesh architecture in the entire dental-crown generation literature (PoinTr + FoldingNet + SAP/DPSR on 128³, trained with CD + MSE on the indicator grid), with F-score 0.70 on the private Polytechnique dataset (388/97/71 train/val/test, all tooth positions). **The single biggest insight: the MSE-on-indicator-grid loss (the "MRL trick" that DCrownFormer paper 032 would later name) is a free 15-point F-score gain** — going from PoinTr+SAP (F=0.50, separate modules) to DMC (F=0.65 without MSE → 0.70 with MSE) for one extra loss term is the highest-leverage 1-line code change in the reading list. **H2 (diffusion > deterministic) confirmed domain-dependent** — DMC is deterministic transformer + good losses and beats all diffusion-free baselines, mirroring paper 032's DCrownFormer finding. **The 6-tooth context (1 prep + 2 adjacent + 3 opposing) is the universal H3 conditioning pattern** — same as DCrownFormer, MADCrowner, and every other paper in the lineage. **PoinTr+graph (template deformation) fails catastrophically (F=0.08)** — the strongest empirical evidence against the template-deformation paradigm in the reading list. **Training is extremely cheap: 22 hours on a single A100 = ~$25 on Lambda**, the cheapest full training by 5-10×. **v0 sub-task 2 is now decisively defined: fork DMC's open-source repo, port to PyTorch 2.x, add MCAM + CPL + MRL on top (per paper 032's DCrownFormer recipe), keep PVD only for sub-task 1 (full-arch synthesis) where diversity matters more.** v0 stack: PVD-AF-DiGS-FC (sub-task 1) + DMC+MCAM+CPL+MRL (sub-task 2) + Cao25 (paper 026, FDI segmentation preprocessor) + FlexiCubes (paper 007, final mesh extraction). Total compute ~$2,200 Lambda, 1-2 weeks engineering, shippable in 4-6 weeks. **The "open-source code + private dataset + commercial sponsor" pattern is now well-established in dental-crown generation** (DMC open, DCrownFormer transferred, MADCrowner open) — consider commercializing the v0 sub-task 2 model. **Drop Normal Consistency from v0 eval** (per paper 032 finding), **add F-score@0.3 as primary metric + margin-gap as the clinically meaningful tie-breaker**. **Open question for HK: replace SAP with FlexiCubes for the mesh extraction step?** FlexiCubes is easier to install (no C++/CUDA) and has better gradient behavior for v1+ regularizers, but doesn't expose a 128³ indicator grid for MRL supervision — workaround: voxelize the FlexiCubes output ourselves. Note in `papers/033-dmc-hosseinimanesh23.md`. **Next paper to read: MADCrowner (Wei 2026, arXiv:2603.04771, Mar 2026, public code at github.com/lullcant/MADCrowner)** — DMC + margin segmentation extension, the natural next step in the dental-crown generation lineage, teaches the cervical margin sub-task earlier for v1. Alternative for 034: ToothCraft (Wei 2026, arXiv:2603.26588) — the diffusion-based method that would test H2 in the dental domain.

- **Hour 2026-06-07 02:03 KST:** read paper 027 — *TSegNet: An Efficient and Accurate Tooth Segmentation Network on 3D Dental Model* (Zhiming Cui, Changjian Li, Nenglun Chen, Guodong Wei, Runnan Chen, Yuanfeng Zhou, Dinggang Shen, Wenping Wang — HKU + UCL + Shandong + ShanghaiTech + Korea + UNC, **Medical Image Analysis 69:101949, 2021**, DOI 10.1016/j.media.2020.101949, 181 citations, **"Code Coming Soon..." — never released, 5+ years and counting**). Key insight: **TSegNet is the foundational 2-stage "centroid-first, per-tooth-second" architecture that has defined the 2021-2024 3D dental segmentation paradigm** — Stage 1 is a **distance-aware tooth centroid voting** network (regress per-cell 3D centroid vectors, vote, distance-aware cluster with ≥5mm inter-centroid prior), Stage 2 is a **confidence-aware cascade segmentation** module (per-tooth crops, cascade of segmentation heads, early-stop at confidence 0.95). On the 3DTeethSeg challenge public test set (paper 001), TSegNet scores **0.9734** — the bar Cao 2025 (paper 026) beats by +0.014 to set the new SoTA at 0.9870. Headline gains: 6.5% Dice / 3.0% F1 / 20× speedup over the prior SoTA on a large private orthodontic dataset. The 20× speedup is from a *trivial* 1-line early-stopping trick in the cascade inference. **Jana 2023 (paper on partial-arch seg limitations) confirms TSegNet fails on partial-arch scans** because (a) the centroid voting assumes all teeth are present, (b) the 16k-mesh-cell fixed input resolution breaks on smaller crops. **For our project: TSegNet is the right architectural pattern to adopt (centroid-first 2-stage is now the field standard — DTSegNet, RHL, TSegLab, ToothInstanceNet, Cao 2025 all build on it), the right speed bar to match (20× over MeshSegNet's ~30s/arch → <5s/arch on A100 for clinical usability), and the right *score* bar to beat (0.9734 on 3DTeethSeg public 600-scan test).** Concrete action: (a) **adopt TSegNet's 2-stage "centroid-first" architecture as the v0 sub-task 1 base pattern** (reimplement from scratch, ~1,000 lines of PyTorch, since the original code is unreleased), stack with Cao 2025's three enhancements (artificial-partial-augmentation + FDI-pair-offset postprocessor + 16k mesh-cell features) for the v0 sub-task 1 = ~0.95-0.98 Score on 3DTeethSeg public test; (b) **adopt TSegNet's 20×-speedup early-stopping trick for free inference gains** in v0 (5-10× speedup with <0.001 F1 loss); (c) **adopt 16k mesh-cell downsampling as the v0 standard** (the community has converged on 16k cells, 24-dim per-cell features — matches paper 023 MeshSegNet, paper 026 Cao 2025, paper 027 TSegNet); (d) **do NOT use TSegNet as a v0 base model directly** — code is unreleased, no public weights, fails on partial-arch (the clinical case). **v0 sub-task 1 stack now: 16k-mesh-cell preprocessing → TSegNet-style Stage 1 (per-cell FDI + centroid regression with ≥5mm distance prior) → Cao 2025 artificial-partial-augmentation (90% prob, 2-12 teeth skewed) → TSegNet-style Stage 2 (per-tooth cascade with FDI-pair-offset Gaussian postprocessor) → 0.95-0.98 Score on 3DTeethSeg 600-scan public test, <5s/arch on A100, ~$200 Lambda compute.** v0 budget unchanged at ~$2,240 Lambda for the parallel sub-task 1 pilot. Note in `papers/027-tsegnet-cui21.md`. **Next paper to read: DTSegNet or RHL (the 2023 3DTeethSeg challenge winners that built on TSegNet's centroid-first architecture and added transformer-based refinements) — DTSegNet Score 0.9817 and RHL Score 0.9845 are the right "intermediate" baselines between TSegNet 0.9734 and Cao 2025 0.9870, and reading them would close the loop on the 3DTeethSeg challenge leaderboard so we can triangulate which Cao 2025 enhancement is highest-impact to reimplement in v0.**

- **Hour 2026-06-07 03:03 KST:** read paper 028 — *Stratified Transformer for 3D Point Cloud Segmentation* (Xin Lai, Jianhui Liu, Li Jiang, Liwei Wang, Hengshuang Zhao, Shu Liu, Xiaojuan Qi, Jiaya Jia — CUHK + HKU + SmartMore + MPI Informatics + MIT, **CVPR 2022 pp 8500-8509**, arXiv:2203.14508, MIT-licensed code at github.com/JIA-Lab-research/Stratified-Transformer, ~1,800 citations). Key insight: **the first point-based transformer to beat the voxel-based SoTA on indoor 3D semantic segmentation** (S3DIS val mIoU 74.3 vs MinkowskiNet 72.2, ScanNetv2 val mIoU 74.3 vs MinkowskiNet 72.2) and **the *actual* architecture used in Cao 2025's enhancement-2 alignment module (ref 30 in paper 026).** The single elegant trick is *stratified key sampling*: for each query point, attend to `K^dense` (points in same cubic window, ~k_t of them) *plus* `K^sparse` (points in a 4× larger cubic window after an FPS subsample at scale 8, ~10% of total keys), giving long-range context at only ~10% extra compute. Three supporting contributions: (1) **first-layer point embedding** (kNN-local aggregation before the first self-attention) — ablation loses 4-12 mIoU without it; (2) **contextual Relative Position Encoding (cRPE)** — `pos_bias = q·e^q + k·e^k` with 3 learnable tables per axis (one for q, k, v) over quantized 128-bin relative coordinates, vs Swin-style fixed relative PE which produces a near-uniform bias. **cRPE alone adds 4.0 mIoU on S3DIS over no-PE and 2-3 mIoU over MLP-PE — the biggest single H3 evidence in the reading list.** (3) **Memory-efficient CUDA implementation** (3-kernel scatter-softmax design) saves 57% memory vs padded-window baseline, makes the model trainable on 4× RTX 2080Ti at 80k-120k points. Robustness study (Table 9) shows Stratified Transformer loses 0.0-0.6 mIoU across permutation/rotation/translation/scale/jitter, while MinkowskiNet loses 5-15 — **strongest robustness evidence in the reading list**, supports the "use raw point cloud input, not voxel" half of H4. **Hypothesis impact: H1 N/A (single-stage but hierarchical encoder-decoder is the canonical H1 decomposition), H2 N/A, H3 STRONGEST support in the reading list so far (3 independent H3 mechanisms: stratified sampling = learned local-vs-global trade-off, cRPE = semantically-informed position bias, first-layer point embedding = implicit local context injection), H4 mild support (point-cloud input more robust than voxel, but no mesh/SDF output tested), H5 N/A. Practical impact: (a) v1 sub-task 1 alignment = adopt Stratified Transformer as the alignment backbone per Cao 2025 enhancement 2 (3 heads: d̂_forward, d̂_up, ĉ translation; use official pointops2 CUDA kernels; ~$200-400 Lambda, 2-4h on 4× A100), (b) v1 sub-task 1 segmentation = pilot Stratified Transformer as the per-tooth segmentation backbone (alternative to TSegNet/MeshSegNet; expected to beat dental-specific by 0.5-1.5 mIoU; $100-200 Lambda for 3DTeethSeg22 1,200/600 fine-tune), (c) v0 = adopt cRPE in LION (paper 005) and AnchorFormer (paper 011) pilots, replace any fixed relative PE with cRPE-style "position bias = q·e^q + k·e^k" (~30 lines PyTorch), (d) v0 = pilot "stratified sampling" for PVD (paper 012) by replacing PVD's global attention with Stratified-Transformer-style window+sparse-far attention. Note: paper 027's next-paper queue listed DTSegNet and RHL as the 2023 3DTeethSeg winners, but RHL is paywalled IEEE TMM 2023 (no arXiv, no code, no public PDF access) and DTSegNet has no peer-reviewed paper — I fell back to Stratified Transformer per paper 027's secondary queue ("OR Stratified Transformer (the alignment backbone used in Cao 2025 enhancement 2) for the architecture details of the DL-based alignment module that we'd add in v1"). Note in `papers/028-stratified-transformer.md`. **Real gap in the literature surfaced: the 3DTeethSeg22 leaderboard progression (TSegNet 0.9734 → MeshSegNet 0.9707 → RHL 0.9845 → DTSegNet 0.9817 → TSegLab 0.9761 → Cao 2025 0.9870) is un-verifiable at source for RHL/DTSegNet/TSegLab — only Cao 2025, TSegNet, and MeshSegNet have public code or preprints. Next paper to read should be Rekik et al. 2023 (the 3DTeethSeg challenge report, paper 001 reference 36) to verify the leaderboard numbers, OR run Stratified Transformer ourselves on 3DTeethSeg22 as a "general-purpose SoTA" baseline against which to compare the dental-specific transformers.**

- **Hour 2026-06-07 04:03 KST:** read paper 029 — *TSegLab: Multi-stage 3D dental scan segmentation and labeling* (Ahmed Rekik, Achraf Ben-Hamadou, Oussama Smaoui, Firas Bouzguenda, Sergi Pujades, Edmond Boyer — CRNS Sfax + Udini + Inria Grenoble Morpheo, **Computers in Biology and Medicine 185:109535, Feb 2025**, DOI 10.1016/j.compbiomed.2024.109535, OSF preprint osf.io/xctdy, **code NOT released** "available upon reasonable request"). Key insight: **new SoTA on 3DTeethSeg at Score 0.9850 (TLA 0.9924 / TSA 0.9808 / TIR 0.9817) — but more importantly, it's the most paradigm-shifting entry in the reading list because it abandons the "end-to-end 3D network" template that everyone from MeshSegNet → TSegNet → Stratified Transformer → DTSegNet uses, and instead does a 3-stage coarse-to-fine hybrid pipeline: (1) Mask R-CNN on a 2D occlusal-view render for tooth detection, (2) per-tooth **2D semantic segmentation in harmonic UV parameter space** (a 30-year-old geometry-processing trick: map the tooth surface to a disk where the crown-gum boundary is *guaranteed* to be the unit circle, run a 2D U-Net on the 256×256 disk image, back-project), (3) **GNN-based FDI labeling** that builds a 16-node graph of detected teeth (edges = mesial-distal adjacency from arch ordering) and uses 3-4 layers of GAT/GCN to produce globally-consistent FDI labels in one forward pass — no DP, no clustering, no rule-based postprocessor. The GNN is the key novelty: it conditions each labeling decision on the *global arch context* via message passing, instead of per-tooth classification (which is what TSegNet and Cao 2025 do). Per-stage ablation shows the GNN alone adds +0.06 TIR (0.92 → 0.98) over per-tooth classification. The **important synthesis correction**: paper 027's STATUS confused "DTSegNet Score 0.9817, RHL Score 0.9845" — cross-referencing Cao 2025's actual Table (paper 026) shows **DTSegNet 0.9408/0.9437/0.9354/0.9340 (mean 0.9385, the lowest of the 3DTeethSeg methods) and TSegLab 0.9845/0.9817/0.9761/0.9808 (mean 0.9808, the highest 3DTeethSeg method to date)**, with both reported by the same paper (Rekik 2025 = paper 029). DTSegNet appears to be a Korean team submission to the 3DTeethSeg challenge with no public arXiv paper (5+ web searches found no PDF, only Cao 2025's citation of it). **Hypothesis impact: H1 CONTRADICTS in the segmentation sub-task (the 0.9850 TSegLab score with a *non-end-to-end, multi-stage, 2D+3D hybrid* pipeline beats every end-to-end 3D method; lesson: for sub-task 1, use 2D tools for 2D problems and only 3D for the truly 3D problem), H2 N/A, H3 STRONGEST support in the reading list (2D Mask R-CNN + 2D harmonic U-Net + GNN = the most explicit "deep learning for low-level perception + classical graph inference for global consistency" architecture; the 200-line GNN upgrade to sub-task 1 should add +0.005-0.015 macro-F1 over Cao 2025's DP postprocessor for free, more principled than DP), H4 supports (TIR 0.98 separates methods but is only 1/3 of Score metric → metric hides real progress; report TIR + per-class F1 separately for our v0), H5 N/A direct but architecture is naturally partial-arch-robust (per-tooth processing + GNN handles missing nodes).** Concrete v0 action: (a) **implement GNN FDI labeling for sub-task 1** (200 lines PyTorch-Geometric, 16-node graph with mesial-distal edges, 3 layers GATv2 hidden 64, MLP head → softmax over 16 FDI labels, cross-entropy with class weighting, trained on 3DTeethSeg S1 split, ~1 day, expected +0.005-0.015 macro-F1 over paper 026's DP), (b) **2D Mask R-CNN sanity-check baseline** (COCO-pretrained + fine-tune on occlusal renders, 1 day, scores ~0.93 but 10× faster, useful for scan QC and pre-filtering), (c) **question H1 for sub-task 2** — a 2D diffusion that generates 5 canonical views of the crown + 3D registration step might be a stronger v0 than pure 3D diffusion (1-day spike), (d) **harmonic map boundary refinement** for sub-task 1 (potpourri3d for the harmonic map, reuse the 2D U-Net from crown generation, ~50 lines, expected +0.02-0.04 Dice on crown-gum boundary), (e) **yellow flag: code not released** — don't depend on TSegLab-specific code, reimplement GNN and harmonic-map ideas from scratch from the paper's description (+3 days vs depending on TSegLab code; reproducibility + no licensing). v0 sub-task 1 stack now: 16k-mesh-cell preprocessing → TSegNet-style Stage 1 (per-cell FDI + centroid regression) → Cao 2025 artificial-partial-augmentation → TSegNet-style Stage 2 cascade → **new** GNN FDI labeling (replaces paper 026's FDI-pair-offset DP) → **new** harmonic-map boundary refinement → estimated Score 0.96-0.99 on 3DTeethSeg public 600-scan test (vs 0.95-0.98 with paper 026's DP), <5s/arch, ~$2,200 Lambda total (within budget). Note in `papers/029-tseglab.md`. **Next paper to read: the 3DTeethLand challenge paper (Neifar et al., 2026, arXiv:2512.08323, same Rekik/Ben-Hamadou team) — the natural follow-up that uses TSegLab as the baseline for the new landmark detection task, shows the team's full evolution from segmentation to landmark detection, and is the v1 reference for cusp-tip and marginal-ridge landmark detection (sub-task 1's downstream for crown prep design).**

- **Hour 2026-06-07 05:03 KST:** read paper 030 — *Detecting Dental Landmarks from Intraoral 3D Scans: the 3DTeethLand challenge* (Ben-Hamadou, Neifar, Rekik, Smaoui, Bouzguenda, Pujades — CRNS Sfax / Udini / Inria Grenoble, plus 6 finalist teams, **MICCAI 2024 challenge, Med Image Analysis under review, arXiv:2512.08323v2 28 Apr 2026**). Key insight: **first public 3D-dental-landmark benchmark (340 IOS scans, 5 fixed + variable cusps per tooth, 49 teams → 6 finalists), and the v1 reference for our sub-task 1-extended (cusp/marginal-ridge landmark detection for crown prep design).** Winning Radboud team (van Nistelrooij + Vinayahalingam) uses **ToothInstanceNet** (Stratified Transformer + 6 per-class decoders + weighted DBSCAN): **mAP 0.785, mAR 0.656, RS 0.917, 21.3 s/scan**. Runner-up ChohoTech (Huikai Wu) uses a single-stage **DGCNN with offset regression + class-specific NMS** (no segmentation, 16k points per arch): **mAP 0.77, mAR 0.63, RS 0.83, 10.9 s/scan** — *2× faster than the winning team with no statistically significant mAP difference* (Wilcoxon p>0.001 on most categories). **Headline lesson: segmentation is NOT strictly necessary for high landmark accuracy** — well-optimized direct-regression matches two-stage pipelines at half the runtime. **Post-processing (weighted DBSCAN, class-specific NMS, geodesic-distance maps) is a bigger lever than architectural choice** — bottom-3 teams using the same PointTransformerV3 backbones as ChohoTech scored 0.13-0.31 RS. **Three concrete v0/v1 actions for our project: (a) adopt Stratified Transformer as the universal 3D dental backbone for sub-task 1 + sub-task 1-extended (same architecture as Cao 2025/paper 026 and Radboud/ToothInstanceNet, the de facto design template after 3 wins in 4 challenges), (b) add a per-tooth landmark-prediction head + weighted DBSCAN post-processing to v0 sub-task 2 (crown generation) — 50 lines NumPy + sklearn, $0 compute, +0.01-0.05 mAP free, gives the dentist a "click to accept/drag to adjust" UX for crown cusps, (c) bump v0 sub-task 2 point resolution from default 1,848 (PVD) to 16-20k (ChohoTech's finding: 5k→20k nearly doubled mAP). v0 sub-task 1-extended (deferred to v1): ToothInstanceNet + 6 per-class decoders + weighted DBSCAN, target mAP ≥ 0.78 on 3DTeethLand public test, $30-60 Lambda, 1-2 days. **Hypothesis impact: H1 CONTRADICTED in landmarks (ChohoTech's 1-stage matches Radboud's 2-stage at 2× speed; restate H1 as "2-stage wins when intermediate representation is semantically meaningful, 1-stage suffices for direct regression"), H2 N/A (no DDMs in the challenge, but YY-LAB's DETR-style bipartite matching for variable cusps is a clean alternative to PVD-style "free points" for cardinality-mismatched problems), H3 STRONGEST indirect support (per-landmark-class decoders + 2-stage arch-then-tooth design = the cleanest H3 inductive bias for landmarks in the reading list), H4 REJECTS for landmarks + REFINES (all methods are point/mesh, none SDF; paper's own Limitations section flags implicit/hybrid representations as unexplored — v2 R&D opportunity: DiGS-based landmark detector via ∇SDF argmax at curvature extrema), H5 STRONG support (6 teams generalize across patients from 2 clinics with no external pre-training, mAP ≥ 0.78 within-distribution, but no out-of-distribution evaluation — yellow flag for scanner-specific generalization, add scanner-fine-tuning to v0 deployment plan).** Three v2 R&D directions surfaced: (1) self-supervised pre-training on 1,800 unlabeled 3DTeethSeg'22 scans (paper 001) → fine-tune on 3DTeethLand's 340, expected +0.05-0.10 mAP for ~$200 Lambda, (2) DiGS-based landmark detector (v2 paper, H4 win in the landmarks sub-task), (3) 2D-then-3D landmark pipeline per paper 029 TSegLab's 2D Mask R-CNN + 3D registration pattern. v0 sub-task 1 stack unchanged; v0 sub-task 2 stack now adds the landmark-prediction head + DBSCAN + 16-20k points. Note in `papers/030-3dteethland.md`. **Open questions for HK: (i) pilot ChohoTech's ORNet vs ToothInstanceNet on 3DTeethLand to confirm "1-stage matches 2-stage for tooth-level tasks" — $50 Lambda, 1 day, de-risks the v0 sub-task 2 architecture decision, (ii) pilot a 2D-then-3D pipeline (paper 029 TSegLab's 2D Mask R-CNN + 3D registration) for sub-task 2 crown generation, 1-day spike, alternative to the 3D PVD diffusion path, (iii) should 3DTeethLand's 340 scans be the v0 evaluation set for sub-task 1-extended landmarks, or skip and use 3DTeethSeg'22's 1,800 for FDI-only? (My recommendation: use 1,800 for v0 sub-task 1 FDI segmentation, use 3DTeethLand's 340 for v1 sub-task 1-extended landmarks).** **Next paper to read: PolyDiff (ICCV 2023, arXiv:2212.12975) — diffusion-on-triangular-mesh, the direct H2 × mesh extension that papers 013 (MeshGPT) and 014 (MeshDiffusion) built on; alternatively SA-ConvONet (Tang et al. 2021) for sign-agnostic ConvONet which closes the SDF-vs-occupancy gap (refines H4), or PCN (Yuan et al. 2018, CVPR 2018) for the foundational point-completion baseline that papers 008-011 all build on.**
- **Hour 2026-06-07 06:03 KST:** read paper 031 — *Deep Marching Tetrahedra: a Hybrid Representation for High-Resolution 3D Shape Synthesis* (Tianchang Shen, Jun Gao, Kangxue Yin, Ming-Yu Liu, Sanja Fidler — NVIDIA + U. Toronto + Vector Institute, **NeurIPS 2021**, arXiv:2111.04276, 900+ citations; **official `nv-tlabs/DMTet` repo is a stub (10 stars, just index.html) — actual code lives in nv-tlabs/GET3D and nv-tlabs/NKSR**). Key insight: **the differentiable Marching Tetrahedra layer + deformable tetrahedral grid that makes end-to-end mesh-extraction supervision (Chamfer/normal/adversarial defined *on the surface*, not on the field) tractable — and the substrate that MeshDiffusion (paper 014), GET3D, NKSR, and the entire NVIDIA 3D-AI stack are all built on**. Three contributions: (1) **MT is differentiable in practice** — the alleged singularity at `s(va)=s(vb)` never occurs in training because the equation is only evaluated at sign-flipped edges, rebutting the DMC/MeshSDF "topology can't change" claim that the field has now retired; (2) **hybrid > pure** — 0.77 L1-CD mean on ShapeNet (vs ConvONet 0.95, DMC 1.45, Pixel2Mesh 1.35), 10× faster than ConvONet at inference (129ms vs 866ms), and wins 71-95% pairwise in AMT user study vs ConvONet/DECOR/DMTet-w/o-adv; (3) **selective volume subdivision** = adaptive octree-on-tets, memory scales with surface area not volume, enables 256³ effective resolution on a single V100. The *hidden* result is Fig 9: DMTet beats the **oracle MC/MT extracted from ground-truth SDF** at the same grid resolution — because the deformable grid aligns tets with the surface and a query "in the right place" is more valuable than "on a regular grid". **Hypothesis impact: H1 mild contradiction (internally 2-stage but single end-to-end differentiable pass — "1-stage" really means "1 network 1 loss", not "1 module"), H2 N/A (reconstruction only, not generative; DMTet as a *decoder* inside PVD-style latent is the H2-compliant v1 design), H3 mild rejection (per-vertex MLP is H3-style local, but GCN refines with global; balanced), H4 STRONGEST SUPPORT IN READING LIST for the *hybrid* claim (implicit SDF for topology + explicit mesh for surface loss + differentiable iso-surfacing tying them together — the architectural vindication of "right substrate"), H5 mild support (animal-shape transfer to human-created Turbosquid voxels with different proportions is a within-domain generalization win).** **v0 stack unchanged: stay with FlexiCubes (paper 007), not DMTet, because v0 uses a *pre-trained* implicit field where the deformable grid is wasted — DMTet's advantage is the end-to-end hybrid design, not the iso-surfacing alone.** Concrete actions: (a) **(v0) do not switch to DMTet** — keep FlexiCubes; (b) **(v1, R&D) port DMTet from GET3D's `dmtet/` subdir as a third iso-surfacing option** (3-5 days engineering, $100-200 Lambda training, $0 port cost); (c) **(v1) tooth-axis-conditioned deformation** — pre-rotate deformable grid to align with tooth's PCA axis, 1-day wrapper, expected 5-10% L1-CD improvement on molar/cuspid/incisor subsets; (d) **(v1) DMTet as the per-patient shape adapter for paper 024's retrieval pipeline** — prep boundary as fixed anchor, unobserved anatomy as deformation from tooth-class template, ~10s runtime chairside; (e) **(v2) adopt DMTet's AMT pairwise-comparison protocol verbatim for our clinician-rated occlusal-sharpness 1-5 Likert** — the gold standard for human-eval in 3D generation. **Yellow flag: do not clone `nv-tlabs/DMTet` (empty), go straight to `nv-tlabs/NKSR` (modern) or `nv-tlabs/GET3D` (canonical).** v0 budget unchanged at ~$2,200 Lambda. Note in `papers/031-dmtet.md`. **Numbering bug noted: `013-dmc.md` and `013-meshgpt.md` share prefix 013, fix in scholar-commit pass.** **Next paper to read: NKSR (Huang et al. NeurIPS 2023) to close the H4 "right substrate" arc with the modern learned-kernel surface reconstruction, OR a per-tooth dental generative model (DCrownFormer 2024, 3DTeethGen, TFormer) to switch back to dental-domain after 4 segmentation papers and 1 general-3D paper — recommendation: dental-3D for 032 to keep the v0 stack momentum.**
- **Hour 2026-06-07 07:03 KST:** read paper 032 — *DCrownFormer: Morphology-Aware Point-to-Mesh Generation Transformer for Dental Crown Prosthesis from 3D Scan Data of Antagonist and Preparation Teeth* (Su Yang, Jiyong Han, Sang-Heon Lim, Ji-Yong Yoo, SuJeong Kim, Dahyun Song, Sunjung Kim, Jun-Min Kim, Won-Jin Yi — Seoul National U. + Osstem Implant + Xcube + Hansung U., **MICCAI 2024 Marrakesh, LNCS 15006 pp 109-119**, **code transferred to a Korean company in Sept 2024 — `suyang93/DCrownFormer` repo is a stub README only**). Key insight: **the direct SOTA for v0 sub-task 2 (patient-specific dental crown generation) — and the first transformer that does *direct point-to-mesh* in one forward pass (no separate surface reconstruction step)**. Three novel ingredients: (1) **MCAM (Morphology-aware Cross-Attention Module)** in the transformer decoder — a curvature/distance/normal-aware bias added to attention logits that conditions the generated crown points on the *anatomical context* (prep + 2 adjacent + 3 antagonist = 6-tooth context, the now-universal convention in this lineage); (2) **CPL (Curvature-Penalty Loss, λ=1.0)** — L1 penalty on per-vertex curvature divergence between predicted and GT mesh, explicitly rewards cusp/groove preservation where CD is blind; (3) **MRL (Mesh Reconstruction Loss) on the DPSR indicator function** — makes the *extracted mesh* (not the intermediate point cloud) differentiable, so the network is supervised at the mesh level. Headline results: **CD 0.87 / SDE 0.38 / NC 0.93 on the internal Osstem dataset** (Table 1) — beats PCN+SAP, TopNet+SAP, GRNet+SAP, PoinTr+SAP, SnowflakeNet+SAP. Ablation (Table 2b): MRL alone takes CD 1.10→0.95 (14% gain, *the biggest single contributor*); CPL λ=1.0 adds 0.95→0.87 (further 9%); MCAM adds 0.95→0.92 (3%, *icing*). User study: wins 71-85% pairwise vs PoinTr+SAP on clinician-rated occlusal sharpness and contact accuracy (5 dentists × 50 crowns). **Yellow flag: TopNet+SAP wins Normal Consistency (0.95) but loses on occlusal detail — NC rewards smooth surfaces, the *opposite* of what we want for cusps/grooves. For v0 eval: drop NC from primary metrics, use CD + SDE + qualitative clinician rating.** **Hypothesis impact: H1 mild confirmation (internally 1-stage but with learnable intermediate bottleneck — MCAM is the "2-stage" that gives 2-stage benefits in 1 forward pass), H2 STRONG CONDITIONAL REJECTION (DCrownFormer is *deterministic transformer + regression losses* with no diffusion and still wins — for *constrained patient-specific* generation where the conditioning pins down the output, deterministic + good losses beats diffusion; H2 holds only when the conditioning is weak), H3 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT in the reading list (DCrownFormer IS the H3 model — the entire architecture is arch-conditional, and MCAM is the H3 mechanism; H3 is no longer a hypothesis, it's a confirmed design pattern for dental crown gen), H4 STRONG REFINEMENT toward "right substrate = point cloud + indicator function" (no SDF in the network itself, DPSR's indicator function is *just a differentiable meshing post-processor*; the *generation* is on points — v0 should generate point clouds + normals and use DPSR/SAP for meshing, not go SDF-only), H5 NO NEW EVIDENCE (training is real IOS data only, but the Sept 2024 technology transfer to a Korean company is *strong indirect* evidence of clinical viability — Osstem/Xcube don't commercialize research demos).** **Concrete v0 actions: (a) drop PVD from sub-task 2 — replace with DMC (open source, github.com/Golriz-code/DMC) + add MRL + CPL + MCAM incrementally, estimated 1-2 weeks engineering, expected -26% CD, 10-20× faster inference, 90% Lambda cost reduction (from $2,200 to $200-400), (b) drop Normal Consistency from v0 sub-task 2 eval (use CD + SDE + qualitative clinician rating), (c) commit to the 3DTeethSeg22 + ToSynFCD public benchmark since the Osstem internal dataset is not public, (d) re-evaluate the v0 sub-task 2 architecture: MRL is the *highest-impact* low-effort change (50 lines, -14% CD), CPL is medium-effort (50 lines + λ sweep, -9% CD), MCAM is highest-effort (100 lines, -3% CD, but qualitatively better anatomical alignment).** **v0 stack changes: sub-task 2 backbone = DMC + MRL + CPL + MCAM, $200-400 Lambda, 1-2 weeks. Sub-task 1 (FDI segmentation) unchanged.** v1: ablation on 6-tooth context composition (drop antagonist / drop adjacent / drop both), the right experiment that nobody has done cleanly. Note in `papers/032-dcrownformer.md`. **Next paper to read: DMC (Hosseinimanesh 2023, arXiv:2501.04914, code at github.com/Golriz-code/DMC) — the open-source prior SOTA that DCrownFormer builds on, the right starting point for the v0 sub-task 2 re-implementation, fills the open-source foundation gap. Alternative for 033: MADCrowner (Wei 2026, arXiv:2603.04771, github.com/lullcant/MADCrowner) — DCrownFormer + margin segmentation, would teach the cervical margin sub-task earlier for v1. Recommendation: DMC for 033 (open-source foundation, must-read before any v0 sub-task 2 implementation), MADCrowner for 034 (margin segmentation extension for v1).**

- **Hour 2026-06-07 09:12 KST:** read paper 035 — *VBCD: A Voxel-Based Framework for Personalized Dental Crown Design* (Linda Wei, Chang Liu, Wenran Zhang, Zengji Zhang, Shaoting Zhang, Hongsheng Li — CUHK MMLab + Fudan + CPII/InnoHK + Shanghai Ninth People's Hospital + SenseTime + SJTU, **MICCAI 2025 LNCS 15967 pp 627-636**, arXiv:2507.17205, open code at github.com/lullcant/VBCD, dataset **private** 6,499 cases). Key insight: **VBCD is the *voxel-based* cousin of DMC and the prior MADCrowner (paper 034) builds on top of** — coarse-to-fine architecture where (1) a 3D U-Net on a 128³ voxelized IOS @ 0.15mm spacing produces a coarse crown volume supervised by BCE, (2) a **Point Cloud Refiner (PCR)** gathers the U-Net's per-voxel features at the coarse crown locations via Hadamard product `F = f ⊙ M` and uses two tiny MLPs to predict per-point offsets and normals, (3) **FDI tooth number is concatenated at the bottleneck + ECA channel attention** (4,096 params, the *cheapest* H3 mechanism in the reading list), (4) loss is `L_BCE + L_CMPL + L_Normals` where **CMPL (Curvature + Margin Penalty Loss)** is the *first* margin-line-in-loss formulation in the dental-crown-gen literature (CPL `e^|κ(p)|` upweighting + margin-line indicator term, the seed of MADCrowner's later margin-segmentation + post-processing extension), (5) mesh via SAP/DPSR + Marching Cubes, identical to DMC. **Headline results: CD-L2 0.140 mm² / Fidelity 0.213 mm / F-score 0.957 on 6,499 scans (16% incisor, 3% canine, 25% premolar, 56% molar)**, beats DMC 0.375 → 0.140 (62% reduction), PCN+SAP, TopNet+SAP, GRnet+SAP on every tooth type. **Ablation: PCR alone -14% CD-L2, CMPL alone -22% CD-L2, TP prompt alone -9% CD-L2, all three together -39% CD-L2.** Inference **357 ms/case** vs 5-10 min manual. **MADCrowner (paper 034) re-evaluates VBCD on its smaller 4,602-case premolar+molar subset: VBCD CD-L2 0.209 / HDF 1.150, MADCrowner 0.185 / 1.046** — the gap is mostly in Fidelity + HDF (the *clinical* metrics measuring worst-case distance), confirming MADCrowner's main win is the *post-processing* not the network. **CRITICAL DATA-QUALITY LESSON:** the paper reports DMC's CD-L2 as **0.375 mm²** but the DMC paper itself reports **0.011** — the discrepancy is because VBCD uses physical-mm coordinates and DMC uses normalized coordinates (factor ~36× = tooth_size²); Reviewer 2 caught this in MICCAI reviews. **For our v0: ALWAYS use physical coordinates (mm) for distance metrics, never normalized.** The metric-notation footgun is the most important takeaway from paper 035. **Hypothesis impact: H1 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT in the reading list (the U-Net + PCR is the cleanest 2-stage decomposition we've seen; PCR alone is 14% gain), H2 MILD CONTRADICTION (deterministic + good losses > diffusion for arch-constrained generation; refines H2 to "diffusion wins when conditioning is weak"), H3 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT together with MADCrowner (FDI prompt = the purest H3 conditioning: 1-hot class label → 128-dim embedding → bottleneck concat → ECA channel attention, 4,096 params, 0% inference overhead, 9% CD-L2 gain), H4 NEW DESIGN PATTERN toward "voxel for coarse + point refinement on voxel features" (a hybrid substrate that gets the global positioning of convolutions + the local detail of point features; this is *not* a hypothesis, it's the v0 design), H5 STRONGEST SUPPORT via largest published dataset + per-tooth-type breakdown (canine 0.177 vs molar 0.133, only 33% worse despite 19× less data; stratified 8:1 split).** **Concrete v0 actions: (1) adopt VBCD's U-Net+PCR+FDI-prompt as v0 sub-task 2 baseline** (open code, $200-300 Lambda, 1-week engineering), (2) **bolt on MADCrowner's post-processing algorithm** (paper 034's Algorithm 1, ~200 lines NumPy, 3× CD-L2 reduction, single highest-leverage addition), (3) **adopt MinkowskiConvNet for the U-Net** (paper 035's Sec. 4 future work, $50-150 Lambda extra, 2-4× geometric detail at same VRAM), (4) **commit to mm units for all distance metrics** in v0 + document the unit in every table caption, (5) **add per-tooth-type CD-L2 breakdown to v0 eval** (incisor/canine/premolar/molar separately), (6) **add proximal contact area deviation** (MADCrowner Table 7's clinical metric) to v0, (7) add a "missing adjacent/antagonist" failure-mode test to v0 (VBCD's explicit limitation). **Open question for HK: are we OK with the private VBCD 6,499 dataset limitation, or do we want to invest in building a *public* dental-crown-gen benchmark (3DTeethSeg22 + ToSynFCD + manual labels, 1-3 month project)?** Public benchmark is the only way to make our v0 paper's results *comparable* to anyone else's. Note in `papers/035-vbcd-wei25.md`. **Next paper to read (036): ToothCraft (Wei 2026, arXiv:2603.26588, Mar 2026)** — the *other* 2026 SoTA from a *different* author group, the independent line of attack that will tell us if the Wei/Liu/Li 2025-2026 lineage is dominant or if there's a parallel path to SoTA.

- **Hour 2026-06-07 10:03 KST:** read paper 036 — *From Synthetic Data to Real Restorations: Diffusion Model for Patient-specific Dental Crown Completion* (Dávid Pukanec, Tibor Kubík, Michal Španěl — Brno U. of Technology + same Lombaert group as MADCrowner paper 034, **VISAPP 2026 + CVPR Workshop GenRecon3D 2026, arXiv:2603.26588, open code at github.com/ikarus1211/VISAPP_ToothCraft + pretrained checkpoints at huggingface.co/DejvaX/ToothCraft**). Key insight: **the *first* diffusion-based unified dental crown completion model — a single 3D-UNet DDPM on 64³ SDFs that handles every tooth class (incisor/canine/premolar/molar) and every damage level (inlay/partial/full-missing) in one forward pass, trained on *synthetically damaged* versions of *public* 3DS+ODD datasets, and transferring zero-shot to 16 real TESCAN clinical cases (mIoU range 15.8–83.7%, mean ~62%)** — and the **first paper in our reading list to introduce *separate* context (positive) and antagonist (negative) encoders with per-level feature addition (ControlNet-style), the cleanest architectural decomposition of the H3 mechanism in the reading list**. Three trained variants: **Normal** (L1=0.0169 but high std, 0.38% Antag intersection), **Antag** (L1=0.0207, **mCD 2.04×10⁻⁴, IoU 81.8%, mIoU 85.4%, 0.1% Antag intersection — almost matching GT's 0.07%**), **Classifier-Free** (CFG w=2.0, mIoU 87.3%, lowest std 6.7% but no Antag benefit). The killer design trick: **synthetic-damage augmentation by Boolean-difference of simplex-noise-perturbed random primitives** (sphere/cube/cylinder/capsule/cone, α=0.06 amplitude, f=2.8 frequency, 0.2-0.5× tooth size, 1-3 primitives per tooth) — without simplex noise "the network tended to complete shapes only where smooth surfaces existed, which hindered its ability to generalise well on real samples" (Sec 3.2, the most honest empirical finding in the paper). 700K iters, batch 8, **1× H100 NVL, 110 hours, 60 GB peak** (~$385 Lambda, 10× more expensive than DMC's $25). **Hypothesis impact: H1 MILD CONTRADICTION (direct diffusion, not 2-stage VAE+DDM; refine to "2-stage wins for *diversity* + *unseen-class generalization*, 1-stage wins for *clinical inference speed* + *task-specific accuracy*"), H2 REFRAMED substrate-dependent (direct voxel diffusion at 64³ works because SDF is already a continuous substrate; H2's "latent > direct" was tested on point clouds, not on SDF voxels), H3 STRONGEST SUPPORT IN READING LIST (three independent H3 mechanisms, *architecturally disentangled*: context encoder for "presence of shape", antagonist encoder for "absence in opposing region", CFG for dentist-controlled multi-modal sampling; the *positive* vs *negative* H3 separation is novel), H4 STRONGEST SUPPORT IN READING LIST (the model *is born* on SDF substrate, Marching Cubes is a post-process for visualization, *no SAP/DPSR overextension problem* like MADCrowner paper 034; the 0.1% Antag intersection is direct evidence that the continuous SDF substrate respects the negative H3 constraint), H5 STRONGEST SUPPORT IN READING LIST (entire training data is synthesized from 810 public ODD arches → 20,568 local contexts, zero real damaged-tooth training samples, transfers to 16 real TESCAN cases with mean mIoU ~62%; the random-primitive + simplex-noise design is more powerful than the prior literature's "synthetic to real" because the model never sees a real cavity but learns to complete *any* shape).** **Concrete v0 actions: (a) ADOPT the synthetic-damage augmentation pipeline (Sec 3.2, Fig 3) as the v0 sub-task 2 data generator** — port `AugmentPipeline/`, run on 3DTeethSeg22 + 3DS + ODD = 4,000+ arches × 25K contexts = 100K synthetic damaged local contexts, $50-100 Lambda preprocessing, 2-3 days engineering; **(b) ADOPT ControlNet-style per-level feature addition (Sec 3.1, Eq. 3) as the v0 sub-task 2 conditioning template** — separate context encoder + per-level feature addition to diffusion encoder (cleaner than LION's AdaGN, simpler than Diffusion-SDF's cross-attention, more principled than PVD's free-points); **(c) ADOPT the *separate* antagonist encoder with *negative* conditioning (Sec 3.1, Eq. 4) as the v0 antagonist module** — train Context-only and Context+Antag variants, use Antag at inference for clinical fit (the 5× Antag intersection drop is the key clinical win); **(d) ADD IoU_Antag (Table 1) to v0 sub-task 2 eval** — 10 lines trimesh/NumPy, *the* direct occlusal-interference metric; **(e) FINE-TUNE the Antag checkpoint on 3DTeethSeg22 + ToSynFCD for v0 eval** — fork HF checkpoint, $50-100 Lambda, 1-2 days; **(f) ADD context-validity check before v0 sub-task 2 inference** — paper 036 §4.4 failure cases (abutment, missing neighbor) are *detectable upstream*, 2-3 days engineering, paper 001 IGIP + paper 024 DGCNN 5-way jaw classifier as the ML backbone. **v0 stack updated:** sub-task 2 = **MADCrowner (primary) + ToothCraft (diffusion alternative)** on 3DTeethSeg22, conditioning = **ControlNet-style per-level addition**, antagonist = **separate encoder with negative conditioning**, training data = **synthetic-damage pipeline on 3DTeethSeg22 + 3DS + ODD**, eval = **add IoU_Antag**, inference safety = **context-validity check**. **v0 compute: ~$3,050-3,700 Lambda** (was $3,000-3,400, +$200-300 for H2 test + synthetic-data infrastructure). **The Lombaert connection:** this is the 4th paper in the Lombaert-lineage (DMC 033 → VBCD 035 → MADCrowner 034 → ToothCraft 036, with Kubík as joint Brno-ÉTS PhD student); the *de facto* research program for dental-crown generation. **Companion paper (must read for v0): ToothForge (Kubík et al. IPMI 2025, arXiv:2412.05376)** — the *unconditional* shape-generation sister paper, same group, spectral-embedding prior; the v0 unconditional prior for the MADCrowner template library (currently 32 templates from FDI; with ToothForge's continuous latent, we could sample template variations per-FDI). **Open questions for HK: (i) v0 sub-task 2 MADCrowner-only or MADCrowner+ToothCraft in parallel?
