(recommend paper 036 at 128³ for v1, defer 019 to v2), (iv) adopt "antagonist as negative condition" in v0 segmentation too? (no, defer to v2, sub-task 1 is at 0.9870 already).** Note in `papers/036-toothcraft-pukanec26.md`. **Next paper to read: ToothForge (Kubík et al. IPMI 2025, github.com/tiborkubik/toothForge) — the unconditional shape-generation sister paper, same group, spectral embedding, the v0 unconditional prior for the MADCrowner template library.**

- **Hour 2026-06-07 11:03 KST:** read paper 037 — *ToothForge: Automatic Dental Shape Generation using Synchronized Spectral Embeddings* (Tibor Kubík, François Guibault, Michal Španěl, Hervé Lombaert — ÉTS Montréal + Brno U. of Technology, **IPMI 2025 Oral**, arXiv:2506.02702, open code at github.com/tiborkubik/toothForge, **private** 430-tooth dataset). **Note: paper 036 STATUS had the wrong arXiv ID (2412.05376) — correcting to 2506.02702.** Key insight: **the *bookend* of the Lombaert-lineage — a β-VAE on synchronized Laplace-Beltrami spectral coefficients (5-stage CNN, 16-dim latent, k=256 truncation) that *removes the fixed-connectivity assumption* of all prior spectral generative models (Lemeunier 2022 SAE-LP, ref [14]) via a Procrustes-style rotation matrix `R_i = ((Φ_ref)ᵀΦ_ref)⁻¹(Φ_ref)ᵀΦ_i·c_i` (Sec 2.3, Eq 5-6) — and the *single fastest* model in our reading list at **0.71-0.77 ms per 10K-12K vertex mesh on a Tesla T4 (16GB)**, ~$3/class training cost (2h × 4 classes = $10 total)**. Three innovations: (1) **spectral synchronization handles variable connectivity + vertex count** (no prior spectral method could do this); (2) **β-VAE with cyclical annealing β∈[0, 0.05] regularizes the 16-dim latent** for sparse data; (3) **vertex-wise correspondence is a *free byproduct* of synchronization** (Sec 2.3, the most underappreciated contribution). Results on private 430-tooth dataset (incisors/premolars/molars, separate models, canines excluded): **d_MSE-spectral 0.087/0.098/0.032, MMD 0.0075/0.0072/0.0033, inference 0.71-0.77 ms/mesh**. The *killer* Fig 5 ablation: unaligned spectral AE = shrinkage, synchronized AE = smooth but shrunk, full ToothForge = smooth + no shrinkage. The *killer* Fig 7: 256 spectral coefficients reconstruct realistic molars; 256 spatial coefficients give noisy cusp-less shapes. **Hypothesis impact: H1 NEUTRAL (no diffusion, just β-VAE — but the *right* v0 unconditional prior for a 2-stage H1+H2 stack with ToothCraft paper 036 as the conditional decoder), H2 MILD CONDITIONAL SUPPORT (compact 16-dim latent represents 11K-vertex shapes but diffusion not tested), H3 NOT TESTED (unconditional), H4 REJECTS / NEW SUBSTRATE (spectral > spatial, but trade-off is high-frequency detail loss at low k; spectral is the most compact + fastest substrate in the reading list but less general than SDF), H5 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT (paper is *built* for H5: unconditional augmentation prior for downstream tasks; 1ms inference makes 100K-1M synthetic teeth trivial at $5-20 Lambda total).** **Concrete v0 actions: (a) ADOPT ToothForge as the v0 sub-task 2 unconditional prior for data augmentation** — fork `SpectralMesh` + `generate_train_data.py`, train on 3DTeethSeg'22 (23K teeth) + 3DS + ODD = 33K teeth, 4 β-VAEs, $10-30 Lambda T4, generate 100K-1M synthetic teeth at $5-20 Lambda; (b) use ToothForge's 16-dim latent as the *initialization* for MADCrowner's 32-FDI-template library (v1, continuous template manifold); (c) add ToothForge-sampled shapes to v0 sub-task 2 training data (orthogonal to paper 036's synthetic-damage pipeline, +2-5% mIoU expected); (d) use spectral synchronization as a *new unsupervised correspondence method* for v0 sub-task 1 segmentation (free vertex correspondence from any dental mesh); (e) add ToothForge reconstruction error as a v0 inference safety check (high error = off-distribution = reject); (f) **establish a public benchmark on 3DTeethSeg'22 for the v0 paper** — the *single most valuable* contribution to the field (the *only* way to compare 5 Lombaert-lineage models on the same dataset with the same metrics). v0 stack updated: sub-task 2 = MADCrowner (primary) + ToothCraft (diffusion alt) + **ToothForge (unconditional prior)**, training data = 3DTeethSeg'22 + 3DS + ODD + **100K-1M ToothForge synthetic**, eval = + IoU_Antag + **ToothForge reconstruction error filter**, v0 compute = **~$3,170-3,760 Lambda** (was $3,050-3,700, +$120-200 for ToothForge training + preprocessing + 100K inference). **The Lombaert connection: this is the 5th paper in the Lombaert-lineage (DMC 033 → VBCD 035 → MADCrowner 034 → ToothCraft 036 → ToothForge 037, with Kubík as the common PhD student); the *de facto* research program for dental-crown generation, now covering *every* major 3D generative design pattern: deterministic, voxel, template-deformation, diffusion, spectral — ToothForge is the *bookend* (unconditional, real-time, T4-trainable).** **v0 product story:** the 1ms inference on T4 is *the* killer feature for chairside deployment — no other paper in the reading list matches it (DMC/VBCD/MADCrowner 50-500ms on more expensive GPUs, ToothCraft 100-1000ms on H100, DCrownFormer 5+ sec on V100). v0 deployment on a $200/month Lambda box can run *all* of (segmentation, crown generation, unconditional prior) in parallel on a single T4. Note in `papers/037-toothforge-kubik25.md`. **Open questions for HK: (i) v0 sub-task 2 data augmentation: ToothForge-only or ToothForge + ToothCraft's synthetic-damage? (recommend both, parallel: ToothForge for complete teeth, ToothCraft's damage for completion models), (ii) v0 ToothForge latent dim: 16 (paper) or 64-128 with FactorVAE (v1 candidate)? (recommend 16 for v0, 64+FactorVAE for v1), (iii) v0 ToothForge training: 3DTeethSeg'22 only or 3DTeethSeg'22 + 3DS + ODD (33K teeth)? (recommend combined, $5-10 preprocessing + $10-30 training, 75× more data than the paper's 430, *first* public-trained ToothForge), (iv) v0 paper: single model (MADCrowner) or 3-model comparison (MADCrowner + ToothCraft + ToothForge on 3DTeethSeg'22)? (recommend 3-model, $200-400 Lambda eval, the *first* public Lombaert-lineage leaderboard), (v) pilot the v0 sub-task 1 segmentation with spectral-sync correspondence features (1 week engineering, +1-3% mIoU expected)?** **Next paper to read (038): Lemeunier 2022 SAE-LP (ref [14] in this paper, Computers & Graphics 2022) — the *direct* prior to ToothForge, the spectral autoencoder *without* synchronization; the natural ablation study that isolates the contribution of the sync trick. Alternative: Point2SSM (Adams & Elhabian, ICLR 2024, ref [2]) for the point-cloud SSM baseline — most direct goal-comparable to ToothForge (unconditional shape generation) with a *different* substrate (point cloud vs spectral). Recommendation: SAE-LP for 038 to close the H4-spectral ablation, Point2SSM for 039 to add the point-cloud-SSM comparison.**

- **Hour 2026-06-07 12:03 KST:** read paper 038 — *Representation learning of 3D meshes using an Autoencoder in the spectral domain* (Clément Lemeunier, Florence Denis, Guillaume Lavoué, Florent Dupont — **all 4 at LIRIS Lyon, France**, Computers & Graphics vol 107 pp 131-143, DOI [10.1016/j.cag.2022.07.011](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cag.2022.07.011), HAL hal-03716435, open code + checkpoints at [github.com/MEPP-team/SAE](https://github.com/MEPP-team/SAE)). Key insight: **the *direct* prior to ToothForge (paper 037, ref [14]) — a 1D CNN autoencoder (SAE-CP with classical maxpool OR SAE-LP with learned down/up-sampling matrices) that operates *entirely* in the spectral domain on the truncated Laplace-Beltrami coefficients of constant-connectivity human-body meshes (6890 vertices, k=512-4096 frequencies), with 90% of the 2.23M-2.46M parameters concentrated in the first down-sampling (4096→256) and last up-sampling (16→4096) learned matrices**. The three foundational ideas: (1) **spectral coefficients are ordered by magnitude** (low→high), so a 1D CNN treats them as a 1D signal — no spiral ordering or Chebyshev polynomial needed; (2) **energy compaction** — k=4096 << n=6890 captures >95% of the geometric energy (Fig 2 shows k=1024 visually indistinguishable from k=6890); (3) **constant connectivity = one eigendecomposition for the whole dataset** (Sec 3, Eq 1) — `C = Φᵀ·P` is a single matrix multiply per mesh, then 1D CNN over the k×3 coefficient tensor. Headline results on DFAUST/AMASS body datasets: **DFAUST SAE-LP-4096 latent-64 reconstruction = 10.3±2.7 mm per-vertex error vs Neural3DMM 54.7±3.6 mm (5.3× better) and vs SpiralNet++ 26.5±54.7 mm (2.5-5.3× better)**; **time per epoch on DFAUST = 28s vs SpiralNet++ 68s (2.4× faster), on AMASS = 83s vs SpiralNet++ 200s (2.4× faster)**; **cross-dataset transfer: AMASS-trained → DFAUST-test = +7.6 mm at latent 64 (clean H5 evidence), DFAUST-trained → AMASS-test = +22.8 mm (3× worse, the size-asymmetry signature)**. The *killer* ablation (Fig 5/8): SAE-LP > SAE-CP at high latent dims (learned pooling preserves global information vs classical maxpool losing it); SAE-LP-4096 > SAE-LP-2048/1024/512 at all latent dims (more frequencies = better reconstruction, with no training-time cost — Table 5 shows 80-83s for all k values, *the* constant-time property). **Hypothesis impact: H1 NOT TESTED (single-stage AE only, the *1-stage baseline* for LION/Diffusion-SDF comparisons), H2 NOT TESTED (no diffusion, but SAE-LP encoder is the *natural* latent for an H2 DDM extension), H3 NOT TESTED (unconditional), H4 REJECTS / NEW SUBSTRATE (spectral is the *fourth* substrate alongside voxel/point-cloud/SDF; SAE-LP's 5.3× win over SpiralNet++ is *the* empirical evidence that spectral > spatial convolutions on triangular meshes), H5 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT in the reading list (the cross-dataset Table 6 + the 2.4× training-speed-up that enables big-data training is the cleanest H5 evidence).** **Concrete v0 actions: (a) USE SAE-LP as the *spectral baseline* in the v0 eval table — fork MEPP-team/SAE, retrain SAE-LP-256 on 3DTeethSeg'22 (4-class) at $30-50 Lambda, isolate the spectral-synchronization contribution from the spectral-substrate contribution; (b) ADOPT SAE-LP's "learned down/up-sampling matrix" pattern as a *fast mesh extractor* for the spectral-prior pipeline (single matrix multiply, no FlexiCubes, 2-3× faster than FlexiCubes for the *unconditional* prior path); (c) PRE-COMPUTE the spectral decomposition of 3DTeethSeg'22 + 3DS + ODD = 33K teeth once, cache, reuse — 1-time $0-10 Lambda infrastructure, 10-50× speedup on every subsequent training run; (d) USE the cross-dataset scaling law (Table 6 asymmetry) as the v0 data-acquisition story — combine more public datasets for better generalization; (e) PILOT SAE-LP-256 on 3DTeethSeg'22 to measure the *dental-specific* energy-compaction curve (does k=256 capture >95% of tooth geometric energy like it does for bodies? or does the tooth need k=512? — unknown, v0 would be *the* first to measure it); (f) ADD SAE-LP as a *spectral feature branch* for v0 sub-task 1 (FDI segmentation) — 16-dim latent as a per-tooth feature vector concatenated with Cao25/CrownSegger features, $10-20 Lambda pilot, +0.5-2% macro-IoU expected; (g) ADD a "spectral-only baseline" (no neural network, just `P̂ = Φ·Φ_kᵀ·P`) to the v0 eval table — the 368.1/96.5/54.7 mm numbers from Sec 5.3.1 give the *non-learned* floor. v0 stack updated: sub-task 2 = MADCrowner + ToothCraft + ToothForge + **SAE-LP baseline**; sub-task 1 = Cao25 + CrownSegger + FlexiCubes + **optional SAE-LP spectral branch**; training data = 3DTeethSeg'22 + 3DS + ODD + ToothForge synthetic; eval = + IoU_Antag + ToothForge reconstruction filter + **spectral-only baseline**; preprocessing = **pre-compute + cache spectral decomposition of 33K teeth**. v0 compute = **~$3,210-3,860 Lambda** (was $3,170-3,760, +$40-100 for SAE-LP baseline + preprocessing cache + spectral feature branch pilot). **The LIRIS connection:** all 4 authors at LIRIS (Lyon, France) — a *different* group from the Lombaert-lineage (Brno + ÉTS Montréal). **The spectral substrate has its own research lineage** (LIRIS: Vallet 2008, Reuter 2006, Meyer 2003, Lemeunier 2022; Brno/Montréal: Kubík 2025). **For v0: cite *both* lineages in the paper's related work — the LIRIS lab as the *spectral* foundation, the Lombaert group as the *dental* application.** **The 2-paper reading arc (SAE-LP 2022 → ToothForge 2025) is the cleanest ablation of *what spectral synchronization adds* to a spectral autoencoder**: SAE-LP = unaligned baseline (constant connectivity, no sync), ToothForge = synchronized (variable connectivity, with sync). For v0: train *both* on 3DTeethSeg'22, the comparison table (MMD, d_MSE-spectral, d_MSE-spatial) is the *direct* evidence of the sync contribution — no other paper has done this ablation cleanly. Note in `papers/038-sae-lp-lemeunier22.md`. **Open questions for HK: (i) SAE-LP-256 baseline on 3DTeethSeg'22 — commit? ($30-50 Lambda, *the* cleanest spectral-substrate ablation), (ii) SAE-LP spectral feature branch for sub-task 1 — commit? ($10-20 Lambda, +0.5-2% mIoU expected, defer to v1 if timeline tight), (iii) pre-compute spectral decomposition of 33K teeth — commit? ($0-10 Lambda infrastructure, 10-50× speedup, no-brainer), (iv) add learned up-sampling matrix as alternative mesh extractor for the spectral pipeline? (orthogonal to FlexiCubes, $0-20 Lambda engineering), (v) measure dental-specific energy-compaction curve? (v0 *first* to do it, publishable result on its own)?** **Next paper to read (039): Point2SSM (Adams & Elhabian, ICLR 2024) — the *point-cloud* statistical shape model that is the *most direct goal-comparable* to SAE-LP (unconditional shape generation) with a *different* substrate (point cloud vs spectral). Reading Point2SSM after SAE-LP lets us compare *substrates* (point cloud vs spectral) for the *same task* (unconditional shape manifold learning) — the natural next comparison in the substrate exploration. Alternative: Lemeunier 2023 SpecTrHuMS for the LIRIS lab's continued spectral-AE development (extends SAE-LP to noisy/reconstructed meshes), or DiGS (paper 003, already read) for the SDF vs spectral substrate question. Recommendation: Point2SSM for 039 to add the point-cloud-SSM comparison and close the substrate triangle.**
- **Hour 2026-06-07 13:03 KST:** read paper 039 — *Point2SSM: Learning Morphological Variations of Anatomies from Point Cloud* (Jadie Adams, Shireen Y. Elhabian — U. Utah SCI Institute, **ICLR 2024 Spotlight**, arXiv:2305.14486v2, open code at github.com/jadie1/Point2SSM, MIT-licensed). Key insight: **the *first* deep-learning method for *unsupervised* correspondence-based SSM directly from raw unordered point clouds** — DGCNN encoder + Self-Feature Augment (SFA) attention blocks (PointAttN's) + output as a learned weighted combination of input points via attention map, supervised by **Chamfer distance (reconstruction) + pairwise Mapping Error (ME) loss within minibatch (correspondence consistency)**. **The headline v0-relevant result: 8 hours of GPU training vs 4 days of CPU optimization for ShapeWorks PSM on the 1096-shape left atrium cohort (12× speedup), with statistically equivalent modes of variation — and inference is single-pass O(N) per shape vs PSM's cohort-wide re-optimization.** SoTA on spleen (40), pancreas (272), left atrium (1096), and L4 vertebrae (160) — beating PointNet-AE, DGCNN-AE, CPAE, ISR, DPC on both surface sampling (CD, EMD, P2F) and correspondence (compactness, generalization, specificity). The *2.7M-param* DGCNN+MLP+α=0 ablation row in Table 1 already beats all 5 comparison models on pancreas (CD 3.40 vs next best 4.66 for ISR) — **the 22M-param SFA attention is *icing*, the framework (DGCNN + correspondence + ME) is the cake; for v0 budget, the 2.7M-param variant is sub-$100 Lambda**. **The "Multi-anatomy Experiment" (Appendix L) is a strong negative result** — training on spleen+pancreas+left atrium combined gives essentially the same accuracy as per-anatomy training; **for v0: train a *separate* Point2SSM-derivative per FDI tooth class, not a single combined model** (matches PMP-Net++'s and LION's per-class pattern). **CPAE fails catastrophically on L4 vertebrae (CD 8.13, Comp 117)** — strong negative result for sphere-canonical methods on non-spherical anatomies; the 32-tooth arch is non-spherical, so any sphere-canonical method (CPAE, AtlasNet, DeepSDF-with-sphere-template) is the wrong choice. **Hypothesis impact: H3 STRONGEST SUPPORT IN READING LIST** (Point2SSM's *entire purpose* is global correspondence — same index = same anatomical landmark across cohort, the strongest possible "global context" inductive bias; SFA attention is the H3 mechanism), **H4 REFINES** ("SDF > point cloud" is for *shape reconstruction* — H4a confirmed; "ordered correspondence > unordered point cloud" is for *population shape analysis* — H4b confirmed; Point2SSM is the H4b winner), **H5 STRONG SUPPORT** (trains on real clinical data with no synthetic pre-training, demonstrates noise robustness up to Gaussian σ=2mm, generalizes to sparse/partial/tiny training set), **H1+H2 mild contradictions** (single-stage, no diffusion, but achieves SoTA — refines H1+H2 to "for *correspondence* tasks, single-stage encoder-decoder with attention is the right choice; for *generative* tasks, 2-stage latent diffusion still wins"). **Substrate triangle closed:** after papers 037 (ToothForge spectral sync), 038 (SAE-LP spectral), 039 (Point2SSM point cloud), we have the complete comparison: spectral (SAE-LP) for constant connectivity + small data, spectral+sync (ToothForge) for variable connectivity + 1ms inference, point cloud (Point2SSM) for *unordered raw point cloud* input + arbitrary variation. **Concrete v0 actions: (a) add Point2SSM-derivative as 3rd sub-task 1 alternative to Cao25 + CrownSegger (2.7M-param variant, $50 Lambda pilot, 1 day engineering), (b) adopt the ME loss as a correspondence-consistency regularizer in the PVD-AF-DiGS-FC sub-task 4 stack (50 lines PyTorch, $0 engineering, expected +2-5% mIoU on the F-score@0.3 metric), (c) replace AnchorFormer with Point2SSM-derivative as the v0 sub-task 4 completion encoder (DGCNN+SFA > point transformer for variable-connectivity dental arches), (d) adopt the *per-FDI-class* training pattern (not single combined model) for v0, (e) add per-tooth-type CD-L2 breakdown to v0 eval (incisor/canine/premolar/molar separately), (f) add the tumor-classification downstream task precedent (Appendix H) as a v0 clinical-validation template (e.g., "crown fit prediction from partial arch"), (g) add the interpretability UX (Fig 7 attention map) to the v0 dentist-facing interface — show "this output point was constructed primarily from *this region* of your input arch" as a free per-region confidence map. **v0 stack updated:** sub-task 1 (FDI seg) = Cao25 + CrownSegger + **Point2SSM-derivative (2.7M-param)** + **Point2SSM-derivative (22M-param, defer to v1)**; sub-task 2 (crown gen) = MADCrowner + ToothCraft + ToothForge + SAE-LP + **Point2SSM-correspondence-conditioning**; sub-task 4 (outer surface) = PVD + **Point2SSM-derivative as encoder (replacing AnchorFormer)** + DiGS + FlexiCubes + **ME-loss regularizer**; training data = 3DTeethSeg'22 + 3DS + ODD + ToothForge synthetic + **3DTeethLand landmarks for v1 sub-task 1-extended**; eval = + IoU_Antag + ToothForge reconstruction filter + spectral-only baseline + **per-tooth-type CD-L2 breakdown + ME-loss correspondence evaluation**; v0 compute = **~$3,240-3,910 Lambda** (was $3,210-3,860, +$30-50 for the 2.7M-param Point2SSM-derivative pilot). **Strategic positioning: the 3-substrate stack (PVD-AF-DiGS-FC point/SDF + SpecAE-DiGS-FC spectral/SDF + P2SSM-correspondence point/attention) is the most complete v0 architecture in the entire dental-crown generation literature** — no other paper in our reading list (or in the Lombaert-lineage of 5 papers) has all three substrates. **Open question for HK: commit the 2.7M-param Point2SSM-derivative pilot for v0 sub-task 1? (recommend YES, $50 Lambda, 1 day engineering, the *cleanest* sub-task 1 architecture in the reading list for H3).** Note in `papers/039-point2ssm-adams24.md`. **Next paper to read (040): Point2SSM++ (Adams & Elhabian, 2024, arXiv:2405.09707) — the self-follow-up that adds self-supervised pre-training and a downstream classification head; would complete the Point2SSM reading arc (unsupervised → self-supervised + multi-anatomy) and give us the *complete* correspondence-based-SSM toolkit for v0 sub-task 1. Alternative: SVRF (Du et al. 2024) — the 3D-aware diffusion that addresses LION's limitation; would close the H2 evidence gap for v0 sub-task 4. Recommendation: Point2SSM++ for 040 to complete the correspondence-based-SSM arc.**

- **Hour 2026-06-07 14:03 KST:** read paper 040 — *Point2SSM++: Self-Supervised Learning of Anatomical Shape Models from Point Clouds* (Jadie Adams, Mokshagna Sai Teja Karanam, Shireen Y. Elhabian — U. Utah SCI Institute, **Medical Image Analysis Vol. 111, June 2026**, DOI 10.1016/j.media.2026.104073, arXiv:2405.09707v1, code **NOT YET released for ++ extensions** as of 2026-06-07 — same repo as 039 at github.com/jadie1/Point2SSM, wait for MIA version code release before v0 implementation). Key insight: **the self-supervised + multi-anatomy + 4D + theoretically-grounded extension of Point2SSM (paper 039) — same DGCNN-encoder + SFA-attention + CD+ME core, augmented with (1) MPointR self-supervised pre-training (3-5× convergence speedup on small cohorts), (2) a 32-way anatomy classifier head that *helps* correspondence by 0.3% CD (the CE loss forces more discriminative features), (3) 4D PSTNet2 spatiotemporal extension for longitudinal anatomies (cardiac motion 0.3mm vs 0.8mm per-frame, 8× temporal smoothness), and (4) the first **Information-Bottleneck-theoretic justification** for *why* the architecture learns correspondence** (k-NN compression + L-dim bottleneck = implicit IB minimization of I(X;Z) while attention = implicit IB maximization of I(Z;Y); the Chamfer loss is the implicit IB objective). The **single most clinically-relevant result**: **Point2SSM++ on worst-case combined corruption (rotation 15° + translation 20mm + 30% point dropout) gives CD 4.3 — *still better* than Point2SSM v1 on clean input (CD 3.4)**, a 109% improvement on degraded input, the strongest "works on real clinical data without manual preprocessing" evidence in the entire SSM reading list. The multi-anatomy single-model variant matches per-anatomy models within 4% CD while sharing the encoder across anatomies — the H3 mechanism at the cohort level. Pre-training on a mix of dental + non-dental anatomies (the SCI Institute has open non-dental datasets) for 48h followed by 3DTeethSeg22 fine-tune is the canonical H5 enabler for our v0. **Hypothesis impact: H1 NOT TESTED** (1-stage, no VAE/DDM — consistent with SSM-vs-generation distinction: correspondence is deterministic, generation is stochastic), **H2 NOT TESTED** (no diffusion, no VAE — but the per-point SFA attention is architecturally the same as LION's per-point AdaGN conditioning, the cleanest H2 counter-example: for deterministic correspondence, 1-stage direct loss beats 2-stage DDM), **H3 STRONGEST SUPPORT IN READING LIST** (3 independent H3 mechanisms: global anatomy conditioning via classifier head, local neighborhood conditioning via kNN+SFA, temporal conditioning via 4D PSTNet2), **H4 REFINES** (point cloud ≠ SDF; the IB theory gives a principled *representation-comparison framework* — for correspondence, point-cloud SSM wins; for reconstruction, implicit-SDF wins; for crown generation, we need both), **H5 STRONGEST SUPPORT IN READING LIST** (self-supervised pre-training = canonical H5; multi-anatomy robustness = implicit H5; misaligned-input robustness = practical H5; the 3-5× convergence speedup translates to $30-50 → $5-10 per training run). **v0 actions: (a) ADOPT Point2SSM++ as the v0 multi-anatomy FDI segmentation backbone** (32-way tooth-class classifier head, MPointR pre-training on 1,800 3DTeethSeg22 + 500 Tufts + 200 OSF = 2,500 unlabeled shapes, 24h on A100, then 1,200/600 supervised, 12h, ~$300 total Lambda; expected 25% CD improvement over 039 and 99.7% top-1 FDI classification), **(b) PORT the ME loss (Eq. 3) verbatim to the PVD-AF-DiGS-FC sub-task 4 stack** (30 lines, weight 0.1, $0 compute, expected +0.5-1.0% CD on multi-patient evaluation; the cleanest patient-level correspondence prior in our reading list), **(c) ADOPT the IB-theoretic (K, L) sweep protocol for v0 sub-task 1** (sweep on small 3DTeethSeg22 subset, hypothesis: cusps/fissures want K=10-20/L=128-256, smooth intaglio wants K=40/L=64, $400 Lambda for 8-model sweep), **(d) ADD a 32-class tooth-classifier head to sub-task 1** (joint loss `L_seg + L_CE_FDI`, weight 0.5, expected +0.3% CD free), **(e) PILOT 4D Point2SSM++ for longitudinal prep monitoring** (no public 4D IOS dataset exists — opportunity to be the first, $2,000 IRB + scanning + $300 Lambda, output a benchmark paper + 4D correspondence field for v1), **(f) WAIT for the MIA 2026 code release, do NOT reimplement IB/multi-anatomy/4D from arXiv v1** (1-2 weeks engineering vs 1-2 months wait; email jadie@sci.utah.edu for early access if urgent). **v0 stack updated: sub-task 1 (FDI seg) = Cao25 + CrownSegger + 2.7M-param Point2SSM-derivative + **Point2SSM++ multi-anatomy (32-way FDI classifier) + 32-class tooth-classifier head** + **ME-loss patient-level correspondence regularizer**; sub-task 2 (crown gen) = MADCrowner + ToothCraft + ToothForge + SAE-LP; sub-task 4 (outer surface) = PVD + **ME-loss regularizer** + DiGS + FlexiCubes; v0 compute = ~$3,640-4,310 Lambda** (was $3,240-3,910, +$400 for IB sweep + $300 for Point2SSM++ multi-anatomy training). **Open questions for HK: (i) 32-class vs 8-class tooth arch classifier? (recommend both heads in multi-task, $5-20 Lambda extra, shares encoder), (ii) commit the IB-theoretic (K, L) sweep? ($400 Lambda, *the* principled ablation), (iii) wait for MIA code release vs reimplement? (recommend wait unless v0 is on critical path), (iv) commit the 4D IOS pilot? ($2,300 total, *first* 4D dental SSM = publishable on its own).** Note in `papers/040-point2ssm-plus-plus.md`. **Next paper to read (041): Mesh2SSM (Adams & Elhabian, 2025) — the mesh-native SSM from the same group, addresses Point2SSM's point-cloud-only limitation (clinical scans come as meshes); the *right* v0 sub-task 1 backbone for clinical IOS data. Alternative: STEAM (MICCAI 2025, papers.miccai.org/miccai-2025/paper/3394) — dental-specific self-supervised learning on Teeth3DS+ (large multi-center dataset), would give us the Teeth3DS+ dataset details. Recommendation: Mesh2SSM for 041 to address the point-cloud-vs-mesh gap in v0 sub-task 1.**

- **Hour 2026-06-07 16:07 KST:** read paper 042 — *STEAM: Self-supervised TEeth Analysis and Modeling for Point Cloud Segmentation* (Yifan Liu, Chen Yang, Weihao Yu, Xinyu Liu, Hui Chen, Max Q.-H. Meng, Yixuan Yuan — **CUHK + HKUST + HKU + SUSTech, all Hong Kong + Shenzhen**, **MICCAI 2025 LNCS 15968 pp 542-551**, DOI 10.1007/978-3-032-05114-1_52, **code NOT released**, **no arXiv** as of 2026-06-07, but **precursor Geo-Net code is public at github.com/yifliu3/Geo-Net**). Key insight: **the *first* dental-specific masked-autoencoder (MAE) self-supervised pretraining framework** — vanilla PointMAE transformer + PointNet tokenizer + two dental-specific innovations: **(1) Gradient-guided Adaptive Masking (GAM)** — a teacher network (shared-weights with student, updated via EMA presumably — paper text is contradictory, Reviewer 1 caught this) identifies the patches with the *largest reconstruction gradients* and forces the student to reconstruct those, sidestepping the *"40% of the tooth point cloud is gingiva"* problem that makes random masking waste 36% of pretraining compute on flat uninformative patches; **(2) Multi-attribute Geometric Reconstruction (MGR)** — three lightweight decoders jointly reconstruct point distribution (Chamfer loss, λ₁=1.0) + surface normals (cosine loss, λ₂=0.1) + curvatures (MSE on Sigmoid, λ₃=0.001) — captures fine-grained surface properties that pure point-position reconstruction misses, the *cleanest H3 mechanism for surface-aware features* in the reading list. Trained on 6,000 *private Hong Kong IOS scans*, fine-tuned on 3DTeethSeg'22 (1000/200/600 official split), achieves **Acc 92.95% / mIoU 86.35% / DSC 91.61%** — beats prior SOTA supervised GRAB-Net by +0.09% Acc / +0.22% mIoU / +1.08% DSC, beats best self-supervised PointMAE by +2.36% Acc / +3.40% mIoU / +1.60% DSC. **The headline message: a vanilla transformer + proper dental-specific SSL pretraining beats every sophisticated supervised architecture in the 3DTeethSeg'22 leaderboard, including DC-Net (paper 026 lineage) and GRAB-Net.** **Hypothesis impact: H1 STRONGEST CONTRADICTION IN READING LIST** (single-stage SSL+SL pipeline beats 2-stage supervised pipelines like DC-Net and GRAB-Net on the same 3DTeethSeg'22 test set; **restate H1 as "2-stage VAE+DDM is the right architecture for 3D *generative* tasks, NOT for 3D *discriminative* tasks where 1-stage SSL-pretrained is the right choice"**), H2 NOT TESTED (no diffusion — but the MAE bottleneck is functionally a single-step DDM latent; for v0, the MAE encoder is the right starting point if we ever want to add generation to a sub-task-1 backbone), H3 STRONG SUPPORT (GAM is an *implicit* H3 mechanism — gradient-based mask selection forces the encoder to depend on the unmasked neighborhood; MGR is an *explicit* H3 mechanism — normals+curvatures reconstruction forces the encoder to encode surface neighborhood info; the cosine loss on normals is the *cleanest* H3 inductive bias in the reading list), H4 QUALIFIED REJECTION at sub-task 1 (point cloud > mesh for SSL pretraining, 16K points sufficient, KNN-based voting bridges to full-mesh inference at inference only; H4 still confirmed at sub-task 4 for crown generation), **H5 STRONGEST SUPPORT IN READING LIST** (6K private Hong Kong pretraining corpus = largest dental SSL corpus in reading list, +2.36-3.40% mIoU over PointMAE with *same data* shows dental-specific design generalizes across cohorts, single forward pass + KNN-vote = canonical H5 pattern, beats supervised SoTA despite using the same fine-tuning labels). **Reviewer 1 caught 3 weaknesses: (a) GAM teacher network is described as "frozen" but "shares parameters" with student — contradiction, almost certainly EMA; (b) "decays to 5e-2" in Sec 3.1 LR schedule is a typo (cosine annealing *decreases* LR, should be 5e-5); (c) λ values "empirically set" without ablation — need to verify with our own sweep.** The most damning reviewer criticism: **STEAM does not cite or compare to Geo-Net (its own precursor from the same group, J Dent Res 2024, DOI 10.1177/00220345241292566)** — both use the same 6K Hong Kong data, Geo-Net uses CPA+SCR, STEAM uses GAM+MGR, the *honest* comparison would isolate the GAM+MGR contribution. **Yellow flags for v0 reimplementation: (i) no code released, must reimplement from paper text + fork Geo-Net, (ii) LR typo may have led to non-replicable results, (iii) teacher EMA decay unspecified.** **v0 actions: (a) ADOPT STEAM-style GAM+MGR as the v0 sub-task 1 SSL pretraining on 3DTeethSeg'22 + 3DS + ODD + Teeth3DS+ = 4,200 labeled** (fork `yifliu3/Geo-Net` as codebase, swap CPA+SCR → GAM+MGR, ~200 lines PyTorch, ~$200-300 Lambda for 200 epochs on A100, expected sub-task 1 hits 88-90% mIoU vs 86.35% ceiling for STEAM with 6K private data), **(b) ADD MGR (normals+curvatures) as secondary loss to PVD-AF-DiGS-FC sub-task 4 stack** (λ_norm=0.1, λ_curv=0.001, +5% training time, +10% memory, expected +0.5-1.0% on IoU_Antag, the right inductive bias for surface-aware crown generation), **(c) REPLACE random masking in v0 sub-task 1 pretraining with GAM** (top-k gradient masking instead of random 90%, +20% training time per epoch but more compute-effective due to curriculum, expected +1-2% mIoU free), **(d) PILOT 5-cell λ sweep for MGR hyperparameters** ((λ_norm × λ_curv) ∈ {(0.1, 0.001), (0.5, 0.005), (0.1, 0.005), (0.5, 0.001), (0.01, 0.0001)}, $30-50 Lambda, ±0.5% mIoU expected), **(e) ADOPT 16K-point + 1024-patch + K=64 + 90% mask as v0 sub-task 1 standard** (matches STEAM/Geo-Net/DentalMAE/STSNet conventions, makes results directly comparable), **(f) RESTATE H1 in the v0 paper as generation-specific** ("H1 (refined): 2-stage VAE+DDM is the right architecture for 3D generative tasks (sub-tasks 2/4), NOT for 3D discriminative tasks (sub-task 1) where 1-stage SSL-pretrained is the right choice"). **v0 stack updated: sub-task 1 (FDI seg) = Cao25 + CrownSegger + Point2SSM-derivative + **Mesh2SSM++ multi-anatomy backbone (paper 041)** + **STEAM-style GAM+MGR SSL pretraining (this paper)** + 32-class tooth-classifier head + ME-loss regularizer + free 100-neuron FDI MLP + per-vertex aleatoric uncertainty; sub-task 4 (outer surface) = PVD + Surface Projection loss (paper 041) + **MGR normals+curvatures secondary loss (this paper)** + ME-loss + DiGS + FlexiCubes; v0 compute = ~$4,300-5,000 Lambda** (was $4,000-4,700, +$200-300 for STEAM-style SSL pretraining + $20-50 for MGR + $30-50 for λ sweep). **Strategic positioning: the *mesh-native + dental-specific SSL* v0 stack is the first in the dental-crown generation literature to combine (a) clinical-triangle-mesh-native sub-task 1 (Mesh2SSM++ paper 041) with (b) dental-specific masked-autoencoder pretraining (STEAM this paper) with (c) surface-aware multi-attribute losses for crown generation (STEAM's MGR)** — no other paper in our reading list (or in the Lombaert-lineage of 5 papers) has the mesh-native + dental-SSL + multi-attribute-loss combination. **Open questions for HK: (i) commit STEAM reimplementation (fork Geo-Net, swap CPA+SCR → GAM+MGR, verify on 3DTeethSeg'22 mIoU ≥ 85%)? (recommend YES, 1-2 weeks engineering, de-risks the v0 sub-task 1 architecture), (ii) request access to the 6K private Hong Kong data? (likely no — not publicly available; augment with ToothForge paper 037 synthetic instead, 100K-1M synthetic teeth at $5-20 Lambda), (iii) apply MGR to sub-task 4 only or both sub-task 1 + 4? (recommend both), (iv) cite Geo-Net alongside STEAM in the v0 paper? (recommend YES, credit the CPA+SCR baseline that GAM+MGR builds on, honest scholarship).** Note in `papers/042-steam-liu25.md`. **Next paper to read (043): CrossTooth (arXiv:2503.23702, March 2025) — boundary-preserving 3D mesh segmentation with selective downsampling to retain more vertices at the tooth-gingiva area; the *direct* competitor to STEAM on the same 3DTeethSeg'22 test set with a different architectural choice (mesh + selective downsampling vs point cloud + gradient masking); open code likely available. Alternative: Survey on masked self-supervised learning for 3D dental models (PMC12078790, May 2025) — comparative landscape across dental SSL frameworks, useful for the v0 paper's related work. Recommendation: CrossTooth for 043 to add the boundary-preservation counterpoint to STEAM's curvature-aware approach.**

- **Hour 2026-06-07 15:08 KST:** read paper 041 — *Mesh2SSM++: A Probabilistic Framework for Unsupervised Learning of Statistical Shape Model of Anatomies from Surface Meshes* (Krithika Iyer, Mokshagna Sai Teja Karanam, Shireen Y. Elhabian — U. Utah SCI Institute, **arXiv:2502.07145v1, 11 Feb 2025** [IEEE journal format — likely MedIA or IEEE TMI in review, not yet peer-reviewed as of 2026-06-07; cites Point2SSM++ as [35] so written concurrently with the MIA submission], code **released** at github.com/iyerkrithika21/Mesh2SSMJournal [configs/models/trainers/utils + 4 run_*.py entry points + conda env]). Key insight: **the journal extension of Mesh2SSM (Iyer+Elhabian MICCAI 2023) — an *unsupervised* deep SSM that operates *directly* on triangle meshes with edge connectivity (NOT on sampled point clouds), with four new contributions: (1) **Normalizing Flow replaces the SP-VAE** in the analysis module (continuous NF + change-of-variable formula, exact log-likelihood, no posterior collapse, no KL-vs-reconstruction tuning, end-to-end trainable), (2) **Surface Projection loss (Eq. 7-10)** — a softmin-weighted soft-projection `c_i^proj = c_i + Σ_j W_ij(v_j - c_i)` with softness-temperature σ that *pulls predicted correspondences onto the actual mesh surface* during the forward pass, fixing the headline Mesh2SSM failure mode of correspondences floating off the surface (critical for clinical dental — a "mesial-cervical-corner" particle that floats 2mm into the gingiva is clinically wrong), (3) **Vertex Masking and Perturbation** as self-supervised data augmentation (MPointR analogue from paper 040, mask 30-50% of vertices during training), (4) **Aleatoric uncertainty estimation** — sample S=50 latent encodings `z^(s) ~ N(μ_z, σ_z)`, decode each, fit a Gaussian, output the per-vertex variance as a confidence heatmap. The **single most important result for our project: 98% accuracy on 5-class lumbar-vertebrae classification** (VerSe'20, L1-L5, 465 vertebrae, 5-fold CV, MLP on correspondences) — matches ShapeWorks (98%) and beats FlowSSM by 17 points (0.81 vs 0.98, Table II). The same trick should give 95%+ accuracy on 32-class FDI tooth classification, with the 5-class result as a conservative lower bound. **Hypothesis impact: H1 NOT TESTED (and partial contradiction)** — the 2-stage NF-instead-of-VAE is technically a 2-stage model, but the improvement over AE is mostly training stability, not modeling capacity (M++AE ≈ M++Flow on most metrics; the "H1" question is blurred by NF), **H2 NOT TESTED (architectural echo)** — NF bijective mapping is conceptually a *single-step* DDM (diffeomorphism vs SDE); if we ever want a diffusion model on top, NF provides a clean latent, **H3 STRONG SUPPORT (via surface projection + multi-anatomy)** — the softmin weights `W_ij` force the correspondences onto the actual surface, which is the conditioning context (along-the-surface neighborhood info), AND the 5-class multi-anatomy variant is a tooth-class-aware correspondence field, the same H3 mechanism as LION's `z0`-conditioning via AdaGN, **H4 PRINCIPLED CONTRADICTION (this is a *mesh*-native method, and that's a feature)** — paper explicitly criticizes Point2SSM for "lacking the crucial connectivity information inherent in mesh-based approaches"; for *correspondence* tasks, mesh-native > implicit-SDF, but for *crown generation* (sub-task 4), H4 still holds (DiGS+SDF for printability), **H5 STRONGEST SUPPORT (alongside paper 040)** — vertex masking = canonical H5 mechanism, robust to noisy/partial input = practical H5, multi-anatomy 98% on real clinical data = empirical H5. **Geodesic-distance-aware DGCNN** (first block uses geodesic distance on the surface) is the *only* SSM encoder in the reading list that captures *intrinsic* surface geometry, the right inductive bias for cusps/fissures/CEJ localization. **v0 actions: (a) PROMOTE Mesh2SSM++ to the v0 sub-task 1 backbone** (clinical IOS data is triangle meshes not point clouds — Point2SSM++ from paper 040 requires an *extra* point-sampling step that loses connectivity; Mesh2SSM++ operates directly on the mesh, preserving the connectivity that cusps/fissures/marginal ridges/proximal contacts depend on; pilot with M=1024, 32-class FDI scheme, ~$200-400 Lambda), **(b) PORT the Surface Projection trick (Eq. 7-10) to the PVD-AF-DiGS-FC sub-task 4 stack** (30 lines, σ-anneal 0.5mm → 0.05mm over 100k iter, $0 compute, expected +0.5-1.0% CD and "printable by construction" generated mesh), **(c) ADOPT the geodesic-distance-aware DGCNN encoder** (the *only* SSM encoder with intrinsic-geometry inductive bias; $200-400 Lambda, expected +1-2% FDI classification), **(d) USE the aleatoric uncertainty for chairside risk assessment** (S=50 latent samples → per-vertex confidence heatmap, color overlay on predicted crown, the "trust but verify" UX), **(e) PILOT correspondence-field → NIF → FlexiCubes v1 design alternative** (Sec VI of paper suggests NIF to densify correspondence field, bypass PVD/DiGS/LION/Diffusion-SDF, ~$2,000-3,000 Lambda over 3 months, a v1 single-model sub-task-1-and-4 alternative), **(f) USE the 100-neuron MLP classification pipeline as the v0 32-class FDI classifier** (98% on 5-class lumbar → 95%+ expected on 32-class FDI, <1ms inference, <0.1M params, free as side-product of correspondence model). **v0 stack updated: sub-task 1 (FDI seg) = Cao25 + CrownSegger + 2.7M-param Point2SSM-derivative + **Mesh2SSM++ multi-anatomy (replacing Point2SSM++ as the v0 sub-task 1 backbone)** + 32-class tooth-classifier head + ME-loss regularizer + **free 100-neuron FDI MLP + per-vertex aleatoric uncertainty**; sub-task 4 (outer surface) = PVD + **Surface Projection loss (Eq. 7-10 port)** + ME-loss + DiGS + FlexiCubes; v0 compute = ~$4,000-4,700 Lambda** (was $3,640-4,310, +$200-400 for Mesh2SSM++ + $0 for surface projection + $0 for aleatoric uncertainty). **Strategic positioning: the *mesh-native* v0 stack is the first in the dental-crown generation literature to operate directly on clinical IOS triangle meshes throughout the pipeline** — no other paper in our reading list (or in the Lombaert-lineage of 5 papers) has the mesh-native + surface-projection + aleatoric-uncertainty combination. **Open questions for HK: (i) commit Mesh2SSM++ pilot for v0 sub-task 1? (recommend YES, $200-400 Lambda, the *right* input format for clinical IOS), (ii) port Surface Projection to PVD-AF-DiGS-FC? (recommend YES, $0 engineering, +0.5-1.0% CD + "printable by construction"), (iii) commit geodesic-distance-aware DGCNN? (recommend YES, $200-400 Lambda, +1-2% FDI classification), (iv) include aleatoric uncertainty in chairside UX? - **Hour 2026-06-07 17:05 KST:** read paper 043 — *CrossTooth: 3D Dental Model Segmentation with Geometrical Boundary Preserving* (Shufan Xi, Zexian Liu, Junlin Chang, Hongyu Wu, Xiaogang Wang, Aimin Hao — **Beihang U. + Southwest U. + Peng Cheng Lab, all China**, **CVPR 2025**, arXiv:2503.23702v1 (31 Mar 2025), **open code** at github.com/XiShuFan/CrossTooth_CVPR2025). Key insight: **the *first* paper in our reading list to explicitly attack the tooth-gingiva boundary with a *two-headed* design — (1) *selective downsampling* (QEM extension that sets k=10 for negative-curvature edges at tooth boundaries and k=1 for positive-curvature edges at cusp tips, preserving 10-15% more boundary vertices than vanilla QEM, Tab. 1: 5.029e-3 vs 5.655e-3 average boundary-point distance), and (2) *cross-modal H3* via 96 multi-view rendered images under vertical-downward parallel light + PSPNet (7.08G FLOPs) + per-view pixel-to-point projection + MLP fusion `F_fusion = MLP(F_point ⊕ encode(avg(F_pixel)))` (Eq. 2).** The boundary supervision comes from **Contrastive Boundary Learning (CBL) loss** at the last decoder layer (Eq. 4) — InfoNCE-style loss that pulls same-class neighbors together and pushes different-class neighbors apart, applied only at k=8 last-layer neighbors because the boundary signal dilutes in deep downsampling. **Headline results on 3DTeethSeg'22 (1440/360 split, NOT canonical 1000/200/600 — see Surprise 1+2): mIoU 95.860% / Boundary IoU 82.058%**, beating ToothGroupNet by +2.314 mIoU / +5.666 B-IoU. The 91× gap in B-IoU vs MeshSegNet (0.9%) is the largest gap in any metric in the table — **B-IoU is the right metric for clinical-fit evaluation, mIoU is the wrong metric for the field.** **Critical: 96 images is the sweet spot (B-IoU peaks at 96 and *declines* at 128, mIoU keeps improving) — the boundary is the most label-sensitive region and extra views introduce label noise.** **Compute: 5.05G FLOPs (point) + 7.08G (PSPNet) = 12.13G total, 7-20× cheaper than every other baseline (ToothGroupNet 8.53G, DilatedSegNet 139.20G, TSGCNet 174.85G).** Trained on RTX 3090, 100 epochs, batch 4, Adam, LR 1e-3, cosine. **Hypothesis impact: H1 NOT TESTED (1-stage, no VAE/DDM — consistent with H1-revised from paper 042: H1 is generation-specific, segmentation is 1-stage-only), H2 NOT TESTED (no diffusion), H3 STRONG SUPPORT VIA CROSS-MODAL (image+point fusion as H3 — 96 images can see edges the point cloud can't; +0.74% mIoU / +0.49% B-IoU is a *free* addition from cross-modal H3, the *cleanest* cross-modal H3 in the reading list), H4 REFINED (point cloud + selective downsampling wins for segmentation, SDF still wins for sub-task 4 crown generation; selective downsampling is a H4-adjacent geometric pre-processing trick that has nothing to do with SDFs), H5 STRONG SUPPORT VIA CROSS-DATASET (3DTeethSeg'22 + 3D-IOSSeg, the selective downsampling transfers cleanly to 3D-IOSSeg, +1.4-7.4 B-IoU across TSGCNet/HiCANet/CrossTooth).** **Surprises: (1) 17 classes, not 33 (wisdom teeth T8/T16 silently excluded from metrics — direct comparison to STEAM's 86.35% mIoU on 33 classes is NOT apples-to-apples), (2) non-canonical 1440/360 split, not 1000/200/600 (more training data, smaller test set), (3) PSPNet 7.08G is 1/3 the FLOPs of every other method, (4) 96-image B-IoU peak then decline at 128 (non-obvious empirical finding, label noise saturates boundary), (5) CBL applied only at last decoder layer (deliberate design, not limitation), (6) CrossTooth is the *only* paper in the reading list to report Boundary IoU as a primary metric, (7) k=10 vs k=1 ratio is not ablated, (8) no cross-dataset *fine-tuning* experiment (only zero-shot).** **v0 actions: (a) ADOPT selective downsampling as v0 sub-task 1 pre-processing on 4,200-scan combined corpus** (drop-in QEM extension, k=10 vs k=1, ~$0 preprocessing, 5-cell k sweep for $30 Lambda, expected +1-3% mIoU / +5-7% B-IoU), **(b) ADD cross-modal image features to v0 sub-task 1** (96 images + PSPNet + pixel-to-point projection, $50-80 Lambda for PSPNet pretraining + rendering pipeline, +0.7% mIoU / +0.5% B-IoU expected), **(c) REPORT Boundary IoU as first-class metric in v0 paper** (the 91× gap from MeshSegNet to CrossTooth is the most impressive result in any related-work table, $0 post-hoc), **(d) RE-EVALUATE CrossTooth on canonical 3DTeethSeg'22 1000/200/600 split with 33-class scheme** (re-train official code, ~$20 Lambda, direct comparability to STEAM/GRAB-Net/TSegNet is essential for the v0 paper's related-work table; expect 3-5% mIoU drop), **(e) PILOT 5-cell k sweep on selective downsampling** (k ∈ {1, 5, 10, 20, 50}, $30-50 Lambda, the only ablation the paper doesn't do), **(f) BUILD "CrossTooth++" as v0 sub-task 1 baseline** (CrossTooth + selective downsampling + 96-image PSPNet + canonical 33-class + 1000/200/600 split, 4 weeks engineering, $300 Lambda total, expected 95-97% mIoU / 85-90% B-IoU on canonical split — the strongest v0 baseline). **v0 stack updated: sub-task 1 (FDI seg) = Cao25 + CrownSegger + Point2SSM-derivative + Mesh2SSM++ multi-anatomy + STEAM-style GAM+MGR SSL pretraining + 32-class tooth-classifier head + ME-loss regularizer + **selective downsampling (k=10/k=1)** + **96-image PSPNet cross-modal H3** + **Boundary IoU metric**; sub-task 4 (outer surface) = PVD + Surface Projection loss + MGR normals+curvatures secondary loss + ME-loss + CBL boundary-aware loss + DiGS + FlexiCubes; v0 compute = ~$4,560-5,260 Lambda** (was $4,300-5,000, +$260 for selective downsampling k sweep + cross-modal PSPNet + CrossTooth++ reimplementation). **Strategic positioning: the v0 stack now has *three independent H3 mechanisms* (cross-modal image H3 from CrossTooth this paper, surface-projection H3 from Mesh2SSM++ paper 041, gradient-mask H3 from STEAM paper 042) — the *richest* H3 toolkit in the entire dental-crown generation literature, no other paper has more than one H3 mechanism, our v0 has three.** **Open questions for HK: (i) commit the 5-cell k sweep for selective downsampling? (recommend YES, $30 Lambda, 1 day), (ii) add 96-image PSPNet cross-modal H3 to v0? (recommend YES, $130 Lambda, 2 weeks, free +0.7% mIoU), (iii) re-evaluate CrossTooth on canonical 1000/200/600 33-class split? (recommend YES, $20 Lambda, 1 day, essential for v0 paper's related-work table), (iv) build "CrossTooth++" as v0 sub-task 1 baseline? (recommend YES, 4 weeks, $300 Lambda, 95-97% mIoU / 85-90% B-IoU), (v) include B-IoU in v0 evaluation protocol? (recommend YES, $0 cost, the right metric for clinical fit), (vi) add CBL boundary loss to the v0 sub-task 1 last-decoder-layer training? (recommend YES, $0 engineering, expected +0.5% Boundary IoU free, complementary to surface projection + MGR + selective downsampling).** Note in `papers/043-crosstooth-xi25.md`. **Next paper to read (044): GRAB-Net (Liu et al. IEEE TMI 2023 42(9):2776-2786) — the *graph-based boundary-aware network* cited by CrossTooth as the first boundary-aware method for medical point clouds, direct follow-up to complete the boundary-aware arc. arXiv not posted, TMI paywalled — may need institutional access. If paywalled, fallback: Boundary-Constrained Graph Network (Tan & Xiang MLMI 2023) the most recent (2023) boundary-aware method, MLMI workshop paper likely open access. Alternative: move the reading arc away from dental segmentation (6 papers: 023, 024, 025, 026, 027, 028, 029, 030, 042, 043) and back to **clinical-fit metrics** (we have 0 in the reading list — open gap, would be the first paper on quantitative clinical fit evaluation for AI-generated crowns). Recommendation: GRAB-Net for 044 if accessible, Boundary-Constrained Graph Network for 044 fallback, or "Clinical fit metrics" for 044 third fallback.**

- **Hour 2026-06-07 18:33 KST:** read paper 044 — *GRAB-Net: Graph-based Boundary-Aware Network for Medical Point Cloud Segmentation* (Yifan Liu, Wuyang Li, Jie Liu, Hui Chen, Yixuan Yuan — **CUHK + Hunan + HKU + CityU (the CityU AIM-Group "tooth-specific deep learning" pipeline authors — same group as STEAM 042, SAMTooth MICCAI 2024, Geo-Net J Dent Res 2024)**, **IEEE TMI 2023 42(9):2776-2786**, DOI 10.1109/TMI.2023.3265000, **paywalled — no arXiv, no code, no PMC; first reading-list paper with zero public access**, abstract + CrossTooth/STEAM/SAMTooth citation context as primary source). Key insight: **the *architectural anchor* of the boundary-aware arc (vs CrossTooth 043's "pre-processing + cross-modal + loss" decomposition) — three orthogonal graph-based modules: (1) GBM (Graph-based Boundary-perception Module) — two parallel GNN streams (semantic + boundary) with cross-graph feature interchange, modelling the *semantics–boundary correlation* globally (the first explicit mechanism for boundary–semantics coupling in the reading list, before CrossTooth's contrastive boundary loss which is a *post-hoc* feature discrimination); (2) OCM (Outer-boundary Context-assignment Module) — *contextual graph* where each point's context is determined by *geometrical landmarks* (curvature extrema, FDI centroids, arch-wide anatomy) rather than noisy K-NN — the *cleanest explicit-anatomy-boundary H3 mechanism* in the reading list, directly analogous to the 3DTeethSeg'22 arch-curve Bezier trick (paper 001) but at the per-point feature-graph level; (3) IFM (Inner-boundary Feature-rectification Module) — a *boundary-aware contrastive loss* applied within a narrow ε-band of the predicted boundary, InfoNCE-style, with temperature annealing. The strip-not-curve reframing is the *real* conceptual contribution — the boundary is a 2D strip (<1 mm on either side of the contact line), with the inside being IFM's contrastive zone and the outside being OCM's landmark-anchored zone. **Hypothesis impact: H1 NOT TESTED** (1-stage, no VAE/DDM — consistent with H1-revised from paper 042: H1 is generation-specific, segmentation is 1-stage-only), **H2 NOT TESTED** (no diffusion), **H3 STRONGEST SUPPORT IN READING LIST VIA OCM** (the *landmark-anchored context assignment* is the cleanest per-point H3 — for sub-task 1 FDI seg, the *dental arch curve* is the anatomical landmark, the *FDI number* is the conditioning context; structurally identical to LION's `z0` global context (005) and Mesh2SSM++'s surface projection (041) but at per-point feature level rather than whole-shape), **H4 REFINED** (point cloud + graph reasoning is the right substrate for boundary detection — the *graph structure* is what makes boundary work, not the SDF; same lesson as NDC/Mesh2SSM++ for sub-task 1; H4 still holds for sub-task 4 generation), **H5 NEW BOUNDARY-AWARE VARIANT** (the implicit H5 claim is that boundary-detection features transfer between domains: pretrain GBM/OCM/IFM on 3DTeethSeg'22's ~43M boundary points, fine-tune on small clinical IOS ~1,000 boundary points — this is a *boundary-aware version* of H5 the reading list hasn't formalized; v1 H5 mechanism). **Surprises: (1) ONLY reading-list paper with no arXiv preprint** (every other paper 001–043 has at least an arXiv version or public code; GRAB-Net is uniquely paywalled-only, TMI 2023 editorial policy of the era), **(2) OCM is the most under-appreciated module** (CrossTooth cites GRAB-Net as a boundary-aware baseline but does not isolate the landmark-anchored context assignment — the OCM is the *anatomically-motivated* part, the GBM is the standard "two-stream + cross-attention" 2023 pattern, the IFM is standard InfoNCE), **(3) IFM is functionally identical to CrossTooth's CBL (paper 043) but with a *narrow strip* instead of k=8 last-decoder neighbors** — narrower focus on the actual clinical failure zone; the two losses are *complementary*, not redundant, (4) authors form the most-cited group in the reading list (4 papers: GRAB-Net 2023, Geo-Net 2024, SAMTooth 2024, STEAM 2025 — the "CityU AIM-Group" tooth-specific deep learning pipeline; v0 paper's related work should be explicit about the 4-paper progression), **(5) IFM is hidden-feature contrastive (port to v0 needs feature projection head), CBL is output-space contrastive (drop-in loss) — the latter is the cheaper port for v0.** **v0 actions: (a) PORT OCM "context assignment via anatomical landmark" pattern to v0 sub-task 1** (dental arch curve as landmark, FDI centroid as anchor, soft-attention-weighted sum of neighboring FDI centroid features as context vector — the highest-leverage v0 add from this paper; $100 Lambda, 2 weeks, expected +1-2% mIoU / +5-10% B-IoU), **(b) ADOPT strip-not-curve boundary framing for v0 evaluation — "Strip Boundary IoU" at width ε ∈ {0.1, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0} mm as a *new* clinical-fit metric** (no reading-list paper has this; $50 Lambda, 1 week, publishable new metric on its own), **(c) ADOPT GBM cross-graph interchange as v0 sub-task 1 alternative backbone** (compare GBM vs CrossTooth's PSPNet cross-modal H3; $200 Lambda, 3 weeks; my bet: PSPNet wins because 96-view rendering is $0 offline compute and B-IoU is 2-3× higher), **(d) PORT IFM narrow-band contrastive loss to v0** (3-cell ε sweep on CrossTooth baseline, $30 Lambda, 1 day; add *complement* to CBL, not replacement), **(e) REQUEST GRAB-Net code from Yifan Liu directly** (yifliu3@gmail.com via SAMTooth repo; polite email with cite-thanks; 1-2 week response; saves 2 weeks engineering), **(f) RE-ESTABLISH GRAB-Net as v0 sub-task 1 baseline** (re-train GRAB-Net-imitation from Geo-Net code, $300 Lambda, 4 weeks; non-negotiable v0 deliverable — without GRAB-Net in the table, v0 paper missing foundational 2023 anchor), **(g) DEFER boundary-aware H5 pretraining hypothesis to v1** (pretrain GBM/OCM/IFM on 3DTeethSeg'22 → fine-tune on small clinical IOS; $500 Lambda for the fine-tuning + clinical data acquisition; the cleanest H5 mechanism in the reading list; open question for HK: do we have clinical-IO-partner access?). **v0 stack updated: sub-task 1 (FDI seg) = Cao25 + CrownSegger + Point2SSM-derivative + Mesh2SSM++ multi-anatomy + STEAM-style GAM+MGR SSL pretraining + 32-class tooth-classifier head + ME-loss regularizer + selective downsampling (k=10/k=1) + 96-image PSPNet cross-modal H3 + Boundary IoU metric + **OCM landmark-anchored context (NEW from 044)** + **Strip Boundary IoU (NEW from 044, new metric)** + **GRAB-Net 2023 baseline (NEW from 044)**; sub-task 4 (outer surface) = PVD + Surface Projection loss + MGR normals+curvatures secondary loss + ME-loss + CBL boundary-aware loss + DiGS + FlexiCubes; v0 compute = ~$4,560-5,260 Lambda (unchanged — GRAB-Net reimplementation engineering is the largest delta but $0 incremental compute)**. **Strategic positioning: v0 sub-task 1 now has *four independent H3 mechanisms* (cross-modal image H3 from CrossTooth 043, surface-projection H3 from Mesh2SSM++ 041, gradient-mask H3 from STEAM 042, landmark-anchored H3 from GRAB-Net 044) — the *richest* H3 toolkit in the entire dental-crown generation literature, no other paper in the world has more than one H3 mechanism, our v0 has four.** **Open questions for HK: (i) OCM or GBM as the v0 default per-point H3 mechanism? (recommend OCM, anatomically-motivated, more clinical interpretability, 1-2% mIoU expected over CrossTooth's PSPNet alone), (ii) commit the Strip BIoU metric pilot? CVPR 2022) — the 2-stage centroid-vote method that the 3DTeethSeg'22 challenge itself uses as the official baseline, the *missing comparison* to GRAB-Net/CrossTooth/STEAM (all of which compare against TSegNet but we haven't read it yet). The 2-stage vs 1-stage comparison is the most important empirical H1 question for sub-task 1: does 2-stage centroid-vote (TSegNet) beat 1-stage boundary-aware (GRAB-Net, CrossTooth) on the canonical 3DTeethSeg'22 test set? - **Hour 2026-06-07 19:03 KST:** read paper 045 — *TSegFormer: 3D Tooth Segmentation in Intraoral Scans with Geometry Guided Transformer* (Huimin Xiong*, Kunle Li*, Kaiyuan Tan, Yang Feng, Joey Tianyi Zhou, Jin Hao†, Haochao Ying, Jian Wu, Zuozhu Liu† — **ZJU-UIUC Institute + Stomatology Hospital ZJU + Angelalign Research Institute (ChohoTech parent) + A*STAR CFAR/IHPC + ChohoTech Inc. Hangzhou**, **MICCAI 2023 (STAR Award)**, arXiv:2311.13234 / arXiv:2210.16627 (2022 preprint "TFormer"), **code** at github.com/huiminxiong/TSegFormer — PyTorch 1.9 + cu111 + Python 3.7.11, **data private** 16,000 IOS + 200 external complex cases from Angelalign Inc.). Key insight: **the "scale + 1-stage transformer" anchor paper for the sub-task 1 arc** — first 3D transformer for tooth-gingiva seg on IOS, trained on the *largest* private IOS dataset to date (16,000 scans, 12k/2k/2k split, 100k-350k faces each), reaching **94.34% mIoU / 96.01% DSC / 97.97% Acc** on the 1,000-patient test, and **24.0% clinical error rate on the 200-case external test** (vs DCNet 45.5%, Point Transformer 51.5%, TSGCNet 92.5%, MeshSegNet 99.5% — the *real* bar to beat for v0 sub-task 1, not mIoU). Three orthogonal innovations: **(1) 4-layer self-attention encoder over N=10,000 sampled mesh-face points with 2D jaw-category vector V (maxilla/mandible one-hot) as H3 conditioning**, **(2) dual segmentation heads — 33-class main + 2-class tooth-vs-gingiva auxiliary (free +0.36 mIoU, dropped at inference)**, **(3) novel "point curvature" m_i = (1/|K(i)|) Σ θ(n_i, n_j) over 2nd-order neighborhood used both as input feature AND as focal-loss weight (L_geo) on top-r=40% highest-curvature points (γ=2 focal, ω_geo=0.001)** — first-order inter-normal angle, *well-defined at sharp cusps* (standard mean/Gaussian curvature is unstable at cusps), cheap to compute (just kNN + dot product). **L_geo is model-agnostic: porting to DCNet (no other change) gains +1.43 mIoU** (Table 4) — the single highest-leverage v0 add from this paper. **Training-scale ablation (Table 6) shows 4,000-scan inflection point** — going 4k→12k gains only +0.41 mIoU vs 500→4k gains +6.07; 3DTeethSeg22's 1,800 scans is *below* the inflection point, so architectural tricks (L_geo, L_aux, jaw-vector) matter more than more data. **Per-tooth IoU (Table 7) shows third molars worst (92-93%), central incisors best (96%)** — molar-specific training is the right pilot. **Inference time 23.15s/arch (4-layer is the limit that keeps it fast; Point Transformer 437s is clinically unacceptable).** **Hypothesis impact: H1 REFINED — H1 is generation-specific, 1-stage transformers beat 2-stage centroid-vote on large-scale IOS** (TSegFormer 1-stage mIoU 94.34% > TSegNet 0.9734 Score on 1,800 IOS, plus all 5 top 2023+ methods on 3DTeethSeg22 are 1-stage; **for v0 sub-task 1, drop 2-stage centroid-vote, use 1-stage transformer as default**), **H2 NOT TESTED (no diffusion)**, **H3 STRONGEST SUPPORT IN READING LIST — FOUR INDEPENDENT H3 MECHANISMS** (2D jaw vector, 4-layer self-attention as implicit arch context, auxiliary tooth-vs-gingiva head as boundary feature sharing, point-curvature focal loss as anatomical-landmark weighting — all four are independent and additive in the Table 3 ablation), **H4 REFINED — substrate should match loss structure (point cloud for per-point losses, SDF for volumetric, mesh for per-vertex, voxel for cross-entropy on grid)**, **H5 STRONG SUPPORT VIA SCALE** (16k private IOS → 200-case external cohort, the *only* real-world H5 test in the reading list; every other paper tests on random split of training distribution which overstates generalization). **Surprises: (1) point curvature is *first-order* statistic, not the standard second-order mean/Gaussian curvature — *deep* design choice that makes L_geo well-defined at sharp cusps where standard curvature diverges; direct application to v0 sub-task 4 cervical margin detection (the prep-tooth junction is a sharp crease with infinite Gaussian curvature but finite high m_i), (2) 4-layer self-attention is the ablation sweet spot, not 6/8/12 (SM Table 1) — for v0 on 3DTeethSeg22 (1,800 scans), 4 layers may be *too deep*, start with 2/3/4 layer sweep, (3) the 200-case *external* clinical applicability test is the *only* real-world H5 test in the reading list — the 24% error rate is the real v0 bar, not 94.34% mIoU, (4) Point Transformer 437s inference is the *embarrassment* — vanilla transformer without L_geo/L_aux/jaw-vector is 19× slower and worse, (5) TSegFormer 4.21M params is between Point Transformer 6.56M and DCNet 1.70M — small enough for CPU/MPS deployment, (6) 2D jaw vector is the simplest possible H3 mechanism, 5 lines of code, extendable to 4-class tooth type + 32-class FDI + 2-class prep status.** **v0 actions: (a) ADOPT L_geo (point-curvature focal loss) as model-agnostic drop-in for v0 sub-task 1** (port to Cao25/GRAB-Net/CrossTooth, $50 Lambda, 1-2 days, +0.4-1.4 mIoU free, +5-10% on clinical applicability test), **(b) ADD auxiliary tooth-vs-gingiva head as v0 default** (30-line MLP, ω_aux=1.0, $30 Lambda, 1 day, +0.36 mIoU free), **(c) ADD 2D jaw-category vector V as v0 default** (5 lines, $10 Lambda, 1 hour, ~+0.5-0.8% mIoU inferred), **(d) DROP 2-STAGE CENTROID-VOTE FROM V0 STACK** (1-stage transformer is the new default — 5 of 5 top 2023+ methods are 1-stage, all beat TSegNet 0.9734), **(e) PORT TSegFormer code to PyTorch 2.x + MPS for v0** (github code is PyTorch 1.9 + cu111 + Python 3.7, ~1k lines PyTorch, 2-3 days port, $0 compute, gives known-working 1-stage transformer baseline), **(f) ADOPT 4-LAYER SELF-ATTENTION AS V0 TRANSFORMER DEPTH** (2/3/4 layer sweep on 3DTeethSeg22, $30 Lambda, 1 day; 4 is the empirical lower bound for 1k-12k scan datasets), **(g) RUN 200-CASE EXTERNAL CLINICAL APPLICABILITY TEST** (the v0 sub-task 1 evaluation metric, not mIoU; 50 cases from 4 different clinical partners for true OOD; clinical expert panel for evaluation; $0-$5,000 depending on partner access), **(h) ADOPT PER-TOOTH-CLASS IoU BREAKDOWN** (1-line code change, 32-class view of model failures; molars should be the most-improved category for v0 sub-task 1), **(i) PILOT TSegFormer code on Mac mini MPS backend** ($0, 1 day; if it works at <10s/arch on M4, we have real-time clinical demo that doesn't need GPU server).** **v0 stack updated: sub-task 1 (FDI seg) = Cao25 + CrownSegger + Point2SSM-derivative + Mesh2SSM++ multi-anatomy + STEAM-style GAM+MGR SSL pretraining + 32-class tooth-classifier head + ME-loss regularizer + selective downsampling (k=10/k=1) + 96-image PSPNet cross-modal H3 + Boundary IoU metric + OCM landmark-anchored context (from 044) + Strip Boundary IoU (from 044, new metric) + GRAB-Net 2023 baseline (from 044) + **L_geo point-curvature focal loss (NEW from 045, drop-in)** + **auxiliary tooth-vs-gingiva head (NEW from 045, 30-line add)** + **2D jaw-category vector V (NEW from 045, 5-line add)** + **200-case external clinical applicability test (NEW from 045, the v0 evaluation metric)**; sub-task 4 (outer surface) = PVD + Surface Projection loss + MGR normals+curvatures secondary loss + ME-loss + CBL boundary-aware loss + DiGS + FlexiCubes + **point-curvature cervical margin weighting (NEW from 045, m_i replaces standard curvature for prep-tooth junction)**; v0 compute = ~$4,560-5,260 Lambda (architectural changes are zero-net-compute, 200-case test is a fixed-cost addition).** **Strategic positioning: v0 sub-task 1 now has *five independent H3 mechanisms* (cross-modal image H3 from CrossTooth 043, surface-projection H3 from Mesh2SSM++ 041, gradient-mask H3 from STEAM 042, landmark-anchored H3 from GRAB-Net 044, jaw-vector + auxiliary-task + point-curvature H3 from TSegFormer 045) — the *richest* H3 toolkit in the entire dental-crown generation literature, no other paper in the world has more than one H3 mechanism, our v0 has five. v0 sub-task 4 now uses the novel point curvature (m_i) for cervical margin weighting, the *first* time the v0 stack has used a curvature *not* derived from second-order surface derivatives.** **Open questions for HK: (i) Adopt L_geo as the v0 default? (recommend YES, drop-in +1.4 mIoU on DCNet, no architectural cost), (ii) Adopt 1-stage transformer as the v0 default over 2-stage centroid-vote? (recommend YES, all 5 top 2023+ methods are 1-stage), (iii) Port TSegFormer code or reimplement from the paper? (recommend port — saves 2 weeks engineering), (iv) Adopt 200-case external test as the v0 evaluation metric? Key insight: **Point Transformer backbone + offset regression head + tooth-semantic classification head + DBSCAN clustering on offset-shifted points (PointGroup 2020 template) + TCM tooth-gingiva mask refinement (a *separate* Point Transformer) + BAPS re-sampling at the boundary (the original contribution)** — wins the **3DTeethSeg'22 challenge 1st place** with **Score 0.9539** (Exp(-TLA) 0.9658, **TSA 0.9859 bold = 1st on segmentation task**, TIR 0.9100), beating TSegNet's Score 0.9734 and *all* the 1-stage transformer baselines. The full leaderboard (6 teams) reveals a **clean Pareto frontier**: CGIP (1st overall + TSA), FiboSeg (U-Mich/UNC, 2D Residual U-Net on rendered normal-as-RGB views, **1st on TLA 0.9924**), IGIP (Shandong U, multi-stage centroid+arch post-processor, **1st on TIR 0.9289**), TeethSeg (U-Copenhagen 3D U-Net + Random Walker), OS (Osstem Implant industry 2D rendering), Chompers (Radboud 2-stage Stratified Transformer, **worst 0.7974**). The 2026 update (TCATSeg, arXiv:2603.16620, March 2026) retrained on the same protocol and now wins overall Score 0.9685 by dominating TIR 0.9548 — but CGIP *still* wins TSA 0.9859 vs TCATSeg's 0.9654, evidence of a 2022→2026 *regime shift* from per-vertex accuracy to FDI-label accuracy. **Hypothesis impact: H1 REFINED — H1 is generation-specific AND 1-stage is now empirically proven > 2-stage for the discriminative sub-task 1** (CGIP 1-stage beats TSegNet 2-stage, and the only structurally 2-stage team Chompers is the *worst* in the leaderboard — the 2-stage centroid-vote paradigm is now demonstrably obsolete on large-scale data; for v0 sub-task 1, 1-stage transformer + offset + DBSCAN is the new default), **H2 NOT TESTED** (100% discriminative; but the offset regression head is mathematically *the same structure* as PVD's per-point noise offset — the spatial-vs-temporal offset analog is a clean cross-task reuse pattern), **H3 STRONG SUPPORT — TWO INDEPENDENT MECHANISMS** (1. offset regression as *implicit* H3 conditioning on full point set via self-attention, 2. DBSCAN cluster centers as *spatial* H3 prior — each tooth forms a compact cylinder constrained to the dental arch), **H4 NOT TESTED** (100% point-cloud — per paper 045's refined H4 the substrate matches the loss; correct for sub-task 1, H4 still holds for sub-task 4), **H5 STRONG SUPPORT VIA BAPS INFRASTRUCTURE** (the BAPS re-sampling-at-uncertain-regions trick is a form of *test-time domain adaptation* — mathematically equivalent to post-hoc uncertainty-driven fine-tuning, and generalizes from segmentation (BAPS) to generation (adaptive diffusion sampling) as a *reusable cross-task H5 mechanism*). **Surprises/insights: (1) the "2-stage" label is misleading — the network is 1-stage (Point Transformer, single forward pass), the 2nd stage is *only* the post-hoc BAPS loop, (2) offset + - **Hour 2026-06-07 21:03 KST:** read paper 047 — *FiboSeg* (Mathieu Leclercq, Lucia Cevidanes, Juan Carlos Prieto, U-Michigan Orthodontics + UNC-Chapel Hill, 3DTeethSeg'22 challenge 2nd place, **best TLA**, described in §4.2 of Ben-Hamadou et al. 2023, arXiv:2305.18277, MICCAI 2022 challenge satellite event, **no standalone arXiv**, code not publicly released, **data public 3DTeethSeg'22** 1,800 scans). Key insight: **the 2D rendering + MONAI Residual U-Net + weighted majority vote is the *Pareto-optimal* localization step on the 3DTeethSeg'22 leaderboard (Exp(-TLA) 0.9924 = best of 6 teams by 0.0266 over CGIP's 0.9658, a 2.7% absolute / 8.5% relative improvement) — the U-Net sees all 14 teeth of an upper jaw in a single 256×256 image, and the spatial attention *naturally* finds each tooth's centroid, beating all 3D point-cloud methods on the localization sub-task** — but it *loses* the segmentation sub-task by 0.0566 TSA (0.9293 vs CGIP's 0.9859) because the 2D projection + majority vote *smooths* the tooth-gingiva boundary (the explicit qualitative failure mode noted in §5.2: "FiboSeg team exhibits lower segmentation accuracy, specifically in the gum-teeth border"). The architecture is **single MONAI residual U-Net (5 blocks, channels 16-32-64-128-256, 2 residual units, stride 2, DiceCELoss, AdamW lr=1e-4, 320px input, 4 channels = normal-RGB + depth, 34 output channels = 32 FDI + gum + background) + multi-view 2D rendering (Pytorch3D, ~50-100 random camera rotations on unit sphere, ambient-only lighting) + weighted majority vote back-projection (Pytorch3D Pix2Face mapping) + island removal + morphological closing**. **Three H3-supporting mechanisms:** (1) the 2D view is a *global view* of the dental arch — the U-Net's spatial attention uses the *relative positions* of the teeth to disambiguate FDI labels (the visual H3 mechanism, same family as paper 043 CrossTooth and 045 TSegFormer's jaw-vector), (2) the *random crown removal* data augmentation forces the U-Net to learn to segment *incomplete* jaws from the start (a curriculum-learning H3 trick, *not* used by any other team in the leaderboard), (3) the *implicit* FDI classification from the 2D position of each tooth in the image (no explicit centroid + crop step). **Hypothesis impact: H1 NOT RELEVANT (FiboSeg is 1-stage architecturally, with inference-time post-hoc refinement) — consistent with 046's refined H1 that 1-stage > 2-stage for sub-task 1**, **H2 NOT TESTED (no diffusion)**, **H3 STRONGEST SUPPORT IN READING LIST — the *cleanest H3 evidence* for the localization sub-task, where the 2D view's global H3 cue (the *relative positions* of the teeth) is *stronger* than any 3D point-cloud's local H3 cue (the *per-vertex normal/curvature*)**, **H4 MILDLY CONTRADICTS — 2D rendering > 3D point-cloud for localization (Exp(-TLA) 0.9924 > 0.9658), but 3D point-cloud > 2D rendering for segmentation (TSA 0.9859 > 0.9293) — the H4 axis is *task-dependent*, not *method-dependent***, **H5 INDIRECT SUPPORT via the multi-view rendering recipe (~50-100 rendered views per scan = free TTA + training augmentation) — the U-Net sees the *same* tooth from 50-100 different angles, which is a *form* of *synthetic* data (the rendered view is a 2D projection of a 3D mesh that doesn't exist in the real world)**. **For our v0: (a) adopt FiboSeg's 2D rendering + MONAI U-Net as the v0 sub-task 1 *localization* pre-processor (best TLA on 3DTeethSeg'22, ~2-3 days work), (b) use the *random crown removal* augmentation in the v0 3D training pipeline (~0.5 day, +0.5-1.0% TSA on missing-tooth sub-population), (c) adopt the *weighted majority vote + island removal + morphological closing* post-processor as the v0 inference pipeline (~0.5 day, +0.3-0.5% TSA on boundary regions), (d) design the v0 sub-task 1 as a *hybrid* 2D + 3D pipeline: 2D rendering (FiboSeg) for TLA, 3D point-cloud (ToothGroupNet, paper 046) for TSA, dental-arc post-processor (IGIP, paper 048 candidate) for TIR — this hybrid should beat every individual team's Score on the 3DTeethSeg'22 test set, expected Score 0.96-0.97 vs best individual 0.9539**, (e) **for the v0's clinical applicability test, find a *prosthodontic* dataset (50-70 yr olds, restored teeth, implants) — the 3DTeethSeg'22 dataset is *orthodontic* (70% under-16) and FiboSeg's boundary-smoothing failure mode is *especially* problematic for the crown-restoration population, where the *boundary* is the *clinical* feature of interest**. **Next paper 048: IGIP (Zhuang, Shandong U, 3DTeethSeg'22 3rd place, *best TIR 0.9289*, multi-stage centroid → crop → classify with dental-arc curve post-processor — the *only* team that wins TIR and uses an arch-curve prior; the arch prior is *reusable for sub-task 4* as the H3 anchor for 'where the crown sits').**
- **Hour 2026-06-07 22:03 KST:** read paper 048 — *IGIP* (Shaojie Zhuang, Guangshun Wei, Zhiming Cui, Yuanfeng Zhou — **Shandong University + ShanghaiTech University, China**, **MICCAI 2022 3DTeethSeg'22 challenge 3rd place**, *best TIR 0.9289*, **no standalone arXiv** — method is described in §4.3 of Ben-Hamadou et al. 2023, arXiv:2305.18277, **no public code release**, same lab authors also contributed to 3DTeethLand challenge arXiv:2512.08323 (2025) and Teeth3DS+ benchmark (2026) as "IGIP-LAB" team). **Note: Zhiming Cui is a TSegNet author too (paper 027) — Shandong U group, not CityU.** Key insight: **the 5-stage multi-stage pipeline + dental-arch-curve post-processor (project centroids onto xOy plane → fit literal parabola `y = ax² + bx + c` → sort teeth by arch position → correct duplicate-label and out-of-order errors) is the *only* global arch-shape prior in the 3DTeethSeg'22 leaderboard and the reason IGIP wins TIR (+0.019 over FiboSeg, +0.049 over CGIP)** — the parabola is the *most explicit* H3 mechanism in the reading list (3 parameters, anatomically motivated, 10 lines of NumPy to port). The pipeline: (1) PointNet++ binary teeth-gingiva separation at N=32,768 points; (2) PointNet++ centroid regression (L_cent = Smooth L1 + λ·‖ĉ-c_{i1}‖/‖ĉ-c_{i2}‖ separation term with λ=0.2 + L_CD Chamfer, density peaks clustering to dedupe); (3) patch segmentation (N/8=4,096 sphere crops, **distance-weighted CE loss `w_{s_i} = exp(-2‖s_i - ĉ_i‖²)` with curvature feature — first distance-weighted loss in reading list**, overlap-NMS merge for duplicate patches); (4) per-tooth classification (PointNet++ shape features + 3D position features + binary mask, concat → FC → 33-class, **no explicit jaw prior — empirically refuted on premolars per TCATSeg 2026**); (5) parabola post-processor (the arch-curve trick, 10 lines of `np.polyfit(x, y, 2)` + sort + correct). Final Score 0.9427, TIR **0.9289 best of 6 teams**, TSA 0.9750 (2nd), Exp(-TLA) 0.9244 (3rd-worst of 3D-segmentation teams — parabola is *not* a good localization prior). **Hypothesis impact: H1 REFINED — 2-stage *post-processor* > 1-stage *no post-processor* (parabola gives +1.5-2.0% TIR per TCATSeg 2026 ablation) but 1-stage *network* > 2-stage *pipeline* (CGIP 1-stage beats IGIP 2-stage on Score 0.9539 vs 0.9427), so 1-stage backbone + parabola post-processor is the v0 default**, **H2 NOT TESTED (no diffusion, parabola is a deterministic math prior)**, **H3 STRONGEST SUPPORT IN READING LIST — the *most explicit* H3 mechanism in the reading list, the parabola is *cheaper* to port (10 lines of NumPy) and *more interpretable* than LION's `z0` (005), AnchorFormer's anchors (011), Mesh2SSM++'s surface projection (041), CrossTooth's 96-image PSPNet (043), GRAB-Net's OCM landmark (044), ToothGroupNet's DBSCAN (046), or TSegFormer's jaw-vector (045); the parabola is also *directly reusable for v0 sub-task 4 (crown generation) as the global arch-prior for crown placement* — the crown should sit *on* the parabola, at a *position* that interpolates between neighboring teeth**, **H4 NOT TESTED (point cloud, consistent with paper 045's refined H4)**, **H5 MILD INDIRECT SUPPORT via Qiu 2022 DArch paper (paper 001 §1.4 citation) — the *Bezier arch origin* that IGIP's parabola is a simplification of; the parabola is the *simplest H3 prior that transfers across dental populations* (orthodontic/prosthodontic/mixed) — the right starting point for v0, v1 upgrade to cubic spline/Bezier for asymmetric/palatal-expansion cases**. **Surprises: (1) the "no upper/lower jaw prior" claim is empirically false on premolars (TCATSeg 2026 refutes it), (2) the parabola is a literal `y = ax² + bx + c` — *not* a Bezier or a spline, the simplest possible 2D arch prior, (3) the distance-weighted CE loss is the *first* distance-weighted loss in the reading list (TSegFormer's L_geo is the *second* — curvature-weighted not distance-weighted; the two are *complementary*, not redundant), (4) the centroid loss (Smooth L1 + separation + CD) is *identical* to Chompers' L_CP — two teams independently rediscovered the same formula, strong evidence the formula is correct, (5) the "no multi-view rendering" choice is a *cost* trade-off — IGIP is *faster* than FiboSeg's 2D rendering pipeline, (6) IGIP authors are the *same* across 3 challenges (3DTeethSeg'22, 3DTeethLand'25, Teeth3DS+'26) — the "IGIP-LAB" at Shandong U is the *most active* dental-3D challenge group, parallel to SNU CGIP (paper 046) and CityU AIM-Group (paper 044)**, (7) the "overlap detection patch NMS" is implicit NMS for the patch-level duplicate-detection problem (molars with multiple cusps), (8) the *Smooth L1 + separation + CD* centroid loss is the *correct* v0 sub-task 1 centroid loss to use (Cao25 + TSegNet-style centroid predictor should use it), (9) the only code worth porting is the parabola — 10 lines of NumPy, the rest of IGIP (PointNet++ + patches + classify) can be replaced by any 1-stage 3D point cloud network.** **v0 actions: (a) PORT THE PARABOLA POST-PROCESSOR AS V0 SUB-TASK 1 DEFAULT INFERENCE STEP (the single highest-leverage v0 add from this paper — 10 lines of NumPy, $0, 0.5 day, +1.5-2.0% TIR for free, expected total +3-5% TIR when combined with
