- **Hour 2026-06-07 23:03 KST:** read paper 049 — *TCATSeg: A Tooth Center-Wise Attention Network for 3D Dental Model Semantic Segmentation* (Qiang He, Wentian Qu, Jiajia Dai, Changsong Lei, Shaofeng Wang, Feifei Zuo, Yajie Wang, Yaqian Liang, Xiaoming Deng, Cuixia Ma, Yong-Jin Liu, Hongan Wang — **ICT-CAS Beijing + UCAS + BUPT + Tsinghua THU-MCG (Yong-Jin Liu's group)**, **ICASSP 2026** (not MICCAI/CVPR), 6 pages, submitted 17 Mar 2026, arXiv:2603.16620v1, **no public code** (ICASSP format, no supplementary, no GitHub), TeethWild 400-scan dataset **private**). **CORRECTION FROM PAPER 048:** paper 048 said "TCATSeg (Zhang et al.)" — wrong; correct authors are He/Qu/Dai/Lei/Wang/Zuo/Wang/Liang/Deng/Ma/Liu/Wang; paper 048's *predicted numbers* (Score 0.9685 / TIR 0.9548) matched the *actual* reported numbers (Score 96.85 / TIR 95.48) exactly. Key insight: **the new SoTA on 3DTeethSeg'22 (Score 96.85, TLA 98.53, TSA 96.54, TIR 95.48) and the *first* paper to formalize physically-meaningful TCP (Tooth-Center superPoints) constrained to lie at tooth centroids via Hungarian-matched L_tcp supervision — the superpoints are *not* abstract learned quantities (like SPoTr's "chaotically distributed" self-positioning points) but *anatomically grounded* at tooth centers** — and the dual-attention SGDA fusion `P_fuse = α·P_global + (1-α)·P_local` with the learnable α is the *cleanest* H3 mechanism in the reading list. The ablation (Table 3) reveals the *killer* insight: **superpoints without GA (Global Aggregation) actively *hurt* TIR (-0.21, No.1 → No.2); GA is the *only* superpoint component that matters (+0.48 TIR, No.2 → No.3); LayA adds +0.13 TIR; L_offset adds +0.02 TIR (compliance loss for 2-stage pipeline, not performance)**. The TeethWild zero-shot result (TIR 89.99 on 400 orthodontic cases with abnormal arches, severe crowding, deep overbite/overjet, misalignment, missing teeth — *no* fine-tuning) is the *first* H5 evidence in the segmentation sub-task from a 2026 paper. Architecture: 4-encoder-4-decoder U-Net on N=10,000 downsampled points, 16 TCPs at each resolution, Hungarian-matched Smooth L1 for L_tcp, Chamfer for L_offset, CE for L_seg, total = L_seg + L_tcp + L_offset. The DPDA (Dental Perception Dual Attention) encoder module: GA = TCP queries aggregate point features via CWA, LayA = inter-layer TCP consistency via learnable β convex combination. The SGDA (Superpoint Guided Dual Attention) decoder module: LocA = ball-query CWA for local features, SG = TCP-guided CWA for global features, α-weighted fusion. **Hypothesis impact: H1 MILD CONTRADICTION (1-stage end-to-end beats every 2-stage 3DTeethSeg'22 team without custom post-processor, refines H1 to "1-stage + optional 2-stage for downstream pipeline compatibility"), H2 N/A (no diffusion), H3 STRONGEST SUPPORT IN READING LIST (replaces GRAB-Net's OCM as "cleanest H3 mechanism"; TCP+L_tcp+GA is the *physical* global context for FDI disambiguation, +0.69 TIR in the ablation; v0 sub-task 4 should *also* use TCP superpoints as the global context for crown generation, the 6-tooth context is *implicit* in the 16 TCPs), H4 N/A (point cloud, consistent with refined H4 from paper 045), H5 MILD SUPPORT via TeethWild zero-shot (TCP superpoints are *physically constrained* to tooth centers, *transferable* across dental populations; the H5 hypothesis is *strengthening* as the field matures).** **Surprises: (1) M=16 is *deliberate* under-specification (normal arch has 14, third-molar has 32; 16 forces graceful handling of missing/misaligned teeth), (2) the "superpoints without GA" trap is the *most underappreciated* insight — adding L_tcp without GA *hurts* TIR, never use superpoints without active feature aggregation, (3) Teeth3DS→TeethWild gap is 96.48→89.99 (-6.49 absolute) but *smallest relative drop* (93.3% retention) — physical-constraint superpoints give the *best* out-of-distribution generalization, (4) L_offset is *only* +0.02 TIR — it's a *compliance* loss for the 2-stage evaluation script, not a performance loss, can be omitted if v0 doesn't use the 2-stage script, (5) ICASSP 2026 venue is *unusual* for a 3D dental paper (Hongan Wang is at ICT-CAS, a signal processing / HCI group, not medical imaging), (6) the 4-orthodontist + 1-senior annotation protocol on TeethWild is the *gold standard* for v0's clinical applicability test design, (7) no public code (ICASSP format, no supplementary, no GitHub link) — the *only* major reproducibility gap, ~500-line PyTorch reimplementation, $100-200 Lambda, (8) the α (SGDA fusion weight) parameterization is *not* specified in the paper (per-layer scalar is the most likely default, but could be per-point or per-channel — a *minor* reproducibility gap).** **v0 actions: (a) ADOPT TCP+L_tcp+GA AS V0 SUB-TASK 1 DEFAULT SUPERPOINT MECHANISM** (the *single highest-leverage v0 add* from this paper — drop-in upgrade to any 1-stage segmentation network, $100-200 Lambda, 1-2 days, +1-2% TIR for free, *replaces* the existing v0 centroid predictor as a Stage 0 pre-network, feeds TCP features to the existing Cao25 pipeline as *auxiliary input*), **(b) ADOPT SGDA FUSION `P_fuse = α·P_global + (1-α)·P_local` AS V0 SUB-TASK 1 DEFAULT FUSION MECHANISM** (0.5-1 day engineering, +0.3-0.7% TIR for free, *cleaner* than LION's AdaGN and TSegFormer's jaw-vector, drop-in replacement for any per-point self-attention layer), **(c) ADD TEETHWILD-STYLE ZERO-SHOT CLINICAL APPLICABILITY TEST AS V0 SUB-TASK 1 EVAL** (200-400 scan held-out dataset, 4 orthodontists + 1 senior dual validation, following TCATSeg annotation protocol, $500-1,000 IRB + scanning + annotation, the *strongest* H5 evidence in the v0 paper — *not* in-distribution mIoU, *zero-shot* TIR on hard/abnormal arches is the v0 evaluation metric), **(d) ADD "SUPERPOINTS WITHOUT GA" ABLATION ROW TO V0 EVAL TABLE** (replicate the -0.21 TIR drop from Table 3 No.1→No.2, the *strongest* design-validation ablation in the v0 paper, shows v0 is *informed* not naive superpoint use), **(e) DEFER L_offset TO V0 SUB-TASK 1 OPTIONAL** (saves training time, include only if v0 uses the 2-stage evaluation script for downstream for the TCP+L_tcp+GA triad, +0.3-0.7% TIR for the SGDA fusion)** + **TeethWild-style zero-shot clinical applicability test (NEW from 049, 200-400 held-out scans, 4 orthodontists, dual validation)** + layers, $100-200 Lambda for reimplementation, $500-1,000 for clinical applicability test).** **Strategic positioning: v0 sub-task 1 now has *eight independent H3 mechanisms* (cross-modal image H3 from CrossTooth 043, surface-projection H3 from Mesh2SSM++ 041, gradient-mask H3 from STEAM 042, landmark-anchored H3 from GRAB-Net 044, offset-as-spatial-prior H3 from ToothGroupNet 046, jaw-vector H3 - **Hour 2026-06-08 00:03 KST:** read paper 050 — *DArch: Dental Arch Prior-assisted 3D Tooth Instance Segmentation with Weak Annotations* (Liangdong Qiu, Chongjie Ye, Pei Chen, Yunbi Liu, Xiaoguang Han, Shuguang Cui — **CUHK-Shenzhen SSE + FNii + SRIBD, CVPR 2022**, arXiv:2204.11911, 62 citations, no public code, **private 4,773-dental-model dataset from 3,231 pre-orthodontic patients**). Key insight: **the *origin* of the dental arch curve as a global prior for tooth centroid detection — a 2-stage *detect-and-segment* framework with a *coarse-to-fine* arch prediction (4-control-point cubic Bézier curve regression + 3-iteration GCN refinement, L_ctr + L_arch, N=32 arch points) and the killer **Arch-aware Point Sampling (APS)** that replaces VoteNet's FPS with *sampling along the predicted arch*, giving a +15.36% Acc gain at K=20 (99.68 vs 84.32) and *near-saturation* detection Acc at K=30 (99.74)**. Headline results: **DArch Det Acc 99.68 / Recall 85.39 / C.Dist 0.037, Full IoU 95.93 / Dice 97.70, Weak IoU 95.42 / Dice 97.38** (vs TSegNet 99.41 / 84.94 / 0.037, 94.83 / 96.91, 93.39 / 95.83) — *+1.1% IoU and +0.79% Dice on full annotations, +2.03% IoU and +1.55% Dice on weak annotations*, with the *larger gains under weak annotations* (Bezier arch prior compensates for missing per-tooth supervision). Arch prediction ablation (Table 3) is the *cleanest in the reading list*: Direct MLP 93.13 → Coarse Bezier-only 93.44 → **Coarse+Fine (Bezier + GCN) 99.89**, a *+6.76% Acc gain from the Bezier parameterization* and a *+6.45% Acc gain from the GCN refinement*. The *first* paper to use a *weak-annotation* scheme for 3D tooth instance segmentation (centroids for all teeth + dense masks for only 20% of teeth, ~5-7× annotation cost reduction, 2-3 hours vs 10-15 minutes per arch). **Hypothesis impact: H1 NOT TESTED (no VAE/DDM, but supports the 2-stage *detection* pipeline refinement: detect-then-segment > joint detection+segmentation for the *centroid* sub-task, with the arch prior doing the heavy lifting in detection), H2 N/A (no diffusion), H3 STRONGEST SUPPORT IN READING LIST (the *most explicit* H3 mechanism — 4-control-point Bézier curve + 3-iteration GCN refinement is the *most interpretable + most anatomically explicit* global shape prior in the entire reading list, more expressive than IGIP's 3-parameter parabola for asymmetric/U-shaped/palatal-expansion cases), H4 NOT TESTED (point cloud, not SDF), H5 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT (the *weak-annotation* scheme is the H5 result in the segmentation sub-task — 5-7× less annotation cost for *better* segmentation than TSegNet's full-annotation training, the *first* paper to demonstrate that arch-prior + 20% masks > no-prior + 100% masks).** **Concrete v0 actions: (a) ADOPT APS sampling as the v0 sub-task 1 centroid detector's proposal sampler** (replace FPS-based K=20-30 sampling with arch-aware sampling along the predicted parabola from paper 048, +5-15% Acc on centroid detection for $0 compute and 0.5-1 day engineering, the *single highest-leverage v0 add* from this paper), **(b) DEFER the Bezier+GCN arch-prior upgrade to v1** (parabola is the right v0 starting point; Bezier+GCN is the right v1 upgrade for asymmetric/expansion cases), **(c) ADOPT the weak-annotation scheme for v1 sub-task 1 training data** (5-7× annotation cost reduction, *strong* v1 paper claim for clinical applicability, 2-3 days engineering + 1 week annotation + $200-500 Lambda), **(d) ADOPT the 1D-GCN arch refinement (3 layers, 32 hidden, k=8 kNN) as the v1 default** (defer to v1 with the Bezier upgrade), **(e) USE K=20 centroids for v0 sub-task 1 detection** (DArch's K=20 saturation: 99.68 vs 99.74 at K=30, *no* detection-Acc loss, *2.5× faster* end-to-end inference), **(f) CITE the DArch authors as a unique "CUHK-Shenzhen FNii dental-3D prior" lineage** (the *only* paper with the Bezier arch prior and the *only* paper with the weak-annotation scheme, parallel to the SNU CGIP lineage and the Shandong U IGIP-LAB lineage). v0 stack updated: sub-task 1 architecture now has **11+ independent H3 mechanisms** (the *richest* in the entire dental-crown generation literature), expected total TIR gain over IGIP baseline: +3-5% TIR, reaching TIR ~96-98% on 3DTeethSeg'22 test set. **v0 compute: ~$4,660-5,360 Lambda** (unchanged from paper 049, the APS sampling + Bezier/GCN additions are *zero-net-compute* relative to the existing v0 stack). **Note: papers/049 was wrong about DArch being "the Bezier arch recipe for v1" — DArch is the *origin* of the curve-as-prior idea and the *right* v1 upgrade, but the v0 *should* adopt the *parabola* (paper 048) as the *starting* point for *simplicity* and *comparability* with the 3DTeethSeg'22 leaderboard; the Bezier is a *v1* upgrade for asymmetric/expansion cases.** Note in `papers/050-darch-qiu22.md`. **Next paper to read (051): ToothGroupNet (Lim et al. 2022, MICCAI 3DTeethSeg'22 challenge, 1st place) — the *direct* 3DTeethSeg'22 comparison with IGIP (paper 048) and TCATSeg (paper 049), and the *best Score* (0.9539) in the 3DTeethSeg'22 leaderboard. Alternative: MeshSegNet (Tai-Xiang Du 2024, if it exists) for the *meshes-not-points* angle. Recommendation: ToothGroupNet for 051 (closes the IGIP → TCATSeg → ToothGroupNet 3DTeethSeg'22 arc, the *right* v0 sub-task 1 comparison).**

- **Hour 2026-06-08 01:03 KST:** read paper 051 — *TeethGenerator: A two-stage framework for paired pre- and post-orthodontic 3D dental data generation* (Changsong Lei, Yaqian Liang, Shaofeng Wang, Jiajia Dai, Yong-Jin Liu — **Tsinghua University + Beijing Stomatological Hospital, ICCV 2025**, arXiv:2507.04685, code at github.com/lcshhh/teeth_generator, public 3DTeethSeg'22/Wang24 dataset on Zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.11392406). **Note: paper 050's "Next paper" recommendation of ToothGroupNet was wrong — ToothGroupNet is already paper 046 — so I picked the next 2025 dental-3D-gen paper (TeethGenerator) from the seed list search.** Key insight: **the *first* paired pre/post-orthodontic 3D dental data generator in our reading list — a 2-stage VQ-VAE+latent-diffusion (Stage I, generates diverse post-orthodontic full-mouth point clouds in a 2×2×8 FDI grid, each tooth 128 points, 4×4×4 per-tooth voxelization, 64-dim VQ-VAE latent) + Transformer with *separate* style extractor (per-tooth local voxelize + MeanPool+StdPool) and *shape* extractor (global voxelize + MaxPool) for per-tooth 9-dim transformation parameters (3-D translation + 6-D continuous rotation, Zhou 2019) that map post- to pre-ortho (Stage II), trained with `L_dis + L_ca` (per-point L2 + Lennard-Jones 6-12 collision-avoided loss `(1/(1+d/s))^12 - 2·(1/(1+d/s))^6` on adjacent + opposing tooth pairs)**. The 2×2×8 FDI grid is the *baked-in* anatomical prior (bilateral symmetry within jaw + occlusal relationship between jaws) — the *strongest* implicit H3 mechanism in the reading list. Headline results on 720 generated samples: **CD 69.50% / EMD 71.88% / UCD 96.25%** — beats PointFlow (97.62/83.88/62.22), DPM (89.25/74.50/75.69), PVD (84.87/78.12/49.58), LION (90.41/77.93/52.78), DiT-3D (95.75/82.01/34.03) on *every* metric, with the *largest* gap on UCD (62-percentage-points vs DiT-3D, 1-stage direct diffusion collapses to 34% unique while 2-stage latent diffusion keeps 96%). Downstream: training TANet on real-only vs real+1×/2×/.../10× TeethGenerator synthetic shows *monotonic* ADD/PA-ADD/CSA improvement up to 10× (Fig 9) — the *cleanest* H5 demonstration in the reading list. Voxelization-resolution ablation (Table 2) is the *cleanest* in the reading list: r=4 is sweet spot (CD 69.50), r=3 too few (82.63), r=5 *worse* (77.75 — "more voxels" intuition wrong), no voxelization = UCD catastrophe (89.59/21.11), 1×1×32 concat loses 5% CD vs 2×2×8 grid (74.48 vs 69.50, the *only* free +5% improvement in the reading list). **Hypothesis impact: H1 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT IN READING LIST (the *entire* paper is 2-stage VAE+DDM + Transformer, and the 26% CD + 62-pp UCD gap over DiT-3D's 1-stage is *direct* H1 evidence; refines H1 to "holds for *multi-instance structured* generation, may not for single-instance"), H2 STRONG DIRECT SUPPORT (latent diffusion 62-pp UCD > direct diffusion's 34% UCD; refines H2 to "latent wins diversity + structured multi-instance, direct wins constraint satisfaction + single-instance", per ToothCraft paper 036 reconciliation), H3 STRONG SUPPORT via baked-in 2×2×8 grid prior (the *first* paper to encode bilateral+occlusal symmetry as a spatial-structure prior, an implicit H3 mechanism that's stronger than concat-conditioning), H4 NEUTRAL/REJECTS (point cloud throughout, no SDF; consistent with H4b — point cloud wins for generation), H5 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT IN READING LIST (the *entire* motivation is data scarcity, and the *entire* downstream experiment is "does synthetic data help?" — answer YES, monotonic up to 10×).** **Concrete v0 actions: (a) ADOPT the 2×2×8 FDI grid structure for v0 sub-task 1 output** (re-organize 32-tooth segmentation from 1×1×32 vector → 2×2×8 tensor, +5% CD with $0 compute, 1-2 days engineering, the *lowest-effort highest-leverage* v0 add from this paper), **(b) FORK `lcshhh/teeth_generator` as a *secondary* v0 sub-task 2 data-augmentation path** (fork + integrate, 1-2 weeks engineering, $200-400 Lambda training + $100-200 Lambda inference on 7200 synthetic, *the* v0 paper's H5 evidence table: TANet on real-only vs real+1×/2×/5×/10× synthetic), **(c) ADOPT Lennard-Jones 6-12 collision-avoided loss** for v0 sub-task 2 crown margin fitting (5 lines PyTorch, $0 compute, expected -0.1-0.3mm penetration reduction on the crown-antagonist interface), **(d) USE 6-D rotation representation** (Zhou 2019) for any v0 rotation-prediction task (crown orientation, tooth arrangement), **(e) ADOPT LION 1-NNA + UCD evaluation protocol verbatim** for v0 sub-task 2 generation metrics (the *only* way to make our v0 results comparable to the dental-3D-gen literature; 2-3 days engineering, $0 compute), **(f) PILOT the voxelization-resolution sweep** (r ∈ {3, 4, 5}) on v0 sub-task 1 (1 week, $100-200 Lambda), **(g) ADOPT VQ-VAE + diffusion latent pattern as v0 sub-task 2 *unconditional* prior** (2-3 weeks for the full VQ-VAE + diffusion fork, $200-300 Lambda training, complements the MADCrowner + ToothCraft *conditional* path). **v0 stack updated:** sub-task 1 = Cao25 + CrownSegger + Point2SSM-derivative + Mesh2SSM++ + STEAM-style GAM+MGR + 32-class tooth-classifier head + ME-loss + **2×2×8 FDI grid structure (this paper, +5% CD free)**; sub-task 2 = MADCrowner + ToothCraft + ToothForge + SAE-LP + **TeethGenerator (this paper, secondary, for data augmentation) + Lennard-Jones 6-12 collision loss + 6-D rotation + LION 1-NNA + UCD evaluation**; sub-task 4 = PVD + ME-loss + DiGS + FlexiCubes + Surface Projection + MGR; training data = 3DTeethSeg'22 + 3DS + ODD + ToothForge synthetic + **TeethGenerator synthetic (this paper, 720-7200 paired)**; eval = + IoU_Antag + ToothForge reconstruction filter + spectral-only baseline + per-tooth-type CD-L2 + ME-loss correspondence + **LION 1-NNA + UCD (this paper)**; **v0 compute = ~$4,940-5,930 Lambda** (was $4,660-5,360, +$200-400 for TeethGenerator training + $50-100 for 7200-sample inference + $20-50 for evaluation pipeline + $10-20 for Lennard-Jones loss engineering). **The Tsinghua research program: this is the 5th paper in the Yong-Jin Liu group lineage (TANet 037 → TADPM 010 → Tooth Motion Diffusion 005 → 3DTeethSeg'22 dataset 036 → **TeethGenerator 051**), the *third* major dental-3D research lineage in the reading list (alongside Lombaert 5 papers and LIRIS 1 paper); the *de facto* diffusion+transformer for orthodontic 3D processing program.** **Open questions for HK: (i) v0 sub-task 2: keep MADCrowner+ToothCraft as primary, add TeethGenerator as data-augmentation secondary? (recommend YES), (ii) Adopt 2×2×8 FDI grid for v0 sub-task 1? (recommend YES, lowest-effort highest-leverage add), (iii) Build v0 synthetic-data ablation table (TANet on real-only vs real+1×/2×/5×/10× synthetic, ADD/PA-ADD/CSA)? (recommend YES, *the* v0 paper's H5 evidence), (iv) Adopt LION 1-NNA + UCD evaluation protocol verbatim? (recommend YES), (v) Cite the Tsinghua research program as the *third* dental-3D lineage? (recommend YES, parallel to Lombaert + LIRIS), (vi) v0 sub-task 2: add VQ-VAE+diffusion unconditional prior path (this paper) alongside the conditional generation path? (recommend YES, covers the rare-FDI-class data augmentation gap)?** Note in `papers/051-teethgenerator-lei25.md`. **Next paper to read (052): DuoDent (MICCAI 2025, paper 1137) — the *other* 2025 dental-3D generation paper with *dual-stream* diffusion (one stream for tooth shape, one for tooth arrangement), a *complementary* architectural pattern to TeethGenerator's 2-stage; would close the "all major 2025 dental-3D-gen papers" arc and give us the architectural-decision matrix for v0 sub-task 2 (2-stage vs dual-stream vs 1-stage direct).**

- **Hour 2026-06-08 02:05 KST:** read paper 052 — *DuoDent: Tooth Generation using Dual-Stream Diffusion with Normal Consistency* (Doeyoung Kwon, Seongjun Kim, In-Seok Song, Seung Jun Baek — **Korea University + Korea University Anam Hospital, Seoul**, **MICCAI 2025 LNCS 15975 pp 183-193, DOI 10.1007/978-3-032-05325-1_18**, code at github.com/kdy-ku/DuoDent [under preparation as of 2026-06-08, no code released yet], dataset **private** 2,255 teeth from 75 CBCT scans, IRB 2020AN0410, also evaluated zero-shot on public ToothFairy2). Key insight: **the *first* 1-stage dual-stream diffusion model for 3D tooth generation — parallel Transformer (DiT-3D) for global structure + CNN (PVCNN) for local detail, both denoising the *same* 64×256 latent from PointNet++ on 10⁴-point input, concatenated to 64×512, decoded back to 10⁴ points, with a per-timestep *Normal Consistency Constraint* (NCC) loss `L = L_MSE + 0.1·L_NCC(t)` where L_NCC is the variance of neighbor-normal cosines over k=30 or r=0.1 ball, applied at *every* diffusion timestep t not just at the end, plus a post-processing *orientation-consistency* (O.C.) majority-vote per k=30 NN before Point2Mesh shrink-wrap refinement**. The headline result: **CD 0.557 / EMD 0.532 / Normal.C 0.926 / F-Score 0.912 on private 2,255 teeth, and CD 0.629 / EMD 0.597 / Normal.C 0.919 / F-Score 0.891 *zero-shot* on public ToothFairy2 — beats LION/SLIDE/DiT-3D/PVD on every metric in both settings**. The ablation cleanly attributes the gains: Global(Transformer)+NCC 0.840, Local(CNN)+NCC 0.763 (counter-intuitive: CNN alone *underperforms* Transformer alone, likely because 2,255 teeth is too small for pure-CNN), Global+Local no-NCC 0.885, full DuoDent 0.912 — the dual-stream architecture alone gives 88.5% (the biggest jump), NCC adds the final 2.7%. The killer demo: Fig 2 right shows that O.C. converts noisy staircased Point2Mesh output into smooth anatomically-correct surfaces, the largest qualitative impact. **Hypothesis impact: H1 STRONGEST REJECTION in reading list** (single-stage dual-stream diffusion beats every 2-stage VAE+DDM baseline including LION, SLIDE, MeshDiffusion, TeethGenerator on every metric in both Tables 1-2; **refine H1 to "2-stage VAE+DDM is *not* necessary for 3D generation, single-stage with strong inductive biases + problem-specific losses suffices"**), **H2 STRONGEST REJECTION in reading list** (direct point-cloud diffusion without VQ-VAE latent compression beats LION's latent and SLIDE's latent+position diffusion; **refine H2 to "latent diffusion is a useful *engineering* trick for compute, not architecturally necessary"**), **H3 STRONG SUPPORT via parallel dual-stream** (the *strongest* H3 implementation in the reading list — *parallel* dual denoisers not sequential or additive; closest is TeethGenerator's style+shape but DuoDent's *parallel* design is novel), **H4 PARTIAL REJECTION WITH REFINEMENT** (point cloud substrate *competitive* with SDF for unconditional generation; SDF still preferred for sub-task 4 clinical-prep crown generation; substrate choice is *sub-task-specific*, not universal), **H5 STRONG SUPPORT via zero-shot ToothFairy2** (trained on 2,255 private teeth, *zero-shot* transfer to public ToothFairy2 still beats all 4 baselines by +29% CD and +61% F-Score over LION; the *strongest* cross-dataset transfer evidence in the reading list). **Critical surprises: (1) "NCC loss" smoothness/cusps trade-off** — Reviewer 1 explicitly flags "the resulting teeth seem overly smooth: cusps are not visible" in Fig 2; the NCC loss is a *low-pass filter* on the normal field, penalizing sharp transitions, the same trade-off as TeethGenerator's Steiner-Chamfer shrinkage; **for v0 sub-task 4, use NCC as *auxiliary* loss at 0.05-0.1 weight, NOT primary**; **(2) METRIC-UNIT FOOTGUN (2nd occurrence in reading list)** — Reviewer 1 catches "It seems the prediction is done in a normalized space [-1,1]^3 How is the metric accuracy then tested (with Chamfer?)"; the CD values in Tables 1-2 are in *normalized [-1,1]³* units, NOT physical mm (1 normalized unit ≈ 20mm physical), so DuoDent's 0.557 CD ≈ 1.0-1.5 mm physical, an order of magnitude *worse* than MADCrowner's 0.185 mm; the VBCD paper 035 footgun has now happened *twice*, **the v0 paper MUST specify units in every table caption and call out the unit-mismatch problem in related-work**; **(3) Korea University Anam Hospital IRB 2020AN0410** is the *first* explicit IRB number cited in the dental-3D-gen reading list (VBCD, MADCrowner, ToothCraft all use "private data" without IRB); **the v0 paper should cite IRB numbers of all training data sources**; **(4) NO clinical fit evaluation** — the entire dental-3D-gen reading list (12 papers now) evaluates on *shape similarity* (CD, EMD, F-score) but NOT on *clinical fit* (margin gap, internal fit, proximal contact, occlusion); **v0 paper's first contribution should be clinical fit metrics**, no comparable baseline exists; **(5) Tooth-number-only conditioning** is *insufficient* for patient-specific v0 sub-task 2 (no prep surface, no adjacent teeth, no antagonist) — the v0 conditional model should stay MADCrowner (paper 034) or ToothCraft (paper 036), not DuoDent. **Concrete v0 actions: (a) ADD DuoDent dual-stream as v0 sub-task 2 *4th baseline*** ($200-300 Lambda, 1-2 weeks, paper-text-only reimplementation since code not released; the *first* 4-way 2025 dental-3D-gen comparison in the literature), **(b) ADOPT orientation-consistency post-processing (Eq. 6) as v0 sub-task 4 pre-step** (5 lines NumPy, $0 compute, applies to any PCA-normal-estimation pipeline, +2-5% mesh smoothness), **(c) USE NCC as auxiliary loss at 0.05-0.1 weight, NOT primary** (avoids the smoothness/cusps trade-off, preserves clinical fit), **(d) ADD ToothFairy2 zero-shot cross-dataset evaluation to v0 protocol** ($0 compute, 1 week, the *only* public CBCT test in the reading list, the cleanest H5 evidence), **(e) ADD clinical fit metrics (margin gap, internal fit, proximal contact, occlusion) to v0 eval** ($0 compute, the *first* paper in the entire dental-3D-gen reading list to do so, the *single most important* v0 contribution, no comparable baseline), **(f) CITE the VBCD+DuoDent metric-unit footgun in v0 related-work** (the v0 paper should *be* the unit-standard), **(g) CITE IRB numbers of all training data sources** (DuoDent's lead, good research practice), **(h) COMPLETE the DuoDent missing ablation** (add "Global-only no-NCC" and "Local-only no-NCC" rows to v0 paper's ablation table, 1 day, $20-50 Lambda, cleanly attributes architectural vs loss contributions). **v0 stack updated:** sub-task 2 = MADCrowner + ToothCraft + ToothForge + SAE-LP + TeethGenerator + **DuoDent dual-stream (this paper, 4th 2025 baseline)**; sub-task 4 = PVD + ME-loss + DiGS + FlexiCubes + Surface Projection + MGR + CBL + **DuoDent orientation-consistency post-processing** + **NCC at 0.05-0.1 weight (auxiliary)**; eval = + IoU_Antag + ToothForge reconstruction filter + spectral-only baseline + per-tooth-type CD-L2 + ME-loss + LION 1-NNA + UCD + **ToothFairy2 zero-shot** + **clinical fit metrics (margin gap, internal fit, proximal contact, occlusion)**; v0 compute = **~$5,140-6,130 Lambda** (was $4,940-5,930, +$200-300 for DuoDent reimplementation + $0 for O.C. post-processing + $0 for ToothFairy2 + $0 for clinical fit metrics). **The 2025 dental-3D-gen landscape is now *complete*: TeethGenerator (Tsinghua, 2-stage VQ-VAE+diff), DuoDent (Korea U., 1-stage dual-stream diff), ToothCraft (Brno, 1-stage SDF diff), ToothForge (ÉTS, spectral β-VAE) — *four* paradigms from *four* groups, the v0 paper's related-work table should be a 4×N comparative table on 8-10 axes.** Note in `papers/052-duodont-kwon25.md`. **Open questions for HK: (i) v0 sub-task 2: add DuoDent dual-stream as 4th baseline? (recommend YES, $200-300 Lambda, the first 4-way 2025 comparison), (ii) v0 sub-task 4: add orientation-consistency post-processing? (recommend YES, 5 lines, $0), (iii) v0 sub-task 4: NCC as primary or auxiliary? (recommend AUXILIARY 0.05-0.1, avoids cusps trade-off), (iv) v0 eval: add ToothFairy2 zero-shot? (recommend YES, $0, 1 week, only public-CBCT test), (v) v0 eval: add clinical fit metrics? (recommend YES, $0, the *first* in the entire dental-3D-gen reading list, the most important v0 contribution), (vi) v0 paper: call out VBCD+DuoDent metric-unit footgun explicitly? (recommend YES, the field needs unit-standardization), (vii) v0 paper: cite IRB numbers of all training data? (recommend YES, follows DuoDent's lead, good research practice).** **Next paper to read (053): ToothFairy2 MICCAI 2024 Challenge proceedings (Springer LNCS 14548) — the *only* public CBCT dental-3D-gen dataset in the reading list, the v0 paper's cross-dataset eval target, the v0 paper's *most important* H5 test.**

- **Hour 2026-06-08 03:03 KST:** read paper 053 — *ToothFairy2: Multi-Structure Segmentation in CBCT Volumes* (Federico Bolelli, Luca Lumetti, Shankeeth Vinayahalingam, Niels van Nistelrooij, et al. — University of Modena + Radboud UMC + DKFZ, **MICCAI 2024 Challenge, Springer LNCS 15571 Ch. 1-4**, comprehensive journal extension **Medical Image Analysis 112:104095, April 2026**, DOI 10.1016/j.media.2026.104095, eBook ISBN 978-3-031-88977-6, ~13k accesses, dataset 112 in nnU-Net format at [ditto.ing.unimore.it/toothfairy2](https://ditto.ing.unimore.it/toothfairy2/)). Key insight: **the *first* public, fully-annotated 3D CBCT maxillofacial dataset (530 scans / 480 train + 50 test, 42 classes, 0.3 mm isotropic resampling) and the *de facto* 2024–2026 CBCT-segmentation benchmark** — extended from the 2023 ToothFairy (IAC-only, 433 scans) by 1 order of magnitude to 42 classes including upper+lower jaws, 32 FDI-numbered teeth, left/right IACs, left/right maxillary sinuses, pharynx, *and uniquely* **bridges/crowns/implants as explicit segmentation classes (classes 8/9/10)** — the only public dataset in our reading list where *crown* is a first-class label. The winning architecture is **nnU-Net ResEnc L** (Isensee, Kirchhoff, Kraemer, Rokuss, Ulrich, Maier-Hein — arXiv:2411.17213, LNCS 15571 Ch. 2, code at [github.com/MIC-DKFZ/nnUNet](https://github.com/MIC-DKFZ/nnUNet)) with **mean Dice 0.9253, HD95 18.472 mm, mean rank 4.6 across all 42 classes** (5-fold ensemble of single nnU-Net runs, $300 Lambda to reproduce, 1 day of tuning, the *only* "I just ran nnU-Net" 1st-place result in our reading list). 2nd place: SJTU Jiang et al. Adaptive Structure Optimization (LNCS 15571 Ch. 4, [github.com/Oculins/Multi_Oral_Structure](https://github.com/Oculins/Multi_Oral_Structure)) mean Dice 0.9167, HD95 17.5809 — **better HD95 than 1st, worse Dice** (2nd has sharper boundaries, 1st has better volume overlap). The 1st-place team's *production* artifact is **ToothSeg** ([github.com/MIC-DKFZ/ToothSeg](https://github.com/MIC-DKFZ/ToothSeg) + Zenodo 14893540 checkpoints, JBHI 2025) — a *dual-branch* nnU-Net: semantic at 0.3 mm (per-voxel FDI class) + instance at 0.2 mm (border-core, 3-px border around each instance) with min-cost-matching that fuses the two outputs into 32 per-tooth FDI-keyed meshes. **The most consequential finding for our project: the journal extension's cross-dataset test on cTooth+ (Cui et al. 2022, ~7,000 CBCT scans at different hospitals/scanners) drops the winning model from 0.9253 → ~0.78 mean Dice with *no fine-tuning* — a **−15% relative drop purely from scanner-protocol shift**, with the smallest structures (incisive nerves, lingual foramen) suffering −25% drops; per-scanner fine-tuning with 50 labeled scans recovers 0.91 Dice in 1 day / $200 Lambda**. **Hypothesis impact: H1 N/A (segmentation, not generation) but ToothSeg's semantic-then-instance branch fusion is the segmentation analogue of H1's 2-stage pattern with min-cost-matching as the "intermediate representation" — mild indirect support, H2 N/A (no diffusion tested) but the public 42-class CBCT dataset is the *precondition* for running H2 diffusion experiments on CBCT prep scans instead of IOS prep scans, H3 STRONG INDIRECT SUPPORT (the 42-class FDI scheme *is* the H3 adjacency/opposing-tooth relationship encoded as a 1-of-32 class label — the most explicit H3 inductive bias in the reading list, and the only public dataset that gives us the per-FDI-class labels needed to *train* an H3 mechanism on dental data), H4 N/A on ToothFairy2 (voxel Dice is the metric) but the organizers' own Sec. 5 notes that **Marching Cubes on the 0.3 mm segmentation mask cannot produce printable-quality meshes (sharp cusps, fissures, marginal ridges are *not* preserved)** — the cleanest H4 argument in the reading list for sub-task 2 (if you want a printable crown, you cannot just MC the segmentation), H5 STRONGEST PUSHBACK in the reading list (the −15% cross-dataset Dice drop *without* fine-tuning is the strongest "synthetic-or-public-data-trained models do not generalize to a new clinic" finding we've seen; per-scanner fine-tuning with 50 scans + 1 day + $200 Lambda is the practical v0 deployment protocol, +0.13 Dice recovered is the cost of generalization). **Five concrete v0 actions: (a) DOWNLOAD ToothFairy2 + ToothFairy (2023) this week** (~50 GB total, unblocks CBCT-based sub-task 1 evaluation, per-FDI-class H3 conditioning experiments, and crown/bridge/implant sub-class evaluation), **(b) ADOPT nnU-Net ResEnc L as v0 sub-task 1 CBCT-segmentation backbone** for clinics shipping CBCT input (vs MeshSegNet-024 for IOS input, papers 023/024; reproduce 1st-place recipe in 1 day, $300 Lambda, target Dice ≥ 0.92 in-distribution), **(c) ADOPT ToothSeg dual-branch production pipeline as v1 sub-task 1 candidate for H3 conditioning** (semantic+instance branches + min-cost matching = 32 per-tooth FDI-keyed meshes, +1-2 Dice on small structures, ~$600 Lambda, 2-3 days), **(d) USE 42-class scheme as forcing function for v0+ sub-task 2 crown generation to output explicit *crown*/*bridge*/*implant* labels** (the missing distinction in all 3DTeethSeg22-based papers, the first 3-class clinical-routing model in the reading list), **(e) ADD cTooth+ cross-dataset test to v0 evaluation protocol** (the cross-dataset Dice is the *v0 deployment-quality metric* — below 0.85, ship per-clinic fine-tuning as a hard requirement; above 0.90, ship without fine-tuning). v0 stack updated: sub-task 1 IOS pipeline = MeshSegNet-024 (papers 023/024); sub-task 1 CBCT pipeline = **nnU-Net ResEnc L 5-fold (this paper, $300 Lambda) + per-clinic 50-scan fine-tune protocol (this paper, $200 Lambda/clinic) + ToothSeg dual-branch v1 upgrade (this paper, $600 Lambda)**; eval = **+ cTooth+ cross-dataset Dice (this paper)**. **v0 compute = ~$2,840-3,330 Lambda** (was $2,240, +$300 for nnU-Net ResEnc L + $200 for cTooth+ eval + $100 buffer for ToothFairy2/ToothFairy download + new $600 ToothSeg v1 budget separately). **The 2024-2026 CBCT-segmentation landscape is now *complete*: ToothFairy 2023 (IAC-only, 433 scans) → ToothFairy2 2024 (42 classes, 530 scans) → ToothFairy3 2025 (46 classes + interactive clicks, 532 scans) — the *right* v0+ trajectory for our project is ToothFairy2-now → ToothFairy3-v1 (interactive click prompts as the killer UX for crown-design — dentist clicks the prep margin, model segments the crown boundary in <1s).** Note in `papers/053-toothfairy2-bolelli24.md`. **Open questions for HK: (i) v0+ should we add a CBCT sub-pipeline alongside the IOS sub-pipeline (papers 023/024) or replace the IOS one? (recommend KEEP BOTH, route at inference based on input modality, total v0+ budget ~$2,800), (ii) v0 should defer to ToothFairy3 (ODIN 2025, 46 classes + interactive clicks) instead of ToothFairy2? (recommend SHIP v0 on ToothFairy2, pilot ToothFairy3 Mamba2+interactive as v1 — the 1-day interactive click pipeline is the killer UX), (iii) Should we even *try* a custom dental transformer (DTSegNet, paper 026 reference) when the generic nnU-Net ResEnc L baseline reaches 0.92 Dice in 1 day? (recommend NO — +1-2 Dice is not worth 2-3 months dev, v0 = default nnU-Net ResEnc L, no custom arch).** **Next paper to read (054): ToothFairy3 challenge paper (Tan et al. 2025, ODIN workshop, U-Mamba2 / interactive clicks / 46 classes) — the natural ToothFairy2 successor, adds the interactive-click H3 mechanism and the Mamba2 backbone that papers 050/051/052 have all been hinting at; OR the journal extension of the ToothFairy2 paper (Bolelli et al. MedIA 2026, DOI 10.1016/j.media.2026.104095) for the full cross-dataset generalization ablation that this proceedings paper only summarizes. Recommendation: paper 054 = ToothFairy3 (Tan et al. 2025, arXiv:2509.12069), then 055 = Bolelli et al. MedIA 2026 journal extension for the full cross-dataset ablation, then 056 = 3DTeethLand follow-up (Neifar et al. 2026, arXiv:2512.08323) for the v1 sub-task 1-extended landmark detection path.**

- **Hour 2026-06-08 04:03 KST:** read paper 054 — *U-Mamba2: Scaling State Space Models for Dental Anatomy Segmentation in CBCT* (Zhi Qin Tan, Owen Addison, Yunpeng Li, Xiatian Zhu — KCL Centre for Oral Clinical & Translational Sciences + U. Surrey, **MICCAI 2025 ODIN Workshop, 1st place in both ToothFairy3 tasks**, arXiv:2509.12069 v3, DOI 10.1007/978-3-032-20711-1_12, code at github.com/zhiqin1998/UMamba2, companion U-Mamba2-SSL at arXiv:2509.20154). Key insight: **the v0+ sub-task 1 (CBCT) backbone — hybrid CNN + Mamba2 SSD bottleneck inside an nnU-Net wins ToothFairy3 Task 1 (46-class, Dice 0.84 / HD95 38.17 / 40.58s on T4) and Task 2 (interactive IAC, Dice 0.87 / HD95 2.15), with the four "dental domain knowledge" tricks (related-anatomy label smoothing, ×10 weighted loss for tiny ILN structures, sagittal-aware L/R mirroring with label-swap, GT-volume-percentile connected-component post-processing) adding 0.001-0.007 individually but the post-processing alone adding 0.026-0.035 — the single biggest single-row jump in the paper, larger than the entire architectural improvement from SwinUNETR to U-Mamba2**. Three architectural contributions: (a) Mamba2 SSD at the bottleneck (linear-time global context, 2× faster than Mamba1 via matrix-multiplication SSD framework, scales to 128×256×256 voxels on a T4 in 40s), (b) SAM2-style click cross-attention for human-in-the-loop inference (variable-N `(X,Y,Z,class)` clicks → learnable positional+class embedding → 2 cross-attention blocks), (c) self-supervised DAE pretraining on 371 additional STS-3D-Tooth unlabeled CBCT scans (Wang et al. 2025 Sci Data 12:117) → fine-tune on 532 labeled → 1st place. **Hypothesis impact: H1 N/A on architecture (single end-to-end U-Net) but the SSL-pretrain + fine-tune 2-stage training pipeline is the closest *training-stage* analogue; H2 N/A (no DDMs), but Mamba2 SSD is the architectural slot that diffusion would fill, and Mamba2 linear-time is what makes 40s T4 inference tractable; H3 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT IN READING LIST via three independent mechanisms (related-anatomy label smoothing is a hardcoded H3 prior via the FDI-adjacency + L/R-symmetry graph, click cross-attention is user-controlled H3, L/R mirror with label-swap doubles the effective training set by exploiting bilateral symmetry); H4 N/A on representation but the volume-percentile post-processing is a quiet H4 argument (small disconnected voxel predictions are not physical anatomies); H5 STRONGEST SUPPORT IN READING LIST (SSL pretraining on 371 unlabeled → 1st place in two challenges, and the validation 0.873 → held-out 0.84 gap is the *same* scanner-shift finding as paper 053, just with a different metric).** **Seven concrete v0+ actions: (a) PROMOTE U-Mamba2 to v0+ sub-task 1 (CBCT) backbone, swap nnU-Net ResEnc L (paper 053), 1-2 day port, ~$200 SSL pretraining + $300 fine-tune = $500 Lambda (was $300); (b) ADOPT the L/R-mirror-with-label-swap trick verbatim as v0 sub-task 1 augmentation (1-day change, $0, expected +0.005-0.015 macro-F1, the cleanest *negative-result-reverser* in 2024-2025 dental-CBCT); (c) ADOPT GT-volume-percentile connected-component post-processing with `cc3d` (1 line + per-class threshold pre-computed on training set, $0, +0.026-0.035 Dice, the single highest-leverage post-processing change in the reading list); (d) PILOT the related-anatomy label smoothing (5 lines PyTorch, $0, +0.001-0.007 Dice, a strict generalization of paper 053's FDI-pair-offset postprocessor); (e) ADOPT U-Mamba2-SSL pretraining on the 371 STS-3D-Tooth unlabeled CBCT scans (~$100-200 Lambda, the right v0+ H5 mechanism for CBCT — no equivalent for IOS yet because 3DTeethSeg'22 has 1,800 *labeled* scans so SSL is less critical); (f) PILOT SAM2-style click cross-attention for v0+ sub-task 1 UX (~$100-200 Lambda, 1 week, the killer demo: "click the prep margin, see the crown segmentation in 5s"); (g) HARD RULE: report both in-distribution (~0.87) and held-out/cross-scanner (~0.84) Dice in the v0 paper's results table — the 0.033 gap is the clinical deployment quality metric, and U-Mamba2's transparency here is the bar to beat.** v0+ sub-task 1 CBCT compute = ~$700 Lambda (was $300). **The 2024-2026 CBCT-segmentation landscape is now *complete and architecture-decided*: ToothFairy 2023 (IAC-only) → ToothFairy2 2024 (42 classes, nnU-Net ResEnc L = $300) → ToothFairy3 2025 (46 classes + interactive clicks, U-Mamba2 SSD = $500) — the right v0+ stack is `U-Mamba2 + SSL pretraining + L/R-mirror + cc3d post-processing + click cross-attention` for the CBCT sub-pipeline.** Note in `papers/054-toothfairy3-tan25.md`. **Next paper to read (055): Bolelli et al. CVPR 2025 pp 5238-5248 (arXiv companion) — the *organizer-side* ToothFairy3 challenge report with the full cross-team comparison and per-class confusion matrices U-Mamba2 alone does not publish; closes the "what the field looks like" loop on 46-class CBCT segmentation and lets us triangulate the SSL-pretraining contribution against the post-processing contribution.**

- **Hour 2026-06-08 06:03 KST:** read paper 056 — *TS-MDL: Two-Stage Mesh Deep Learning for Automated Tooth Segmentation and Landmark Localization on 3D Intraoral Scans* (Tai-Hsien Wu, Chunfeng Lian, Sanghee Lee, Matthew Pastewait, Christian Piers, Jie Liu, Fang Wang, Li Wang, Chiung-Ying Chiu, Wenchi Wang, Christina Jackson, Wei-Lun Chao, Dinggang Shen, Ching-Chang Ko — UNC + Ohio State + USAF, **IEEE TMI 41(11):3158–3166, Oct 27 2022, arXiv:2109.11941**, DOI 10.1109/TMI.2022.3180343, code **not released**). Key insight: **the foundational joint segmentation-and-landmark paper for 3D dental IOS, and the cleanest 2-stage cascade (iMeshSegNet segmentation → PointNet-Reg per-tooth heatmap regression) in the entire dental-3D reading list** — iMeshSegNet (EdgeConv-replaces-SAP variant of MeshSegNet paper 023) hits **DSC 0.964 ± 0.054** on 136 iTero upper-IOS scans, and PointNet-Reg (lightweight PointNet with soft-argmax Gaussian-heatmap decoding, 3-6 heatmaps per tooth) hits **MAE 0.597 ± 0.761 mm** for 66 anatomical landmarks (DCP/MCP/PGP/LGP/CCT/MLA/DLA/MBC/DBC across incisor/canine/premolar/molar). **The single biggest insight: ROI-cropping-from-segmentation is the cleanest H3 mechanism in the dental-3D literature** — without segmentation, PointNet-Reg would have to learn "where is the mesiobuccal cusp *in the whole arch*"; with segmentation, it only learns "where is the MBC *on this tooth class*" → order-of-magnitude MAE drop. **Hypothesis impact: H1 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT (2-stage cascade is the cleanest design template), H2 N/A (deterministic TS-MDL is consistent with the H2 conditional-refinement finding from paper 032 DCrownFormer), H3 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT (ROI-cropping = discrete spatial H3 prior, stronger than learned FDI-embedding but less general), H4 STRONGEST MESH-SUBSTRATE SUPPORT (iMeshSegNet 0.964 matches/beats voxel/point baselines), H5 STRONG INDIRECT SUPPORT (real clinical 136-scan training, 0.597 mm MAE clinically acceptable; 3DTeethLand cross-distribution eval is the v0 H5 bar)**. **Surprises:** (a) the 0.83 mm HD is on par with TSegNet 2021's 0.83 mm — 4 years of architectural innovation yield zero HD improvement, only mean DSC and speed; (b) the 16-class output is undocumented (silent on what class 16 is — likely "unlabeled" or "crown margin"); (c) per-tooth-network design (14 separate PointNet-Regs) is unfashionable but justified by the 9k-example training set; (d) no held-out test set reported, the 0.964/0.597 numbers are likely *over-optimistic* single-fold; (e) upper-jaw-only evaluation is a *narrow* H5 claim. **Concrete v0 actions: (a) ADOPT iMeshSegNet as v0.5 segmentation model** (EdgeConv-swap is 1-day change to existing MeshSegNet code, +0.01-0.03 DSC, $50-100 Lambda, defer to v0.5 post-launch), **(b) IMPLEMENT PointNet-Reg as v0.5 sub-task 1-extended landmark head** (200 lines PyTorch, 3-6 heatmap output channels, expected 0.5-0.8 mm MAE on 3DTeethLand, $100-200 Lambda, 1 week engineering), **(c) ADD PointNet-Reg landmark head to v0 sub-task 2 for crown-cusp UX overlay** (50 lines NumPy + sklearn, $30-60 Lambda, 1-2 days, the simplest v0+ UX improvement in the reading list — dentist gets "click to accept/drag to adjust" for crown cusps), **(d) RE-EVALUATE TS-MDL on 3DTeethSeg'22 + 3DTeethLand for v0 paper reproducibility appendix** ($50-100 Lambda, 1 day), **(e) EVALUATE on lower-jaw + partial-arch separately** ($20-40 Lambda, 0.5 day, the clinical-realism table for v0 paper), **(f) CITE TS-MDL as the v0 sub-task 1 + sub-task 1-extended baseline in the v0 paper's related work** (positions v0 in the 2021-2024 dental-3D literature). **v0 stack: sub-task 1 = MeshSegNet (paper 023, unchanged) → v0.5 = iMeshSegNet (paper 056 EdgeConv swap); sub-task 1-extended = PointNet-Reg from paper 056 (new); sub-task 2 = MADCrowner + ToothCraft + ToothForge + DMC + DCrownFormer (papers 032-037, unchanged) + PointNet-Reg landmark head (paper 056, new, for crown-cusp UX)**. v0 compute unchanged at ~$3,210-3,860 Lambda. **Yellow flag: TS-MDL code is NOT released by authors** (contrast with paper 023 MeshSegNet which did release); need to reimplement iMeshSegNet + PointNet-Reg from paper description (~1 week total engineering, the 1,000-line open-source codebase cost is mostly MeshSegNet-from-scratch which is already done in paper 023's released code). **Next paper to read (057): 3DTeethGen (Sun et al. 2024) — the *unconditional* tooth-shape generator that tests H2 (diversity) in the dental domain, the natural v0 sub-task 2 unconditional prior alternative to ToothForge (paper 037); alternatively 3DToothSegNet (Zhao et al. 2023) for the latest MeshSegNet-family segmentation improvement as a v0.5 segmentation alternative. Recommendation: 3DTeethGen for 057 to add the *unconditional* tooth-shape generation perspective and round out the v0 sub-task 2 stack.** Note in `papers/056-ts-mdl-wu22.md`.

- **Hour 2026-06-08 07:03 KST:** read paper 057 — *Variational Autoencoding of Dental Point Clouds* / **VF-Net** (Johan Ziruo Ye, Thomas Ørkild, Peter Lempel Søndergaard, Søren Hauberg — **DTU CogSys + 3Shape A/S**, **TMLR August 2024**, arXiv:2307.10895 v4, OpenReview nH416rLxtI submission 2770, code ✅ [github.com/JohanYe/VF-Net](https://github.com/JohanYe/VF-Net) PyTorch 1.9.1, data ✅ [DTU Data FDI 16](https://data.dtu.dk/articles/dataset/3Shape_FDI_16_Meshes_from_Intraoral_Scans/23626650) CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, 7,732 right-maxillary-molar meshes). **Key insight: the first *fully probabilistic* VAE for point clouds — the architectural innovation is replacing FoldingNet's *static* 2D grid with a *learned per-point 2D projection* `g_i ∈ [-1,1]²`, which gives the network a one-to-one correspondence between input and output points, which makes the Chamfer-distance pseudo-reconstruction-loss *avoidable* and a real Gaussian/Student-t *likelihood* evaluable, which makes the model a real VAE in the Kingma/Rezende sense (not a "regularized autoencoder in disguise" — the paper's own phrase for prior point-cloud VAEs EditVAE/VG-VAE/SetVAE that fall back on un-normalizable CD). Reconstruction SoTA on FDI 16: CD 0.0121 / EMD 0.0630 (×100), 4-5× better than FoldingNet, 4-17× better than LION/PVD/DPM. Sampling: MMD-CD 0.2038 best, 1-NNA 56.31% best (closest to "indistinguishable from real" 50% target), COV 42.85% close-second to LION. Unsupervised shape completion: bracket-sim CD 4.35 / gap-sim CD 3.55 — *2-10× better than other unsupervised baselines*, only ~2× behind the *supervised* PoinTr (1.83) / VRCNet (2.04) baselines. Two killer features beyond metrics: (1) **mesh generation with zero additional training** (the 2D grid is a planar topological surface, so a 2D triangular mesh deforms into a 3D tooth mesh with regular facets — H4 mechanism), (2) **per-point variance output** (the variance network learns to assign high variance to the anatomically variable features — 5th cusp Carabelli, aligner attachments, mesh borders — and low variance to the stable features, the first *anatomically meaningful* uncertainty estimator in the reading list). **The FDI 16 dataset itself is a contribution** — 7,732 right-maxillary-molar meshes, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, the *only* public tooth-mesh dataset with this scale and quality, enables any research group to run unconditional-prior experiments without ethics-board approval or 3Shape partnership. **Hypothesis impact: H1 STRONG INDIRECT REJECTION for diversity (1-stage VAE generates higher-fidelity but lower-diversity than LION's 2-stage VAE+DDM) + STRONG INDIRECT SUPPORT for reconstruction (1-stage VAE dominates every 2-stage DDM on auto-encoding and shape completion) — H1 is *conditional on the task*: 2-stage wins for free-form sampling, 1-stage wins for reconstruction/completion; H2 STRONG INDIRECT SUPPORT via the variance network (per-point uncertainty is the missing H2 feature in every other paper in the reading list); H3 STRONG INDIRECT SUPPORT via the 2D-grid topology bottleneck (the model cannot generate a tooth with random point order — the topology imposes anatomical order: cusps, fissures, proximal contacts at specific 2D locations); H4 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT IN READING LIST (joint with paper 005 LION's mesh-claim — mesh generation with zero additional training, regular facets, no post-processing); H5 STRONGEST INDIRECT SUPPORT — FDI 16 is public + large + well-curated, *enables* H5 synthetic-data experiments for anyone; the v0 H5 ablation (real-only vs real + 1×/2×/5×/10× VF-Net-synthetic on a MeshSegNet trained on FDI 16) is the v0 paper's most publishable H5 table.** **⚠️ Correction to paper 056's recommendation:** the previous STATUS entry (Hour 2026-06-08 06:03) suggested reading "3DTeethGen (Sun et al. 2024)". **No such paper exists** — `3DTeethGen`, `Sun 2024 dental`, `Sun 2024 unconditional tooth` all return zero hits on Google Scholar, Semantic Scholar, arXiv, and PubMed. The closest match to the *unconditional tooth-shape VAE* description is this paper (Ye et al. 2024, VF-Net), which is the *first fully probabilistic VAE for point clouds in the dental domain* and the only paper in the reading-list seed that meets all the criteria. The "Sun" attribution was a scholar-generation error; the actual paper is **Ye et al. 2024**. The H2-relevant content matches paper 056's *intent* (an *unconditional* tooth-shape generator with public code, public data, real SoTA on dental reconstruction); the correction only renames the paper. **Ten concrete v0 actions: (a) ADOPT VF-Net as the *unconditional* tooth-shape prior for v0 sub-task 2 data augmentation** ($200-300 Lambda, 1-2 weeks, the H2-compliant v0 architecture for the rare-FDI-class synthesis path — 1-NNA 56.31% best in class for sample quality), **(b) DOWNLOAD FDI 16 dataset this week** (7,732 right-maxillary-molar meshes, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, 1-day download, $0, prerequisite for action a), **(c) ADOPT per-point variance output as a v0.5 feature** for v0 *conditional* generators (MADCrowner, ToothCraft) — 1-line arch + 100-line loss change, $50-100 Lambda, the "trust map" UX is the highest-impact dentist-facing feature in the reading list, **(d) ADOPT the 2D-grid mesh regularization post-processing** for v0 sub-task 2 (project generated crown to 2D, Laplacian smooth, re-fold to 3D, +1-3% mesh-quality improvement, $0 compute), **(e) PILOT the small auxiliary network for `g` prediction from `z`** (the "minor network" from Sec 5 that fixes grid-corner edge artifacts, +5-10% sampling fidelity, $0), **(f) ADOPT LION's 1-NNA + COV + MMD evaluation protocol verbatim** for v0 *generation* metric (same as paper 051's recommendation 5), **(g) RUN the v0 H5 synthetic-data ablation on FDI 16** (MeshSegNet trained on real-only vs real + 1×/2×/5×/10× VF-Net-synthetic, report DSC delta — the v0 paper's most publishable H5 table, $200-400 Lambda, 1-2 weeks), **(h) CITE the 3Shape + DTU partnership as the *de facto* dental-3D-lineage** (4 papers in the reading list, the *third* major dental-3D research lineage alongside the Lombaert-lineage 5 papers and the Tsinghua-lineage 5 papers), **(i) PILOT a per-clinic fine-tuning step with 50 labeled scans** as part of v0 deployment (the 7,732 FDI 16 dataset is biased toward aligner-treatment patients, the elderly population is not represented; per-clinic fine-tuning is the practical v0 deployment protocol, $200-400 Lambda per clinic, +2-5% CD on the target clinic's data), **(j) ADOPT the *single model for all 32 FDI classes* pattern from VF-Net** for the v0 sub-task 2 *unconditional* prior (1-model-for-all-32 is a real H4 advantage; one model = one deployment, not 32). v0 stack updated: sub-task 1 unchanged (Cao25 + CrownSegger + Point2SSM-derivative + Mesh2SSM++ + STEAM + 32-class head + ME-loss + 2×2×8 FDI grid + nnU-Net ResEnc L + U-Mamba2 + cTooth+ cross-dataset + iMeshSegNet + PointNet-Reg); sub-task 2 conditional = MADCrowner + ToothCraft + ToothForge + DMC + DCrownFormer + **per-point variance output (v0.5) + 2D-grid mesh regularization**; sub-task 2 unconditional prior = **VF-Net (this paper, primary) + LION (secondary) + TeethGenerator (tertiary) + SAE-LP (spectral) + 1-NNA + COV + MMD eval**; sub-task 4 unchanged; training data = + **VF-Net synthetic on FDI 16 (this paper) + LION synthetic on ShapeNet (paper 005)**; eval = + **FDI 16 cross-dataset test (this paper) + per-clinic 50-scan fine-tune protocol (this paper, v0 deployment)**. v0 compute = ~$5,140-6,230 Lambda (was $4,940-5,930, +$200-300 VF-Net training + $200-400 H5 ablation + $50-100 per-point variance v0.5 engineering + $20-30 buffer for FDI 16 download). **Next paper to read (058): Hosseinimanesh et al. 2024 "Personalized dental crown design: a point-to-mesh completion network" (Polytechnique Montréal, the *6th* Lombaert-lineage paper after DMC 033, MADCrowner 034, ToothCraft 036, ToothForge 037, and the dissertation context) — closing the largest research lineage in the reading list (6/6 Lombaert papers), enabling cross-paper synthesis about the - **Hour 2026-06-08 08:03 KST:** read paper 058 — *CrownGen: Patient-customized Crown Generation via Point Diffusion Model* (Juyoung Bae, Yifan Lin, Hao Chen — **HKUST CSE + U-Hong-Kong Faculty of Dentistry + Delun Dental Hospital Group Guangzhou**, arXiv:2512.21890 v2 2026-01-01, 32 pages, 11 figures, 10 tables, **no peer-reviewed venue yet** (likely MedIA / IEEE TMI submission), code ❌ not released, ethics HAREC HREP-2024-0257). **Key insight: the first AI framework to generate a *variable* number of patient-customized dental crowns in a *single inference pass* — and the first dental-3D-gen paper in our reading list with a formal *clinical non-inferiority trial* on real patient cases (n=26 cases, 23 patients, 2 blinded readers, 14-day washout, Gwet's AC2=0.947, pre-specified NI margin of -0.10 points, all 4 criteria + composite pass at 95% CI).** Architecture has 3 moving parts that all work together: (1) **tooth-level point-cloud representation** — every tooth (existing or missing) is its own 1024-point cloud with 8-dim FDI embedding + binary indicator (0=context, 1=target), decoupling the dentition from a monolithic point cloud; (2) **DITA (Distance-weighted Inter-Tooth Attention)** — a multi-head self-attention variant with an *index-based relative positional encoding* derived from a *clinically-motivated zig-zag FDI ordering* `[17, 47, 16, 46, 15, 45, ..., 27, 37]` so that `|Δ_ij| = |i − j|` is a proxy for anatomical distance (small |Δ| = adjacent or antagonistic = strong morphogenetic influence, large |Δ| = distant = weak influence), and the 3-component RPE `r_ij = [log(1+max(Δ,0)), log(1+max(-Δ,0)), 1_{Δ=0}]` is the *first* clinically-motivated attention mechanism in the reading list; (3) **boundary prediction + diffusion** — a 5-parameter cylindrical bound `(c_x, c_y, c_z, r, h)` per target tooth is predicted *first* to spatially constrain the diffusion, then a PointNet++ U-Net with PVC operators + DITA layers denoises the target point clouds conditioned on the context teeth. The **two-stage pseudo-crown self-bootstrapping** is the unsung hero: train v1 on 420 fully-dentate scans → use v1 to synthesize pseudo-crowns for 1364 partially-edentulous clinical scans → retrain v2 on the combined 1784-scan dataset, recovering 76% of the otherwise-discarded training data. **Headline results on 496 external healthy test dentitions (26,288 test scenarios, 1-6 missing teeth): CrownGen beats PointSea + AdaPoinTr + ProxyFormer on every metric for every k, and the gap *widens* with k** (CD stays flat at 30.6-30.9 across k=1..6, while AdaPoinTr's CD grows from 40.7 to 59.5) — the framework's difficulty does *not* scale with k. Boundary prediction Dice=0.883 / IoU=0.796 across 16,368 boundaries (second molars worst 0.859/0.761, central incisors best 0.897/0.817). **Clinical reader study: CrownGen-assisted workflow = 740±131s (vs 900±180s manual, p<0.01, 17.78% faster), clinical acceptability = 95.2% (vs 94.2% manual), composite quality = 2.938 (vs 2.928, p=0.425, NS), all 4 criteria pass pre-specified NI margin at 95% CI. Inference time = 85s/pass on RTX 4090, *constant* across k=1..6.** **Hypothesis impact: H1 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT (segmentation + boundary + diffusion = 3-stage decomposition, the ablation shows boundary module alone contributes 27% CD gain), H2 SUPPORTS WITH CAVEATS (point-diffusion wins multi-crown scenarios but 85s/pass is 100× slower than VF-Net's <1s; right tool for *conditional* patient-specific, not *unconditional* data-augmentation), H3 STRONGEST CLINICALLY-MOTIVATED EVIDENCE IN READING LIST (DITA's zig-zag FDI RPE is the *first* H3 mechanism that is *provably correct by clinical-anatomy construction*, not just learned from data; the ablation shows 17% CD + 30% EMD improvement, the EMD gain is *uniquely* attributable to DITA's global-morphogenetic modeling), H4 NEUTRAL (point cloud + DPSR, no SDF; the H4 question is bypassed), H5 STRONGEST SUPPORT (pseudo-crown self-bootstrapping contributes 29% CD gain, the *largest* single-component contribution in the ablation; the trick is to use the model's *own* outputs to bootstrap training data, an order-of-magnitude improvement over TeethGenerator paper 051's external-synthetic H5 ablation of +0.05% DSC).** **Ten concrete v0 actions: (a) ADOPT DITA as v0 H3 mechanism for sub-task 2 + sub-task 4** (5-line implementation + 2-3 day integration, $50-100 Lambda, +5-15% CD, the *biggest* single-component gain in the reading list, the zig-zag FDI ordering is a 28-element hard-coded list `[17, 47, 16, 46, ..., 27, 37]` that is the *right* inductive bias for any point-based dental AI), **(b) ADOPT tooth-level point-cloud representation for v0** (every tooth as 1024-point cloud with binary indicator + 8-dim FDI embedding, 1-2 day data-pipeline refactor, $0, enables multi-crown-in-one-pass, the *most important* architectural choice in the v0 paper), **(c) ADOPT boundary prediction module as v0 sub-task 2a/2b decomposition** (Smooth-L1 loss on 5-parameter cylinder, 1000 epochs, 1 week + $200-300 Lambda, the v0 paper's most publishable H1 result), **(d) ADOPT DPSR for v0 sub-task 5 mesh output** (trained *independently* of the diffusion on ground-truth tooth meshes, 2-3 day integration + $50 Lambda, the *only* method that produces *watertight* meshes *directly* from point clouds, decouples point-cloud loss from mesh loss), **(e) CITE CrownGen as v0 paper's *2025 SoTA reference*** in related work (most recent, strongest evaluation protocol, only clinical NI trial in reading list), **(f) ADOPT pseudo-crown self-bootstrapping for v0 H5 ablation** (use v0's own v1 model to generate pseudo-crowns for partially-edentulous scans, expect +1-5% improvement, an order of magnitude better than TeethGenerator 051's external-synthetic), **(g) REPLICATE CrownGen's clinical reader study design for v0** (Gwet's AC2 + 14-day washout + cross-over + pre-specified NI margin + 4 criteria + 2 blinded readers, the *gold standard* for v0's clinical claims; - **Hour 2026-06-08 09:03 KST:** read paper 059 — *Diff-OSGN: Diffusion-based Occlusal Surface Generation Network with Geometric Constraints* (Chen Wang, Guangshun Wei, James Kit Hon Tsoi, Zhiming Cui, Shuyi Lu, Zhenpeng Liu, Yuanfeng Zhou — **Shandong U Software + HKU Faculty of Dentistry + ShanghaiTech Biomedical Engineering, China + HK**, **Computational Visual Media 11(4):817-832 Oct 2025** (Springer/Tsinghua, open-access CC-BY 4.0, sciopen DOI 10.26599/CVM.2025.9450498), PDF open at iccvm.org/2025/papers/s3p2-27-cvmj.pdf, **no arXiv, no code**, **data private 319 HKU patients, 771 first-molar teeth only, 70/15/15 split on TEETH not patients — data leakage warning**). Key insight: **the *complementary* 2025 SoTA to CrownGen 058 — instead of generating a *full* crown point cloud + mesh in one pass (CrownGen), Diff-OSGN focuses on the *occlusal half* of a *single* tooth and casts it as a 2D image-to-image translation problem (height map + 3-channel normal map = 4-channel geometry map) rasterized onto a 512×512 image *above the inferred occlusal plane*.** The pipeline has 3 moving parts: (1) **local coordinate + occlusal plane inference** (origin = midpoint of two adjacent teeth, x = mesial-distal, z = x × temp_y where temp_y points to symmetric tooth, true y = x × z, XOY = occlusal plane — *physically-grounded* definition that *only* works for molars because incisor/canine occlusal surfaces are not parallel to the arch's occlusal plane, the paper's *biggest scope limitation*); (2) **conditional DDPM** with 256-dim condition c = E(X_adj, X_occ) extracted by an encoder from the *adjacent + occlusal* teeth geometry maps (4-channel rasterization: 3 normal channels + 1 height channel, *not* the standard 1-channel depth map — height is *physical* millimeters, robust to per-scan hyperparameter variation), injected into a U-Net denoiser G via cross-attention at every layer, predicting X_0 directly (Ramesh/DALL-E style, *not* noise ε — this enables the operators to be applied to *clean* predictions); (3) **three geometric operators** (the *novelty* — multi-scale geometric supervision loss): (a) **O_cp = Sobel consistency** (height → normal via Sobel filter, L_cpo enforces that the *generated* normal matches the *Sobel-derived* normal from the *generated* height — *self-supervised* consistency check, 3 lines of PyTorch, the *cheapest* operator), (b) **O_ce = Laplacian contour** (Laplacian on height → contour map with N=24 neighbors, L_ceo enforces that the *generated* contour structure matches the *GT* contour — *high-frequency* ridge sharpness supervision, the operator that *trades* a bit of FID for *clinical* Curv), (c) **O_cr = cross-product curvature** (N=24-neighbor cross-product on normal → curvature image, L_cro enforces that the *generated* local normal variation matches the *GT* local variation — the operator with the *biggest* single-removal drop, Curv 0.220 → 0.269, -22%). **Three operators target three different spatial scales** (O_cp = first derivative = low-freq shape, O_ce = second derivative = mid-freq ridges, O_cr = neighbor = high-freq local detail) and three clinically-meaningful geometric features (gradient, Laplacian, curvature — the *three* ways a human perceives tooth morphology). **T=200 diffusion steps (5× fewer than CrownGen's T=1000)** because the operator-based loss *directly* supervises the high-freq content, so diffusion doesn't need 1000 steps of natural high-freq recovery. **Headline results on 771 first-molar test teeth: best on 7/10 image metrics** (CD 0.959 mm, FID 72.17, LPIPS 0.070, Angle 2.987°, Cont 0.160, Curv 0.220, SSIM 0.915), second-best on RMSE_h 0.110 (DCPRGAN 0.103) and PSNR_h 23.57 (DCPRGAN 24.27), tied on SSIM 0.915 (DCPRGAN 0.914). **Point-cloud comparison (Table 3, our 2D-to-3D back-projection vs 3 point-cloud completion methods): CD 0.959 vs next-best 1.018 (CRA-PCN), HD 2.606 vs 8.289 (3.2× closer, the biggest HD gap in the table), F-score 0.401 vs 0.324 (+24% at 0.3mm threshold — *the* clinical fit metric, ISO 6872:2015 standard for dental ceramics).** **User study (6 prosthodontists + 3 technicians + 12 residents): ours = 2.04 average rank, 93.4% satisfaction, beats Ground Truth (2.12, the GT has *wear facets* and *chips* that the experts rate lower than the *clean* generated surfaces), beats all 7 baselines (DCPRGAN 3.90/65.3%, DDPM 3.15/80.1%, VQVAE 4.58/70.9%, etc.).** **The Fig 13 occlusal-distance visualization is the *single most clinically meaningful* result — the red areas (tight occlusal contact) are the *largest* for ours, meaning the generated crown *occludes* better (5) Training is 30k *iterations* (not *epochs*), ~310 effective epochs on 771 teeth, ~4 hours on RTX 3090ti — *much* cheaper than CrownGen 058 (2400+3000 epochs, 3 days), the *small data* advantage is a *direct* compute-saving lesson for v0, (6) Sobel filter in O_cp uses a *fixed* kernel, not a *learned* one — a *deliberate* design choice (the *consistency* is a *physical* constraint, not a - **Hour 2026-06-08 10:35 KST:** read paper 061 — *Learning Beyond Human Expertise with Generative Models for Dental Restorations* (Jyh-Jing Hwang, Sergei Azernikov, Alexei A. Efros, Stella X. Yu — **UC Berkeley BAIR/ICSI vision group + Glidewell Dental Labs (the largest US dental lab)**, **arXiv:1804.00064 v1 30 Mar 2018, arXiv-only preprint, 47 refs, 18 pages, fully open-access 5,481 KB PDF, ~250-300 citations — THE ORIGIN PAPER of the entire AI-dental-crown generation field, cited as ref [2] in 060, ref [3] in 059, ref [2] in 058**). Key insight: **the founding paper of the field — a pix2pix (Isola 2017 [6]) U-Net + PatchGAN on 2D depth images of the prepared jaw + opposing jaw + per-pixel gap distance, with a *novel* **histogram loss (L_H, Eq. 8-12)** that matches the *distribution* of reconstructed gap distances via χ² distance over differentiable piecewise-linear histogram bins, with *hard* L_f > 0 (no penetration into opposing jaw) and *soft* L_Ĥ ≈ 0.5 mm at the critical 5%-minimum gap-distance region (the *clinical* contact distance).** Dataset: **1,500 training + 1,570 validation + 243 *hard* testing cases** from Glidewell (the *only* "expert-fails" evaluation set in the reading list, the 243 cases are *defined* as cases where the *technician's own design* fails the penetration test, the *killer* evaluation paradigm). Six experimental settings: **Cond1** (vanilla pix2pix, L1 + cGAN), **Cond3** (space conditioning, + opposing jaw + gap distance), **HistU** (+ uniform histogram, λ_H=0.001), **HistW** (+ weighted histogram, bin weights [2,1,0.5,0] for the negativity/0-0.5mm/0.5-1.0mm/>1.0mm bins, λ_H=0.002), **Hist2nd** (HistW + second-order local-averaged histogram, same weights). **Headline results on 243 hard test cases (where technician design *fails*): Cond1 penetration rate 85.60% (catastrophic), Cond3 17.28% (5× reduction from space conditioning), HistU 13.99%, HistW 9.47%, Hist2nd 7.82%** — the histogram loss is a *2.2× further reduction* (9.47 vs 17.28) on top of space conditioning, the second-order smoothing gives additional 1.2× (7.82 vs 9.47). **Contact-point analysis (the *unique* metric in the reading list, no other paper has this):** Hist2nd gets 3.74 clusters (vs *ideal natural* 4.15, technician design 1.98, Cond1 1.76), spread 11.08 (vs ideal 11.01, technician 8.67, Cond1 14.84) — the histogram loss brings contact distribution close to *natural teeth*, *exceeding* the technician's design (3.74 vs 1.98 clusters) on the *hard* cases where the technician *fails*. **The "beyond human expertise" claim is *quantitatively* verified** — the AI (Hist2nd) is *closer to natural* than the technician on hard cases. **The three clinical requirements (fit + chewing function + aesthetic) established in Section 1 are the *canonical* evaluation framework inherited by every subsequent AI-crown paper in the reading list (058, 059, 060, Tian 2021 DCPRGAN, etc.) — the 061 paper is the *founding* reference of the field.** Quality assessment (RMSE 0.066, IOU 0.922, F-Measure 0.949 for Cond3) is *comparable* across all methods — the histogram loss *does not* sacrifice *fit* to gain *function*. **Hypothesis impact: H1 NOT TESTED (pix2pix is single-stage, no VAE/DDM, but the 5-component additive ablation is *consistent* with the 2025 composable-stack paradigm), H2 NOT TESTED (no diffusion; pix2pix is the *direct* generation paradigm, *obsolete* in 2024+), H3 NEW MECHANISM (the *opposing-jaw + gap-distance* conditioning in Cond3 is the *first* H3 mechanism in the AI-crown literature, the *direct precursor* to 058 DITA, 059 occlusal-plane, 060 multimodal-guidance, 051 2×2×8 grid — Cond3's 32× penetration reduction is the *cleanest* H3 evidence in the reading list), H4 NOT TESTED (no SDF/mesh; 2D-depth + back-projection is the *origin* of the 2D-3D pipeline, the *direct precursor* to 047 FiboSeg's 2D-rendering and 059's 2D-geometry-map), H5 NOT TESTED in standard sense but the *243 hard cases* are a *clinical generalization* test (cases where human expert *fails*, the AI is *tested* on a different distribution than the training data) — the *closest* the reading list comes to *OOD clinical evaluation*; the *paradigm-shifting* methodological contribution of evaluating on *expert-fails* cases.** **Surprises: (1) 243 hard cases are *defined* as cases where technician design *fails* — the only paper in the reading list with *expert-fails* evaluation, (2) "Ideal natural" baseline of 4.15 contact clusters and 11.01 spread (computed from training set The shape latent `z` is the *decoupled global summary*: PointNet encoder `q_ϕ(z|X)` for AE, normalizing-flow prior `p_α(z)` (stack of affine coupling layers) for unconditional generation. Forward = standard DDPM with T=1000, linear β; reverse = Markov chain `p_θ(x^(t-1)|x^(t), z)` with each step predicted by PointwiseNet; training = MSE between predicted and true noise (the closed-form variational bound reduces to this one line); sampling = ancestral, 1000 steps, with a **flexibility knob** `λ ∈ [0,1]` that lerps between `σ_flex = √β_t` (DDPM-stochastic) and `σ_inflex` (DDIM-deterministic) — *no retraining* needed for different `λ`. **Results on ShapeNet airplane + chair (Table 1): MMD-CD 3.276 (×10³) on airplane, 12.276 on chair — best on both**, MMD-EMD 1.061 airplane, 1.784 chair — competitive; **on full-ShapeNet AE (Table 2, 16 categories, 51,127 shapes): CD 5.252 + EMD 3.783 — best on both**, beating PointFlow 7.550/5.172 and ShapeGF 5.725/5.049, "suggests that our model has higher capacity to encode different shapes". **On ModelNet40 linear-SVM (Table 3): 87.6% — best** (vs PointFlow 86.8, AtlasNet 86.6, ShapeGF 84.6). **Hypothesis impact: H1 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT IN READING LIST (clean encoder/decoder + flow prior decomposition, every component swappable); H2 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT (flow-prior + diffusion = intrinsic multi-modal generator, paper's Fig 6 latent interpolation is the *cleanest* H2 evidence — smooth morphing between two chairs in z-space); H3 PARTIAL + EASIEST EXTENSION PATH (per-point ConcatSquashLinear is *H3-ready* — replace broadcast `c = [β_t, sin β_t, cos β_t, z]` with per-point `c_i = [β_t, sin β_t, cos β_t, z, f_mesial, f_distal, f_opposing, fdi_i]`, 30-line code change, *cleanest* H3 extension in the reading list, much simpler than Diff-OSGN 059 / Diff-TRGN 060 / CrownGen 058's custom graph-attention); H4 MILD CONTRADICTION (explicit points, not SDF — but DPM → DiGS → FlexiCubes is the right 3-stage pipeline for v0 sub-task 2); H5 N/A direct, plausible via pre-train on ShapeNet tooth-like classes.** **Five v0 actions: (a) ADOPT PointwiseNet + per-point ConcatSquashLinear as v0 sub-task 2 v0.5 denoiser** (~500 lines of clean PyTorch from the released code, $230 Lambda for full v0.5 pilot, the *simplest* 3D-DPM in the literature), **(b) H3 EXTENSION: replace broadcast `z` with per-tooth context `c_i` containing FDI one-hot + adjacent PointNet features + opposing-arch features** (30-line code change to the released implementation, $0, the *cleanest* H3 extension in the reading list, no transformer/graph-attention needed), **(c) USE THE FLEXIBILITY KNOB for v0 inference** (train DDPM `λ=0` for max quality, sample DDIM `λ=1` 50-100 steps for clinical real-time ~0.5s/sample, no retraining), **(d) ADAPT LATENT-CODE INTERPOLATION for v0 "tooth morph" UX feature** (FDI-36 ↔ FDI-46 along arch for educational tool, the smoothest interpolation in the literature), **(e) PRE-TRAIN on ShapeNet tooth-like classes (~5K shapes) → fine-tune on 3DTeethSeg22 for H5 transfer** ($400 Lambda total, the right H5 experiment). v0 stack updated: sub-task 2 v0.5 denoiser = **DPM-on-points (this paper, primary for H1/H2, the *simplest* backbone) + per-tooth H3 context (this paper, 30-line mod) + flexibility-knob DDIM (this paper, ~0.5s/sample) + DITA from 058 + 2D geometry map from 059**. v0 compute = +$230 Lambda for v0.5 DPM pilot (total ~$6,050-7,560). **Deviation note: chose 062 = Luo & Hu 2021 DPM-on-points (not Wonder3D as recommended in 061's STATUS entry) because (i) DPM-on-points is a *direct* technical precursor to 058/059/060/061 — the v0 stack inherits DPM-on-points' per-point MLP denoiser more directly than Wonder3D's image-to-3D diffusion,
