- **Hour 2026-06-08 12:03 KST:** read paper 063 — *Wonder3D: Single Image to 3D using Cross-Domain Diffusion* (Xiaoxiao Long*, Yuan-Chen Guo*, Cheng Lin†, Yuan Liu, Zhiyang Dou, Lingjie Liu, Yuexin Ma, Song-Hai Zhang, Marc Habermann, Christian Theobalt, Wenping Wang† — **HKU VAST + Tsinghua VAST + UPenn + ShanghaiTech + MPI Informatik + Texas A&M, 6-institution axis (the most international 2023-2024 paper in the reading list)**, **arXiv:2310.15008 v1 23 Oct 2023 → CVPR 2024 Highlight**, **code ✅** github.com/xxlong0/Wonder3D MIT, pretrained ✅ HuggingFace flamehaze1115/wonder3d-v1.0 + OneDrive mirror, **Wonder3D++ extension 2024-12-22**, **2 follow-up works by same authors: GeoWizard 2024-03, CraftsMan3D 2024-05, Era3D 2024-05**). **Key insight: the *cleanest* 2-stage H1 cross-modal generation paper in the 2023-2024 literature — generate 6 multi-view normal maps + matching color images via a *cross-domain* Stable Diffusion variant, then fuse via instant-NGP/NeuS/Neuralangelo into a textured mesh in 2-3 minutes on a single GPU.** Three orthogonal innovations: **(1) domain switcher `s`** (1-token integer [RGB=0 or normal=1] positional-encoded + concatenated with time embedding, injected into the SD UNet) — the *least invasive* mechanism to extend a pretrained 2D diffusion model to multi-domain, *explicitly* avoids "catastrophic model forgetting" that comes from channel expansion ("a straightforward solution is to add four more channels to the output of the UNet... however, we notice that such a design suffers from low convergence speed and poor generalization"); **(2) cross-domain attention** (inserted BEFORE the cross-attention layer in every UNet transformer block, K and V from BOTH RGB and normal domains concatenated, queries from A attend to A+B — the "first agree between domains, then attend to input" ordering is a *subtle* design choice) — enforces geometric/visual consistency between the two domains; **(3) geometry-aware normal fusion** (neural SDF optimization on the 6 normal + color images with: (a) `cos(angle(normal, view_ray))` reliability weighting — normals orthogonal to view rays are more reliable, (b) outlier-dropping loss — sort color/mask errors descending, drop the top largest, eliminates isolated geometries, (c) instant-NGP/NeuS/Neuralangelo backbone). **6 fixed azimuths, no elevation, 0°/±45°/±90°/180°**, **input-view-related coordinate system** (NOT canonical — Z+ and X+ aligned with UV of 2D input, Y+ perpendicular — no elevation estimation at inference, strong generalization on unreal images, but cannot do canonical evaluation). **Trained on LVIS subset of Objaverse, ~30K objects, 8×A800, 3 days, 30K steps, batch 512, 256×256, fine-tuned from Stable Diffusion Image Variations Model with *only* the new cross-domain parameters trainable (rest frozen → preserves 2D prior).** **GSO single-view 3D reconstruction leaderboard (OpenCodePapers): Wonder3D CD 0.0199 / IoU 62.44 (2023-10, the SOTA at publication) > SyncDreamer CD 0.0261 / IoU 54.21 (2023-09, the prior SOTA), and beat by Unique3D CD 0.0145 / F-Score 68.45% (2024-05, 7 months later, IoU still loses to Wonder3D 55.38 vs 62.44).** **Inference 2-3 min/end-to-end on 1 GPU, 12GB VRAM, xformers + fp16, DDIM 20 steps for multi-view generation, 1-2 min for SDF optimization.** **Bug-fix history (2024-08-29): (a) training had `zero_init_camera_projection=True` which should be False (otherwise domain/pose controls invalid), (b) inference CFG batch-halves order was wrong for cross-domain attention — RGB and normal must be in the first/second halves, NOT the typical unconditional/conditional split — *the* deployment lesson.** **Hypothesis impact: H1 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT (2-stage = multi-view diffusion + neural-SDF fusion, the *exact* H1 decomposition), H2 STRONG SUPPORT (multi-view diffusion is the *only* 2D prior strong enough for OOD, beats SDS-based 3D methods that need 30-60 min per shape and give inconsistent geometry), H3 PARTIAL + CLEANEST H3 EXTENSION MECHANISM IN 2023-2024 LITERATURE (single-image conditioned, but the *cross-domain attention* is the architectural primitive to fuse (target, adjacent, opposing) modalities — replace (RGB, normal) with (target_render, adjacent_render, opposing_render), generalize domain switcher to a 1-token modality switcher), H4 STRONG SUPPORT (mesh extraction is *explicitly* neural SDF / NeuS / instant-NGP / Neuralangelo, "Unlike alternative representations like meshes, SDF offers compactness and differentiability, making them ideal for stable optimization"), H5 STRONG SUPPORT (synthetic-only Objaverse 30K → real GSO, OmniObject3D, AI-generated images, paintings — the *strongest* synthetic-to-real H5 evidence in the reading list via the 2D intermediate representation).** **Ten surprises: (1) domain switcher is *just* a 1-token integer, the simplest possible mechanism for multi-domain diffusion, (2) cross-domain attention inserted *before* cross-attention (not after) for the "first agree between domains, then attend to input" pattern, (3) geometry-aware normal loss weights by `cos(angle(normal, view_ray))` — a *cheaper* H3 mechanism, the viewing geometry is itself a reliability signal, (4) outlier-dropping loss is the cleanest robustness trick — drop top X% largest errors at every iteration, wrong predictions lack cross-view consistency → large errors → get dropped, equivalent to a *hard* attention over the loss terms, (5) 6 fixed azimuths with NO elevation — the simplest possible view setup, *non-obvious* v0 modification: add 2 elevation views (top + bottom of arch) to capture occlusal/incisal surfaces for crown generation, (6) input-view-related coordinate system, NOT canonical — no FDI-relative canonical pose estimation needed (FDI implicit in input tooth's identity in the IOS scan), but cannot do canonical evaluation; for v0 sub-task 4 the v0 wants *canonical* so this is *not* what we want, but the cross-domain/multi-view attention mechanisms *are*, (7) trained on only 30K Objaverse objects (not 800K) — *small, clean* dataset is sufficient with right architecture, v0 lesson: ~1-5K carefully curated IOS scans might be sufficient, (8) the 2024-08-29 bug fix is a *deployment lesson* — even SOTA papers have subtle inference-time bugs, v0 should have CI tests for cross-domain attention CFG batch halves, (9) Wonder3D's 6-view setup maps *perfectly* to the *6 azimuths* a dental crown is naturally observed from in a 3D IOS scan (mesial/mesio-buccal/buccal/distal/disto-buccal/lingual) — a *direct* port path, (10) the same authors' follow-up works (GeoWizard, CraftsMan3D, Era3D, Wonder3D++) suggest a *research arc* — each addresses a Wonder3D limitation (resolution, consistency, focal length, scale) — the v0 should *follow this arc pattern* by starting with a v0 v0.5 Wonder3D-style pilot and iteratively addressing limitations in v0.5/v0.6.** **Ten v0 actions: (a) ADOPT cross-domain diffusion as v0 v0.5 *side-track* pre-training step** ($500 Lambda, 4 weeks, 6-view renders of 3DTeethSeg22 arches - **Hour 2026-06-08 13:33 KST:** read paper 066 — *DentalRecNet: A Dual Discriminator Adversarial Learning Approach for Dental Occlusal Surface Reconstruction* / "DentalRecNet" (Sukun TIAN, Renkai HUANG, Zhenyang LI, Luca FIORENZA, Ning DAI, Yuchun SUN, Haifeng MA — **Shandong U Mech Eng (Tian's new institutional home) + Jiangxi U Sci Tech + Monash Biomedicine Discovery (international) + Nanjing U Aeronautics (Dai's new home) + Peking U Stomatology (Sun's clinical-collaboration group, same as CMEMO 064, DCPR-GAN 064)**, **J Healthc Eng 2022:1933617 (Hindawi/Wiley, *not* BCA as 065 incorrectly noted), 14 pages, published Apr 12 2022, doi 10.1155/2022/1933617, PMID 35449834, PMCID PMC9018184 (open-access CC-BY), **no arXiv, no code (consistent with 2018-2022 AI-crown line), no public data** (1000 patients from PKU Stomatology, 2 scanners, *larger* than DCPR-GAN 2021's 780 patients by 28%, the *largest* AI-crown dataset to date in 2022). Key insight: **the *discriminator-side H3 follow-up* to CMEMO 2021 / DCPR-GAN 2021, the *3rd* paper in the 2021-2022 2-stage GAN triad (CMEMO 2021 → DCPR-GAN 2021 → **DentalRecNet 2022**), the *first* paper in the AI-crown literature to *explicitly* use a *global-local dual discriminator* and to *decompose* training into 3 stages (generator-only → discriminator-warmup → joint-adversarial).** Five orthogonal innovations: (1) **image-entropy-assisted adaptive visual distance orthogonal projection** — `pixel = 255·(h^α − d^α)/h^α` (Eq. 1) with `h=6mm`, the enhancement factor α chosen by *maximizing* the depth-image's image entropy `H = −Σ P_t log₂ P_t` (Eq. 2), the *first* paper in the AI-crown literature to use *information-theoretic* criteria for depth-encoding hyperparameter selection (empirically α=2 maximizes H, *same value* as CMEMO 2021's n=2, but *principled*); (2) **encoder-decoder generator with dilated convolutions** — the *first* explicit dilated-conv in the AI-crown literature, dilation rates `d ∈ {1,2,4,8}` (concave-cat receptive field growth, ~0% parameter overhead, ~30% more receptive field), with *fractional-stride 1/4* downsampling (preserves *sub-millimeter* cusp tips that standard max-pool blurs) and *multi-scale feature integration* via concatenation of *all* dilated conv outputs (the *first* multi-scale integration pattern in the AI-crown line, later reused by Diff-OSGN 059's operator-based supervision); (3) **global + local dual discriminator** — the *first* explicit global-local dual discriminator in the AI-crown literature, *global* D sees the whole 256×256 occlusal surface image and judges *arch-coherence*, *local* D sees a 64×64 crop around the missing tooth and judges *tooth-level quality*, jointly provide *complementary* judgments (the *architectural* H3 mechanism — where the discriminator *attends*); (4) **3-stage training protocol** — Stage I (generator alone with composite L1+MSE+perceptual on spatial-constraint + biological-morphology), Stage II (freeze generator, train D_g+D_l from scratch with MSE+perceptual), Stage III (joint end-to-end with L = λ_L1·L1 + λ_mse·L_mse + λ_per·L_per + L_adv_g + L_adv_l, λ_L1=100, λ_mse=50, λ_per=50, *first* explicit L1-dominant weighting in AI-crown literature, L1 : L_mse : L_per = 2 : 1 : 1); (5) **3D mesh reconstruction via region growing** on the 256×256 depth image → 3D crown surface (same as CMEMO 2021 / DCPR-GAN 2021, 1ms, deterministic, watertight for single-valued surfaces). **Headline results on 60 first-molar test cases (#36/#46, 3Shape D700 scanner, GTX 1080Ti, TensorFlow, Adam β1=0.5, β2=0.999, LR=2e-4): PSNR 34.264±1.228, FSIM 0.993±0.008, SSIM 0.985±0.005, RMS 0.114 mm** (the *best* sub-200μm result in the AI-crown literature up to 2022, *better* than DCPR-GAN 2021's ~0.180 mm by 37% and DAIS 2021's 0.164 mm by 30%); **beats all 5 baselines (Pix2pix 28.352, CrownDesNet=Hwang 2018 28.917, Dental-GAN=Yuan 2020 30.133, DAIS=Tian 2021 31.454, GL-GAN=Iizuka 2017 29.705) by +2.81 PSNR / +0.011 FSIM / +0.011 SSIM / -0.050 mm RMS**. **Stage-wise ablation (Tab. 1): I (27.833/0.961/0.933) → II (28.129/0.967/0.948) → III (34.264/0.993/0.985) — Stage II → III is the *biggest* single-stage gain in the AI-crown literature (+6.13 PSNR, 20× larger than I → II), confirming the adversarial signal is critical for biological-morphology capture**. **Dilated conv ablation (Section 3.5): -0.078 mm SD / -0.081 mm RMS vs general conv, the *cheapest* "more parameters" gain in the reading list (~0% parameter overhead)**. **α sweep (Section 3.4): α=2.0 maximizes image entropy H (Fig. 4) and achieves best PSNR (Fig. 10), consistent with CMEMO 2021's n=2 consensus**. **ANOVA + Kruskal-Wallis: p ≪ 1e-3 for both 6-method comparison and DentalRecNet-vs-rest, the *first* paper in AI-crown to use *both* tests, the *second* paper (after DCPR-GAN 064) to use *any* statistical-significance test**. **The contralateral-tooth test (Fig. 13, "in theory, the left teeth (#36) and right teeth (#46) of a person are symmetrical") is the *first* clinical-naturalness evaluation in the AI-crown reading list** — the AI-designed #36 crown is "morphologically similar to the contralateral tooth (#46) of the same patient", "more personalized than the crown designed by dentist", a *clinical-H5* metric that *complements* the standard PSNR/FSIM/SSIM pixel-similarity. **Discussion (Section 4) has 5 substantive paragraphs: (a) occlusal fingerprint dissipates tensile stresses over the dental crown (the *biomechanical* link, the *first* explicit stress-biology connection in the AI-crown line, implies FEA-based supervision is the *open problem*), (b) occlusal groove determines food-flow direction and masticatory efficiency (the *biomechanical* link, implies food-flow-direction alignment is a *novel* clinical metric), (c) 32 tooth types have 32 different design rules (the *tooth-type-conditional* paradigm, the *right* design for v1's 32-FDI-class generalization), (d) end-to-end 3D is the *open problem* (the *call to action* for the 2025-2026 diffusion era), (e) mandibular first molar scope is a *deliberate* design choice (highest defect rate, *not* a limitation). **Hypothesis impact: H1 NOT TESTED** (single-stage generator, 3-stage is *training-time* not *architecture-time*, consistent with 065's H1 finding that H1 is *generation-specific*; for the 2D-rasterization-then-lift 2-stage GAN paradigm, single-stage is the empirical default), **H2 NOT TESTED** (pure GAN, no diffusion, no VAE, no flow prior; the *right* non-diffusion baseline for the 256×256 depth-image v0 v0.5 sub-pilot), **H3 STRONG SUPPORT — TWO INDEPENDENT MECHANISMS** (generator-side: 3 spatial-constraint conditioning inputs [opposing jaw + gap distances + biological morphology features] = the *same* H3 design template as Hwang 2018 [paper 061, Cond3] and Yuan 2020 [paper, gap-distance constraint]; *discriminator-side*: global-local duality is a *new* H3 mechanism — the global D judges *arch-coherence* (full-arch view), the local D judges *tooth-quality* (per-tooth view) — the *complementary-information* form of H3, the *first* paper in the reading list to use it; v0 should adopt the dual-discriminator pattern for v0 v0.5 sub-pilot, drop-in, 1 week engineering, +1-2 PSNR expected), **H4 NOT TESTED** (no SDF, no implicit surface, no mesh extractor; the 2021-2022 AI-crown line is *necessarily* 2D-rasterization + post-hoc lift; H4 is a *generation paradigm* question that requires the *different* PVD+DiGS+FlexiCubes pipeline from papers 003, 007, 012), **H5 NOT TESTED in the standard sense, BUT a *contralateral-tooth naturalness test* is a form of cross-arch H5 generalization** (Fig. 13: AI-generalizes from training distribution [1000 patients] to *contralateral tooth* of a *specific* patient, the *first* clinical-H5 test in the AI-crown reading list; the *easiest* clinical-H5 test to adopt for v0 [1-2 days]). **Surprises: (1) image-entropy depth-encoding adaptation is a *new* H3 mechanism, the *principled* version of the consensus (n=2, l=6mm) encoding, (2) *fractional-stride 1/4* downsampling is the *first* sub-pixel-accurate downsampling in AI-crown, preserves sub-millimeter cusp tips, (3) *multi-scale feature integration* via concatenation of *all* dilated conv outputs is the *first* multi-scale integration pattern in AI-crown, (4) the *contralateral-tooth* clinical evaluation is the *first* clinical-naturalness metric beyond PSNR/FSIM/SSIM, (5) the *composite loss weighting* `L1 : L_mse : L_per = 2 : 1 : 1` is the *first* explicit L1-dominant weighting in AI-crown, (6) the *ANOVA + Kruskal-Wallis* double test is the *first* in AI-crown, the *robust* statistical-significance protocol, (7) the *Stage I → Stage II → Stage III* training-time decomposition is *not* the *same* as the *architecture-time* 2-stage decomposition (VAE+DDM), the v0 paper should clarify this distinction, (8) *no data-leakage-control* statement (no patient-level separation specified, consistent with 2018-2022 AI-crown literature but inconsistent with 2023+ best practice, v0 should adopt *patient-level* split as a *defensive* measure), (9) *first mandibular molar only* (#36/#46) scope is a *deliberate* design choice (highest defect rate, justifies the v0 v0.5 sub-pilot scope), (10) the *mesial-side* missing tooth emphasis is the *first* explicit *defect-rate distribution* across tooth types in AI-crown (50%+ of crown - **Hour 2026-06-08 14:08 KST:** read paper 067 — *Efficient Computer-Aided Design of Dental Inlay Restoration: A Deep Adversarial Framework* / "DAIS" (Sukun Tian*, Miaohui Wang*, Fulai Yuan, Ning Dai, Yuchun Sun, Wuyuan Xie, Jing Qin† — **Peking U Stomatology + Shenzhen U (Wang's new home) + Nanjing U Aeronautics & Astronautics + HK PolyU (Qin, the *international* co-PI, *new* to the Tian group)**, **IEEE TMI 40(9):2415-2427 Sep 2021 (the *first* TMI paper in the AI-crown reading list, Q1 medical imaging IF ~10-11, the *highest-impact* Tian group paper)**, DOI 10.1109/TMI.2021.3077334, PMID 33945473, **no arXiv, no code (consistent with 2018-2022 AI-crown line), no public data** (830 patients, 3 hospitals, 3Shape D700, *largest* inlay-specific AI-crown dataset in the reading list — *larger* than 064's 780 by 6%). Key insight: **the *4th paper* in the Tian group 2021-2022 4-paper arc, the *efficiency-focused* inlay paper, the *Wasserstein + DuNet + GroNet + histogram* member — and the *provenance* of 066 DentalRecNet 2022's dual discriminator (one year earlier) and 064 DCPR-GAN 2021's GroNet**. Four orthogonal innovations over CMEMO 2021: (1) **Wasserstein GAN with Earth-Mover distance** — replaces vanilla CGAN's JS divergence with EM distance, *continuous* loss, *stable* training on 830-sample dataset, *no mode collapse* (the *first* paper in the AI-crown reading list to *explicitly* address training instability); (2) **DuNet dual discriminator** with explicit *local* (defective tooth alone) + *global* (defective tooth + adjacent teeth) discrimination, *first* explicit dual-discriminator in AI-crown literature, *predates* 066 by one year; (3) **GroNet occlusal-groove parsing loss** — a *pre-trained* Pix2Pix network (frozen, for parsing) that extracts occlusal grooves from both generated and target depth images, with L1 loss on the parsed groove maps, *first* paper in AI-crown to use a *separate* pretrained network as a *parsing* loss for anatomical structures; (4) **Histogram loss on occlusal-gap distribution** — *first* paper in AI-crown to use *distributional* matching for the opposing-tooth contact, with *learnable* bin centers μ and widths ω via differentiable soft histogram h_{k,b}(x_k) = Σ max(0, 1 - |x_k - μ|/ω). Plus (5) **3-stage incremental-loss training protocol** (Test1: L1 + L_Dlocal + L_PG; Test2: + L_GroNet + L_Dglobal; Test3: + L_histogram), *first* paper in AI-crown to *explicitly* decompose training into 3 incremental-loss stages, +3.10 PSNR over single-stage (the *biggest* single-paper training-protocol ablation in the reading list). Plus (6) **lightweight inlay surface segmentation with heuristic search** for the Dental Biological Feature Line (DBFL), 3-5 interaction points, 6-10 seconds per inlay, *classical-geometry* alternative to deep-learning segmentation. Plus (7) **0.05mm cement-layer offset** for inner surface extraction, *first* paper in AI-crown reading list to *explicitly* model the cement layer. Plus (8) **Laplacian mesh deformation** to align generated occlusal surface boundary with inner cavity surface boundary, *first* paper in AI-crown reading list to use *Laplacian* mesh deformation for inlay boundary alignment. Plus (9) **ITU-R BT.500-11 DSCQS MOS subjective test** with 17 subjects and 5 quality levels, DAIS ranks *best on 4 of 5* inlay types, *first* paper in AI-crown reading list to do a *rigorous* subjective evaluation. Plus (10) **5 inlay types** (single/double/triple/four-surface + inlay) — the *scope* of the paper, the *narrowest* but *most inlay-diverse* of the 4-paper arc. **Headline results on 80 test samples (5 inlay types, GTX 1080Ti, TensorFlow, Adam β1=0.5 β2=0.999 LR=2e-4, 830 total, 750 train / 40 val / 80 test): PSNR 29.25, RMSE 7.15, MS-SSIM 0.82, FSIM 0.89** (vs Pix2Pix 25.85/10.11/0.78/0.85, PAN 27.75/8.36/0.81/0.86, GFC 28.17/7.42/0.80/0.87, Dental-GAN 28.32/7.53/0.81/0.87 — *best* on all 4 metrics, +0.93 PSNR over strongest baseline Dental-GAN at *similar* inference time 13.24s vs 13.17s). **Hyperparameters fully reported** (Table 3, the *only* paper in AI-crown to report all hyperparameters): λ_L1=100 (dominant), λ_GroNet=50, λ_PD=20, λ_h=50, λ_PG=50, λ_P1/P2/P3=1/2/2. **Ablation Test1 → Test2 → Test3 incremental**: 26.15 → 28.18 → 29.25 PSNR, the *biggest* single-paper training-protocol ablation in reading list (+3.10 PSNR, -3.55 RMSE, +0.03 MS-SSIM, +0.04 FSIM). **Ablation γ projection-plane distance** (Table 7): γ=1.5 wins (29.25 PSNR) over 0.5/1.0/2.0, the *consensus* Tian group parameter. **Ablation 4×4 filter / 15 layers / 14×14 patch** (Table 6), the *standard* 2D-GAN hyperparameters. **Hypothesis impact: H1 STRONG support (3-stage training protocol, the *first* paper in AI-crown to *explicitly* decompose training into 3 incremental-loss stages, the *provenance* of 066's 3-stage training), H2 NO evidence (uses GAN not diffusion, 2018-2022 AI-crown is *pre-diffusion*; architectural choices are *agnostic* to H2 and can be *ported* to a diffusion-based generator as the v0 v0.5 sub-pilot), H3 STRONG support — TWO-PRONGED (generator-side: WGAN conditioned on [c, d_gap] where c=opposing tooth and d_gap=occlusal gap distance, *first* paper in AI-crown to use occlusal gap distance as explicit generator conditioning signal; discriminator-side: DuNet global D sees defective tooth + adjacent teeth, *first* paper in AI-crown to use adjacent teeth as discriminator-side conditioning signal, the *complementarity* of generator-side and discriminator-side H3 is the architectural innovation), H4 NO evidence (2D depth images + 3D mesh via region growing + Laplacian, *not* implicit SDF; architectural choices are *agnostic* to H4 and can be *ported* to implicit-SDF pipeline as v1 product), H5 NO evidence (real clinical data only, no synthetic pretraining)**. **Surprises: (1) WGAN's stability is the *real* contribution**, not the architecture (every 2018-2022 AI-crown paper reports training instability; WGAN fixes this), **(2) GroNet is *pretrained* and *frozen*, not jointly trained** (decoupled design is *critical* for stability), **(3) histogram loss learns bin centers AND widths** (not just bin counts), **(4) MOS subjective test is *ruthless*** (17 subjects, 5 levels, 5 types — the *only* rigorous subjective test in the AI-crown reading list), **(5) Laplacian deformation is the *unsung hero*** (not in any ablation, but 0.05mm cement-layer offset is the *biomechanically-aware* detail that *every* clinical paper ignores), **(6) 5 inlay types are the *scope*, not just a test detail** (the *sweet spot* between 064/066's full-crown and 065's 1-type inlay), **(7) hardware is a *single* GTX 1080Ti** (single-GPU training is the *practical* constraint for dental hospitals, the v0 v0.5 sub-pilot is small enough for M4 Mac mini)**. **v0 actions (drop-in adds, all $0 compute): (a) ADOPT WGAN with Earth-Mover distance as v0 v0.5 sub-pilot generator** (50 lines PyTorch, 1-2 weeks, training-stability DCrownFormer 2024 is the **actual** next paper in the AI-crown reading list — the *first* paper *not* by the Tian group in the recent arc, the *first* MICCAI paper, the *first* Korean paper, the *first* tech-transferred paper, the *transformer + MCAM + CPL + MRL* bridge to the 2025-2026 diffusion era. Key insight: **PCT encoder-decoder + Morphology-aware Cross-Attention Module (MCAM, Q from decoded+coarse, K,V from skip-connected encoded+Pi) + Curvature-Penalty Loss (CPL, e^{λ|κ(y)|} with κ_max=5, λ=1.0 inverted-U optimum) + Mesh Reconstruction Loss (MRL, direct DPSR indicator-grid supervision ||Rp-Rg||²) + Differentiable Poisson Surface Reconstruction (DPSR) → Marching Cubes**. **Headline result on 2317 plaster-cast scans (1393/464/460 train/val/test, 3Shape D2000 scanner, mandibular+maxillary molars, designed by dentist on 3Shape TRIOS): CD 15.06±3.29 (×10⁻³) / F 0.953±0.062 / NC 0.798±0.047 / MAE 1.84±0.53 (×10⁻³) / R² 0.694±0.163 / SDE 6.47±2.15 (×10⁻³) — best on 5/6 metrics, 2nd on NC (loses to TopNet+SAP 0.850)**. CD -14.2% vs GRNet+SAP (next-best 17.56), F-score +4.5% vs GRNet+SAP, MAE -30% vs TopNet+SAP, SDE -25% vs TopNet+SAP. PointTr+SAP *disaster* (CD 40.39, MAE 20.24) — generic point-completion collapses on dental task. MCAM ablation: Baseline 15.26 → +SAM 15.20 → +MCAM 15.06. MRL ablation (the *killer*): without MRL +SAP → CD 15.38 / MAE 4.74 / SDE 8.03; with MRL → CD 15.06 / MAE 1.84 (-61%) / SDE 6.47 (-19%) — MRL is the **biggest** single-component gain for mesh-quality metrics. CPL λ inverted-U: λ=0 (vanilla CD) → λ=1.0 (best) → λ>1 (worse). 2,317-sample dataset = *largest* in AI-crown reading list for *real plaster-cast* data. **Hypothesis impact: H1 SUPPORTS (real patient scans + dentist-designed + future 3D printing, but no clinical fit validation), H2 STRONGLY SUPPORTS (the *first* paper in reading list to *directly* output a 3D mesh via *direct* indicator-grid supervision, predates MADCrowner 2026 margin-aware and ToothForge 2025 spectral designs), H3 STRONGLY SUPPORTS (the *first* paper in reading list to use *curvature-weighted* Chamfer Distance — e^{λ|κ(y)|} is the *direct* mathematical formulation of "high-curvature regions matter more", the *first* inverted-U in AI-crown literature), H4 PARTIALLY SUPPORTS (the *hybrid* between completion and generation — MCAM cross-attention between decoded and encoded is a *completion* mechanism, but the output is a *generation* of points+normals+indicator-grid), H5 STRONGLY SUPPORTS (the *first* AI-crown paper tech-transferred to a major dental implant company — Osstem Implant, ~$1.2B market cap, Seoul HQ; the *concession* (no code, no data, no weights) is the *price* of commercial deployment, the *first* documented commercial translation in AI-crown reading list).** **Surprises: (1) PointTr+SAP disaster (CD 40.39) — generic point-completion fails on the *highly-constrained* dental task, *specialized* designs (MCAM, CPL, MRL) are *necessary*; (2) MCAM attention-map visualization shows *qualitative* focus on morphology-relevant points (antagonist, proximal, margin line) but *quantitative* gain is small (15.06 vs 15.20 for SAM, -0.93%); (3) CPL inverted-U is *non-trivial* — too much curvature weighting *hurts* overall performance, the *sweet spot* λ=1.0 is *consistent* with the Sun-Jeong Kim 2002 discrete-curvature norm normalization, not with the "more weighting = better" intuition; (4) MRL ablation is *under-reported* in main text — MAE -61% is *huge* but the paper doesn't explain *why* MAE is *so much* more sensitive to MRL than CD; (5) Osstem Implant + KMDF co-funding is the *first* industrial co-funder with *equity* in AI-crown reading list; (6) 3D printing is mentioned as future work but no quantitative print-and-fit validation — the *first* AI-crown paper to *actually* print and validate fit is *not yet* in the reading list; (7) 3Shape D2000 + TRIOS is the *de facto* industry standard for AI-crown data acquisition and design; (8) the Hosseinimanesh 2023 MICCAI reference [18] comparison is *not directly comparable* (different datasets, different unit conventions: Hosseinimanesh reports raw CD, DCrownFormer reports ×10⁻³ CD, a 2nd occurrence of the *metric-unit footgun* that the v0 paper should standardize).** **v0 actions: **(c) ADOPT MRL ||DPSR(Pp,Np)-DPSR(Pg,Ng)||² as v0 sub-task 4 default mesh supervision** (1-2 days engineering, $0 compute, *direct* indicator-grid supervision, the *first* in v0 stack, *biggest* mesh-quality gain), **(d) USE 6-metric evaluation (CD, F-score, NC, MAE, R², SDE) verbatim for v0 sub-task 4** (the *most* comprehensive eval in AI-crown reading list, $0, 1 day, *direct* baseline shortlist — avoid PointTr-style generic collapse), **(f) CITE DCrownFormer as v0 sub-task 4's *MICCAI 2024 baseline*** (the *canonical* 2024 point-to-mesh reference), **(g) DEFER the Osstem-style tech-transfer strategy to v2** (v0 = open-source release for *research impact*; v1 = pilot with a small clinic; v2 = tech-transfer if commercial path materializes), **(h) ADD clinical IRB).** **v0 stack updated:** sub-task 4 (outer surface / crown) = PVD + ME-loss + DiGS + FlexiCubes + Surface Projection + MGR + CBL + **MCAM cross-attention decoder (NEW from 068, drop-in, - **Hour 2026-06-08 16:23 KST:** read paper 069 — *DM-CFO: A Diffusion Model for Compositional 3D Tooth Generation with Collision-Free Optimization* (Yan Tian, Pengcheng Xue, Weiping Ding, Mahmoud Hassaballah, Karen Egiazarian, Aura Conci, Abdulkadir Sengur, Leszek Rutkowski — **Zhejiang Gongshang University + Shining3D Tech Co. + Nantong University + Prince Sattam U + Tampere U + UFF + Firat U + Polish Academy of Sciences + AGH Krakow + SAN University**, **IEEE TVCG vol. 41 no. 8, August 2026**, arXiv:2603.03602 v1, 4 Mar 2026, **no public code** (project page demo-only at amateurc.github.io/CF-3DTeeth, likely Shining3D tech-transfer target), **3 private commercial datasets: Shining3D 1,416 meshes + Aoralscan3 1,999 + DeepBlue 2,061 (2-of-3 are Shining3D-internal, 1st author Tian is a Shining3D employee), no public release**). Key insight: **the *first* AI framework to use 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) + graph diffusion + collision-loss regularization for compositional 3D tooth generation — and the *first* to achieve sub-100μm inter-tooth penetration distance (PD 0.07mm) on a real Shining3D dataset, well within the ISO 6872:2015 50-100μm clinical-fit threshold**. The architecture has three moving parts: **(1) Graph Diffusion Model (GDM) for layout** — a 5-layer 8-head 512-dim Graph Transformer that denoises a target graph `Gt = (Ct, Lt, Ft, Et)` with edge types ∈ {Neighbor, Symmetry, Arch}, conditioned on the source graph `Gs` (the input jaw) and a text prompt `ys`; **(2) Dual-level Score Distillation Sampling (SDS)** — alternating optimization of (a) scene-level SDS via ControlNet guidance (global jaw coherence) and (b) instance-level SDS via MVDream (per-tooth realism), both denoising the *same* 3D Gaussian parameters; **(3) Gaussian Collision Loss (GCL)** — a per-tooth intravariance `R_i = (1/K_i) Σ ||p_i^k - p_i^m||²` is *learned*, and `L_col = Σ max(0, R_i - ||p_j^k - p_i^m||²)` penalizes neighboring tooth Gaussians whose distance to the anchor's mean is *less than* the anchor's intravariance, with empirically 0.1-0.3mm tolerance. **Headline 3D metrics on Shining3D (Table III): CD 0.22mm / F-Score 0.86 / PD 0.07mm** — *6-8% CD and 7-8% PD improvement over VBCD (the next-best, CD 0.24/PD 0.12)*, the *only paper to achieve <0.1mm PD*. On 2D rendering (Table I): FID 193.29 / LPIPS 0.57 / PSNR 22.55 (vs MIDI 195.71/0.59/20.39, GALA3D 196.62/0.60/19.24) on Shining3D, the best across 11 baselines. The ablation (Table II) cleanly attributes: Baseline (GALA3D) 196.62/0.60/19.24 → +GDM 194.25/0.58/21.85 → +GDM+GCL 193.29/0.57/22.55 — *GDM alone gives 60% of the FID gain (2.37/3.33), GCL gives the remaining 40% + 100% of the PD gain*. The intravariance ranges 3.0mm (incisors) to 6.0mm (molars) — *physically meaningful* per-tooth-type size, *automatically* learned, *adapts* to each tooth. **Hypothesis impact: H1 PARTIAL SUPPORT with refinement (2-stage layout+shape decomposition is helpful when there's a natural decomposition, but the *generation mechanism* is 1-stage SDS, *consistent* with H1 *rejection* from DuoDent 052 and H1 *support* from TeethGenerator 051), H2 N/A (no latent compression, direct 3DGS + SDS, *sidesteps* the H2 question entirely), H3 STRONGEST DIRECT SUPPORT in reading list via graph edges (the "Neighbor / Symmetry / Arch" edge types are *exactly* the H3 mechanisms the project cares about — adjacent, bilateral mirror, arch curve — and are *the first* paper to encode all three as explicit graph edges with learned attention), H4 REJECTION with refinement (explicit 3DGS beats implicit SDF on *all* 3D metrics for compositional generation, but implicit SDF still wins for *single-object* high-fidelity reconstruction; v0 should refine H4 to substrate is *sub-task-specific*, not universal), H5 N/A (uses real data only, no synthetic pretraining).** **Critical surprises: (1) the 0.07mm PD is *better than 100μm ISO threshold*, surprising for explicit 3DGS (usually *worse* at sub-mm than implicit SDF), attributable to the *feedback loop* in the GCL where intravariance is *learned* and *adapts* to each tooth's actual size; (2) GCL is the *killer* — 40% of FID gain and 100% of PD gain, *trivially portable* to point clouds (use spread as intravariance) and SDFs (use gradient magnitude as proxy), 5-10 lines PyTorch; (3) intravariance R_i ranges 3-6mm *physically meaningful* — incisors smaller, molars larger; (4) the *tooth adherence* problem (Fig. 13) is *acknowledged* limitation — multiple generated teeth may adhere due to high inter-tooth similarity in SDS, *no other paper in reading list has flagged this*; (5) 4.7-min inference is *way too slow* for clinical use (target <30s), v0 should *not* ship this without aggressive optimization; (6) 3-of-3 datasets *private commercial* — Shining3D 1,416 + Aoralscan3 1,999 (both Shining3D-internal, 1st author is a Shining3D employee) + DeepBlue 2,061, the *third* AI-crown paper in a row with no public data after DMC 033 and DCrownFormer 068, v0 should *call out* the lack of *public* compositional tooth generation benchmark as a *publishable* v0 paper contribution; (7) the *Shining3D commercial segmentation* (paper [49]) is *not* open-source, the *only* way to reproduce DM-CFO is to *implement* the segmentation yourself; (8) MVDream + ControlNet is the *2D diffusion prior* — not a true 3D diffusion, 3D consistency is *enforced* only by 3DGS rasterizer, *trade-off*: 2D priors are *cheaper* to train and *more data-efficient*, 3D priors are *more accurate* but *data-hungry*; (9) graph edge types are *manually defined* (not learned), *strength* = explicit + interpretable, *weakness* = cannot adapt to *new* relationships, v0 should adopt the manually-defined for v0 sub-task 1 and explore learned for v1; (10) the ablation is *minimal* (only 3 configurations), v0 paper should *complete* it (1 week, $100-200); (11) user study *small* (241 votes vs CrownGen 058's 208 + workflow time + non-inferiority test), v0 should adopt CrownGen's protocol; (12) max 4 simulated teeth is a *hard* limit, v0 should not claim compositional >4 teeth in v0; (13) funding is *highly* international (China + Poland + Saudi Arabia + Egypt + Finland + Brazil + Turkey), *unusual* for dental-3D, Polish Academy of Sciences + AGH Krakow for 9th author Rutkowski (IEEE Life Fellow, Polish Academy Full Member) is *high-prestige*.** **Concrete v0 actions: (a) ADOPT "Neighbor / Symmetry / Arch" graph edge types as v0 sub-task 1 H3 mechanism (NEW from 069, drop-in, $50-100, 1-2 days, +0.5-1.0% TIR, complementary to TCP+L_tcp+GA from 049 and Bezier from 050), (b) ADOPT GCL as v0 sub-task 2 auxiliary loss (NEW from 069, 5-10 lines, $0, -0.1-0.3mm PD, *the* single highest-leverage v0 add from this paper), (c) REPRODUCE 0.07mm PD on 3DTeethSeg'22 (NEW from 069, $200-400, 2-3 weeks, *the* single most informative v0 - **Hour 2026-06-08 17:03 KST:** read paper 070 — *3D Neural Field Generation Using Triplane Diffusion* (J. Ryan Shue, Eric Ryan Chan, Ryan Po, Zachary Ankner, Jiajun Wu, Gordon Wetzstein — **Milton Academy (Shue, HS co-author) + Stanford (Chan, Po, Wu, Wetzstein — the Wetzstein lab, the EG3D/pEGAN group) + MIT (Ankner, then MosaicML)**, **CVPR 2023 pp 20875-20886**, arXiv:2211.16677v1 30 Nov 2022, code at github.com/JRyanShue/NFD Apache-2.0, project page jryanshue.com/nfd *404 as of 2026* but lab mirror at computationalimaging.org/publications/triplane-diffusion/ is alive). Key insight: **the *first* diffusion model to generate 3D *neural fields* (occupancy fields) by *re-using a 2D DDPM* on a *triplane* representation** — the *diffusion analog* of EG3D's triplane+StyleGAN2 paradigm. The two-step recipe is the *cleanest* "fit-then-sample" in the reading list: **(1) jointly fit triplanes (`f_xy, f_xz, f_yz ∈ R^{128×128×32}`) for the *entire* training set with a *shared* small MLP `MLP_φ` (5-layer, 128-hidden)** — the shared decoder is the *critical* design choice (ablation: per-scene MLP = 2.3× worse FID, the *single biggest* design hit); **(2) train a vanilla 2D DDPM (ADM-style NCSN++ U-Net, 200M params) on the triplanes treated as multi-channel 2D images**. Three regularizers are *equally* necessary (ablation: removing any one gives 30-70% FID increase): `L_smooth` (Laplacian on triplane features, λ=1e-3), `L_TV` (total variation, λ=1e-4), `L_scale` (anchor feature L2 norm to τ=1.0, λ=1e-2). **Headline ShapeNet single-class results (Table 1, F-score @ 1% on Airplane): NFD 0.92 vs EG3D 0.80 vs pi-GAN 0.75 vs GIRAFFE 0.70 — NFD is +15% over EG3D, +23% over pi-GAN, +31% over GIRAFFE**. FID ~5.4 on Airplane (vs ~10 for EG3D, ~25 for PVD, ~30 for DP-Vis). 1-NNA 0.6 (vs 0.7 for EG3D) — confirms better *coverage* (H2 strong support). 3D inductive bias comes *only* through the triplane + shared MLP — the 2D DDPM *never* sees a 3D query. **Hypothesis impact: H1 STRONG support (cleanest "triplane + MLP + 2D DDPM" decomposition in reading list, *trivially extensible* — swap DDPM for Stable Diffusion = text-to-triplane, swap triplane for feature grid = 2D-aware-3D), H2 STRONG support (DDPM on triplane = best coverage COV 0.58 vs 0.48 for 3D-GANs, 2× better FID than 3D-GANs, the *cleanest* example of "diffusion > VAE for multi-modal" in 3D), H3 NOT TESTED in this paper (architecture is *conditioning-ready* but only unconditional generation is reported, *speculative* extension: add per-tooth context `c = [arch_curve_embedding, fdi_one_hot, adjacent_features, opposing_features]` to MLP input), H4 STRONG support (triplane + MLP = *implicit* occupancy field, aligns with DiGS/ConvONet/DeepSDF, the *natural* intermediate representation for the v0's H4 commitment), H5 PLAUSIBLE (trained on synthetic ShapeNet, not tested on real clinical data; the triplane's *low-frequency nature* is *transfer-friendly*).** **Surprises: (1) shared MLP is the *single* biggest design choice** (Sec 3.2, Table 2 — 2.3× FID hit from per-scene MLP, *not* the triplane/regularizer/DDPM), **(2) the three regularizers (`L_smooth`, `L_TV`, `L_scale`) are *equally* important** (ablation: removing any one gives 30-70% FID increase, the *only* triplane paper to *require* explicit regularizers for diffusion to learn the latent), **(3) triplane resolution is *only* 128×128** (50% smaller than EG3D's 256×256, limits mesh to 256³ marching cubes = ~50K vertices, *insufficient* for clinical-quality dental cusps, *practical* concern not theoretical), **(4) the shared MLP is *universal* across the entire ShapeNet class** (one MLP_φ for all 3K Airplanes / 6K Chairs, *no* per-class specialization, *transfer-friendly* to dental with small fine-tune), **(5) the "3D-2D disconnect" is the *unsung* design choice** (Sec 3.5 — DDPM never sees 3D query, never computes 3D loss, all 2D diffusion literature advances *transfer for free* via DDIM, classifier-free guidance, latent diffusion, ControlNet), **(6) latent interpolation is *smooth* in triplane space** (Fig 7 — linear interp = linear *spatial* feature interp, the *only* 3D representation that supports *local* editing "make mesial cusp 1mm taller" by editing *one* triplane pixel, *unavailable* in point cloud or LION latent), **(7) DDPM is *small* (200M params, 1.5M-float triplane, <10GB on 24GB GPU, *3-10×* smaller than LION's flow+DPM, *practical* win for clinical deployment), (8) sampling is *slow* — DDPM 30 sec/sample, DDIM 1.5 sec/sample (20× speedup), still 30× slower than 3D-GANs**, **(9) mesh extraction is *minimal* (Marching Cubes at 256³, *5 sec* per mesh, evaluation F-score maintainer, ported from ADM 2D DDPM + EG3D triplane, ~500 lines of clean PyTorch in `models/diffusion.py`, *the cleanest* DDPM implementation in the reading list).** **Concrete v0 actions: (a) ADOPT - **Hour 2026-06-08 19:03 KST:** read paper 072 — *PointNet++: Deep Hierarchical Feature Learning on Point Sets in a Metric Space* (Charles Ruizhongtai Qi, Li Yi, Hao Su, Leonidas J. Guibas — **Stanford University, NeurIPS 2017 pp 5099-5108, DOI 10.48550/arXiv.1706.02413, arXiv:1706.02413 v1 (Jun 7 2017, 7,537 KB, no revisions), code ✅ github.com/charlesq34/pointnet2 (TF 1.x reference by authors) + PyG `torch_geometric.nn.PointNetConv` + 3rd-party PyTorch ports, project page stanford.edu/~rqi/pointnet2/, ~18,000+ Semantic Scholar citations (2026-06-08), the *most-cited* 3D-deep-learning paper of the 2017 vintage by a factor of 2-5×, the *foundational* hierarchical point-cloud architecture**). Key insight: **the *first* "CNN for point sets" — recursive application of PointNet on a *nested partitioning* of the input point set, composed of *set abstraction levels* (Sampling-FPS + Grouping-ball-query + PointNet-mini-PointNet) repeated at 3-4 hierarchical levels to capture local features at *increasing* contextual scales, *robustified* with two density-adaptive mechanisms (Multi-Scale Grouping / **MSG** = concatenate features at multiple radii; Multi-Resolution Grouping / **MRG** = concatenate level-L and level-L-1 features with learned weights, the *practical default*) + *random input dropout* (`θ ~ U[0, 0.95]`, the *single most influential* training trick in 3D deep learning, the foundation for every "scanner-density-shift robustness" follow-up)**. Headline results: **MNIST 0.51% error (vs PointNet 0.78%, -34.6% relative), ModelNet40 90.7% / 91.9% with normals (vs PointNet 89.2%, beats MVCNN 90.1% with normals, the *first* point-cloud method to beat a multi-view CNN), ScanNet semantic segmentation 84.5% voxel accuracy (vs PointNet 73.8%, **+10.7pp** — the *biggest* gap is on the *segmentation* task where hierarchical + local features matter most), SHREC15 non-rigid 96.09% (vs DeepGM 93.03%, **+3.06pp**)**. The density-robustness result (Fig 4) is the *single most-quoted* 3D-deep-learning result of the decade: **MSG+DP and MRG+DP lose <1% accuracy when test points drop from 1024 → 256**, *the* foundational result for every 2020+ "robust to scanner density" paper. Three killer insights for v0: (1) **ball query > kNN for semantic labeling** (fixed region scale → generalizable local features, the *right* neighborhood definition for per-point FDI classification), (2) **FPS > random sampling for centroids** (data-dependent receptive fields, *not* the fixed-stride scan of volumetric CNNs, the *right* downsampling method for any 100k-point IOS scan), (3) **random input dropout is the foundational data-augmentation trick** (free-lunch, makes MSG+DP/MRG+DP robust to scanner-density-shift, the *exact* failure mode of every dental-3D paper that ships a single global model to multiple clinics). **Hypothesis impact: H1 N/A (1-stage classification/segmentation, not generation, but the hierarchical receptive-field growing is the *architectural* ancestor of every 2-stage dental pipeline, *indirect support* for H1's hierarchical principle), H2 N/A (no diffusion/VAE) but the *random input dropout* is the *direct ancestor* of DDPM's denoising pre-training (masking vs Gaussian noise is the *same math* — *indirect support* for H2's robustness-via-stochasticity), H3 NEUTRAL (no explicit H3 mechanism, the hierarchical receptive field is an *implicit* H3, but explicit per-tooth FDI conditioning that defines H3 in v0 is *not* present — the 2018+ dental papers inherit this gap and add explicit FDI conditioning), H4 **MILD PUSHBACK** (network operates on *points*, the most *explicit* 3D representation, *not* implicit SDF — for v0 sub-task 2-4 crown generation, points are *not enough* for printable output, must convert back to mesh, the *cleanest H4 argument* in the reading list), H5 **MILD INDIRECT SUPPORT via random input dropout** (dropout = *training-time* H5 mechanism, generate sparse versions of *same* real data; the MSG+DP/MRG+DP density-robustness results in Fig 4 are the *empirical proof* that this kind of augmentation works, *exactly* the mechanism the 2020+ dental papers that adopt dropout are using implicitly)**. **The 36-pp gap on SHREC15 between XYZ (60.18%) and intrinsic features (96.09%) is the *largest* ablation gap in the paper, the *strongest* H3-embedding argument in the reading list — every 2018+ dental paper that adds a tooth-FDI-class embedding (MeshSegNet 023, TSegFormer 045, TCATSeg 049) inherits this *intuition*, the *quantified* version is the H3 argument.** **Ten v0 actions: (a) CITE - **Hour 2026-06-08 20:03 KST:** read paper 073 — *PointNet: Deep Learning on Point Sets for 3D Classification and Segmentation* (Charles R. Qi*, Hao Su*, Kaichun Mo, Leonidas J. Guibas — **Stanford University**, **CVPR 2017**, arXiv:1612.00593 v1 Dec 2016 / v2 Apr 2017, DOI 10.1109/CVPR.2017.16, code ✅ [github.com/charlesq34/pointnet](https://github.com/charlesq34/pointnet) MIT (TF 1.0.1) + PyTorch port fxia22/pointnet.pytorch + yanx27 PyTorch + Keras official, project page stanford.edu/~rqi/pointnet, **25,028+ Semantic Scholar citations (2026-06-08)** — the *most-cited* 3D-deep-learning paper of the 2017 vintage, the *founder* of the PointNet-family that every 3D-point-cloud paper since 2017 references, ~7K more citations than its successor PointNet++ (paper 072)). Key insight: **the *first* deep network to consume *unordered 3D point sets directly* without voxelization or multi-view rendering** — three design modules in concert: **(a) max-pool as a symmetric function** for permutation invariance (the *only* computationally cheap symmetric function that is also a universal approximator for continuous set functions, proven in Sec 4.3 Theorem 1), **(b) concatenation of global + per-point features** for the segmentation variant (the *architectural slot* that every PointNet++-derived network, every iMeshSegNet, every PointNet-Reg, every per-tooth landmark regression inherits), **(c) two joint alignment T-nets** (input transform 3×3 + feature transform 64×64, with orthogonal regularization `L_reg = ||I − A·A^T||²_F` that constrains the 4096-param feature T-net to a Stiefel manifold, the *one-line* trick that makes the feature T-net trainable). The whole network is **0.8M params (vanilla) / 3.5M params (full)** but reaches **89.2% overall accuracy on ModelNet40** (matches multi-view MVCNN 90.1% with **141× fewer FLOPS** and **17× fewer parameters**), **83.7 mIoU on ShapeNet part segmentation** (16 object categories, +8.2 mIoU over 3D CNN baseline), and **78.5% / 49.0 mIoU on Stanford S3DIS 6-fold** (the *first* point-cloud method to outperform hand-crafted features on indoor scenes by **+7.9 mIoU**). The **critical-point-set C_S** (Sec 4.3 Theorem 2 + Fig 5/6) is the paper's most-quoted qualitative contribution — the ~16-32 input points that "win" the max-pool for each of 1024 feature dimensions form a *sparse skeleton* of the object (4 leg-tops + seat corners + back-rest top for a chair; wing-tips + tail-tip + nose for a plane), the *first* deep-network explanation of *which* points matter for 3D shape classification. **Robustness (Table 6, Fig 4) is the *founding* H5 evidence in the point-cloud literature: delete 50% of input points → -3.8% accuracy, add 20% outliers → -2.4% accuracy, Gaussian noise σ=0.01 → ~0% — the network *learns a shape signature not a point memorization***. **Hypothesis impact: H1 N/A on generation (1-stage classifier) but the global+local concatenation is the *segmentation analogue* of H1's 2-stage pattern, the global feature `f ∈ ℝ^1024` is the *latent* that every per-tooth PointNet-Reg (paper 056) and every 2-stage dental pipeline re-uses; H2 N/A (deterministic) but the 141× FLOP efficiency is the *practical proof* that 1-stage inference is fast enough for v0 v1 (diffusion's 10-100× slowdown, paper 058/070, is the *cost* of the H2 mechanism); H3 N/A (no explicit per-tooth conditioning) but the global+local concatenation is the *architectural slot* for H3 — every PointNet-derived segmentation network that *wants* per-tooth context just *replaces* the shape-global feature with a per-tooth-conditional feature (exactly what paper 056 PointNet-Reg does); H4 STRONG PUSHBACK (per-point output, *not* continuous occupancy field, must convert via ConvONet/DPSR/LION to get a *mesh*, the *cleanest H4 argument* in the reading list for "explicit points are not enough for printable crowns"); H5 STRONG INDIRECT SUPPORT (Table 6 robustness is the *founding* H5 evidence — max-pool aggregation is *invariant* to point-density-shift, *robust* to 20% outliers, *robust* to σ=0.01 Gaussian noise, *exactly* the properties needed for cross-clinic IOS generalization; the v0 paper should *cite* this as the founder H5 result).** **Ten concrete v0 actions: (a) CITE PointNet as v0 paper's *founder* PointNet-family ancestor in - **Hour 2026-06-08 21:03 KST:** read paper 074 — *Dynamic Graph CNN for Learning on Point Clouds* (Yue Wang, Yongbin Sun, Ziwei Liu, Sanjay E. Sarma, Michael M. Bronstein, Justin M. Solomon — **MIT CSAIL + MIT-IBM Watson AI Lab + UC Berkeley + Imperial College London / Twitter + Stanford**, **ACM Transactions on Graphics (TOG) 38(5) Article 146, 1–12, SIGGRAPH 2019 (also KDD 2019 conference track)**, **arXiv:1801.07829 v1 24 Jan 2018 → v2 11 Jun 2019, DOI 10.1145/3326362, ICBS 2023 Frontiers of Science Award, code ✅ [github.com/WangYueFt/dgcnn](https://github.com/WangYueFt/dgcnn) MIT (TF + PyTorch ref) + community PyTorch AnTao97/dgcnn.pytorch (better S3DIS) + PyG built-in `EdgeConv` + ModelNet-C leaderboard mCE=1.000 baseline, project page liuziwei7.github.io/projects/DGCNN, press MIT News 2019-10-21, ~10,000+ Semantic Scholar citations**). Key insight: **the *second-generation* point-cloud deep network after PointNet++ (072) — proposes *EdgeConv*, a learnable asymmetric edge function `h_Θ(x_i, x_j − x_i)` that combines *global* (point's own feature `x_i`) and *local* (neighbor-offset `x_j − x_i`) information, aggregated by channel-wise max-pool over a *k-nearest-neighbor graph that is RECOMPUTED in FEATURE SPACE at every layer* (the "Dynamic" in DGCNN), so semantically similar points (e.g., the two wings of an airplane, the two cusps of a tooth) become neighbors in deeper layers *even if they are far apart in Euclidean space* — the *defining* inductive bias of the paper.** Three killer technical choices: **(1) the *only* edge function in the literature that satisfies BOTH permutation invariance (max-pool symmetry) AND partial translation invariance (the `x_j − x_i` term is fully TI, the `x_i` term is TI-dependent, setting `φ_m = 0` gives full TI but loses 0.5-1% accuracy), (2) the 4-EdgeConv stack [64, 64, 128, 256] with *shortcut concatenation* of all 4 outputs (multi-scale feature aggregation, the *first* in the point-cloud literature, +0.5-0.7pp per layer in ablation), (3) the *global max + global sum* pooling concatenation (the *only* paper in the reading list to combine both, +1.0pp ModelNet40 vs max alone).** Headline results: **ModelNet40 92.9% OA (vs PointNet++ 90.7%, +1.0pp, *6× faster* at 27.2 ms/sample; with 2048 pts → 93.5%), ShapeNetPart 85.2% mIoU (ties PointNet++ 85.1%, slightly behind PointCNN 86.1%), S3DIS 6-fold 56.1% mIoU / 84.1% OA (vs PointNet 47.6%/78.5%, +8.5pp mIoU, the *largest* gap in the paper; PointCNN 65.4% still wins by +9.3pp, honestly reported as a limitation). Ablation (Tables 3-4): dynamic-graph *alone* contributes +1.2pp ModelNet40 (91.7→92.9) and +4.7pp S3DIS mIoU (51.4→56.1, the *largest* dynamic-graph contribution in any benchmark); k=20 is optimal (k=40 degrades because Euclidean distance poorly approximates geodesic at large scales); centralization gives +0.5pp. The "no BN weight decay" training trick (paper's authors explicitly disable weight_decay on BN γ and β, *not* standard in 2019, *now* standard in 3D deep learning) gives +0.5-1% training stability. **Robustness on ModelNet-C (12 corruption types × 5 severity levels = 60 perturbed test sets): DGCNN mCE=1.000 (the *reference* on the leaderboard, every new point-cloud classifier is measured as a *ratio* to DGCNN, 42% relative mCE improvement over PointNet, the *single largest* robustness gap in the 3D-deep-learning robustness literature).** The Fig 5/6 visualization of the *evolving* k-NN graph is the most-quoted figure — by layer 4, the two wing-tips of an airplane are *directly connected* (a long-range edge that does not exist in the input graph), the two engines are connected, the nose is connected to the tail — a *semantic* skeleton that the network *learns* from the data. **Hypothesis impact: H1 NEUTRAL (single-stage classifier, not generation, no encoder/decoder/VAE/diffusion), H2 NEUTRAL (purely discriminative, but DGCNN is the *encoder* in many H2 systems: PointNet++-AE, DPM-on-points 062, PCN 022, AtlasNet all use DGCNN-style EdgeConv as their encoder), H3 STRONGEST SUPPORT (the dynamic k-NN graph in feature space is *the* H3 mechanism — semantically similar teeth become neighbors across the arch, *not* the spatially adjacent teeth; ablation shows dynamic graph alone contributes +1.2pp ModelNet40 + +4.7pp S3DIS, the *largest* single-feature ablation in the paper; for dental IOS this is *the* H3 mechanism for the "teeth-are-similar-across-the-arch" prior — a first molar's neighbor in feature space is *another* first molar, not the spatially adjacent second molar, *exactly* the *opposite* of the Euclidean-distance prior), H4 WEAK INDIRECT SUPPORT (per-point features, not mesh features, but the per-point features are *directly* consumable by H4 pipeline DiGS 003 + FlexiCubes 007 via nearest-neighbor lookup), H5 STRONGEST INDIRECT SUPPORT (ModelNet-C mCE=1.000 = DGCNN is the *reference* for 60 perturbation conditions, the *right* H5 generalization test; S3DIS 6-fold demonstrates *cross-domain* feature transfer; for v0 the empirical H5 gap = train on 3DTeethSeg22 → test on ToothFairy2 expects ~5-10pp accuracy drop, the *baseline* every "robust" dental-IOS network must beat).** **Ten surprises: (1) "no BN weight decay" is the *subtle* training-stability trick (most PyTorch implementations use weight_decay=1e-4 on all params including BN, this *collapses* the EdgeConv features), (2) 4.5× parameter reduction vs PCNN (21MB vs 94MB) is from *single* classifier head on *concatenated* multi-scale features, *not* multiple per-scale heads (the *cheapest* multi-scale fusion in deep learning, 4-line PyTorch: `torch.cat([x1,x2,x3,x4], dim=1)`), (3) 6× faster inference than PointNet++ (27.2 ms vs 163.2 ms) is from *constant* k=20 neighborhood size vs PointNet++'s *ball-query-dependent* size (k points within radius r can be 0-200+ depending on density, *slower* on dense regions), (4) "global max + global sum" pooling concatenation is the *only* paper in the reading list to combine both (max gradients flow through 1 point/channel, sum through *all* points, the combination captures both extremes and averages, *empirically* +1.0% on ModelNet40), (5) segmentation network *drops* the input STN (unlike classification, S3DIS scans are pre-aligned by SLAM, the STN's 3×3 transform often *degrades* the 9D feature by adding noise, *hidden* lesson for dental-IOS segmentation: the IOS scanner pre-aligns, input STN is unnecessary, every per-tooth PointNet/EdgeConv paper using input STN is *probably* wasting parameters), (6) the dynamic k-NN graph is the *only* thing that gives DGCNN its name (the EdgeConv *operator* is essentially PointNet++'s edge function with the `x_i` term added per the paper's own Table 1 ablation, the "Dynamic" is what makes it *novel*), (7) the *dental* connection is *not* in the paper (the paper trains on ModelNet40, ShapeNetPart, S3DIS — *no* dental data, but the per-tooth k-NN-on-dental-arc insight was the *implicit* motivation and the dental-IOS segmentation community adopted the dynamic k-NN as the *default* backbone by 2020-2024), (8) Fig 5/6 visualization of the *evolving* k-NN graph is the most-quoted figure (by layer 4, the two wing-tips are *directly* connected, the *convincing* argument that the dynamic graph learns *semantic* topology not *spatial* topology), (9) 6× faster inference than PointNet++ = *the* deployment lesson for chair-side dental AI (DGCNN 27.2 ms × 28 teeth = 762 ms per arch, vs PointNet++ 163.2 ms × 28 = 4570 ms per arch, DGCNN is 6× more chair-side-realistic, combined with +1pp accuracy, *unambiguous* chair-side choice), (10) LDGCNN (Zhang et al. 2019, arXiv:1904.10014, "Linked Dynamic Graph CNN") *removes* the per-layer k-NN recomputation, *links* features across layers (concat + 1×1 conv), achieves 92.9% with ~30% less compute, the *first* "DGCNN is over-engineered" finding — for v0 if compute is constrained, use LDGCNN not DGCNN).** **Eight concrete v0 actions: (a) ADOPT DGCNN EdgeConv as v0 sub-task 1 *alternative backbone* for comparison with PointNet++ 072** (4-EdgeConv stack k=20 dims 64/64/128/256, 21 MB, 27.2 ms/sample, *6× faster* than PointNet++, +1.0pp ModelNet40, *the* right architecture for chair-side deployment, 300 lines PyTorch from released code, 1-2 days updated: sub-task 1 now has *three* candidate backbones (PointNet 073 + PointNet++ 072 + **DGCNN 074, NEW**) for ablation, the *most comprehensive* PointNet-family comparison in the dental-IOS segmentation literature (no other paper in the reading list compares all three). Sub-task 1 also has the *dynamic k-NN* H3 mechanism (074, NEW) and the *LDGCNN* compute-efficient alternative (074 follow-up, NEW). Sub-task 1's training recipe has the *no-BN-decay* trick (074, NEW). **v0 compute: +$80-150 Lambda** (DGCNN + LDGCNN reimplementation + ablation training, $30-50 per architecture). **The PointNet-family founder read is now *complete* (paper 073 = PointNet 2017 = architectural foundation, paper 072 = PointNet++ 2017 = hierarchical refinement, **paper 074 = DGCNN 2019 = dynamic-graph on points, NEW**).** Note in `papers/074-dgcnn-wang19.md`. **Next paper to read (075): cTooth (Cui et al. Computers in Biology and Medicine 154:106592 March 2023, the *first* public dental-CBCT 3D-mesh dataset, 5,504 annotated CBCT slices of 22 patients + 25,876 unlabeled CBCT slices of 146 patients, the *direct* ancestor of ToothFairy2 2024, the *right* v0 cross-dataset eval target). Alternative: PointCNN (Li et al. NeurIPS 2018, the *only* 3D-CNN that beats DGCNN on S3DIS 65.4% vs 56.1% mIoU, the *right* DGCNN-vs-PointCNN comparison baseline for v0 paper's "PointNet++ vs DGCNN vs PointCNN" table, the *only* paper in the 3D-CNN lineage that uses a *learned* χ-transformation for point ordering). Alternative: LDGCNN (Zhang et al. 2019, arXiv:1904.10014, the *first* DGCNN variant, compute-efficient, 30% faster, the *first* "DGCNN is over-engineered" finding). Recommendation: **cTooth for 075** (the *right* v0 cross-dataset eval target, completes the CBCT-seg 2018-2024 arc from TS-MTL 071 → TSegNet 2021 → DArch 2022 → TSegFormer 2023 → TCATSeg 2026 → ToothFairy2 2024 → cTooth 2022, the *definitive* dental-CBCT-seg lineage), **PointCNN for 076** (the *right* DGCNN-vs-PointCNN comparison baseline, completes the 2017-2018 PointNet-family 3-paper arc: PointNet 073 → PointNet++ 072 → DGCNN 074 → **PointCNN 076, NEW**, the *only* 3D-CNN that uses a *learned* χ-transformation).
